# The Cauchy Estimate Across Cutoffs: Matched Terms, Age Decay, and the One-Step Inequality

27 October 2026. Own working note on item (9) of `WILSON_IT1_CONTINUUM_LIMIT_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md`: a summable bound a_K on |F_{K+1}(C) − F_K(C)|. It edits no other note. The two-run comparison is organised at the stopping lattice, term by term of Bałaban's representation CRE (2.18), and the content of the stability statement S of the continuum note's (9.4)(e) is reduced to one one-step inequality (G), derived in §8, and to the lattice-artifact estimates of §§9–10; the bound (Q) of the continuum note on the relative weight of the large-field terms is replaced by the derived bound of §11.

**Open: the input (LF) (§6.2, §8.6).** For a history born at a scale n > k/8, so that its scales m are all ≥ n, the run-dependence of the ingredients (β) and (γ) below is to change a term by at most (exp(Σ_XΣ_mC_Ms^{LF}_{n(X)}L^d|Z_m ∩ X|_m) − 1) times its majorant, |Z_m ∩ X|_m the number of scale-m points of the component's history at the scale m, n(X) the birth scale of the component X, and s^{LF}_n ≤ C_hθ̃^n: one bound per point of the history, at its birth scale. It is asked in two forms: (LF-1), for the young matched pairs of §6.2, against the majorant M_τ of (11.1) built from Bałaban's bound (1.79); (LF-2), for the renormalisation quotients of §8.6 at complex V in the inductive class, against Bałaban's majorants n̄ of their numerators and |d| of their denominators. For (β) below, and for the stopping-scale shells of the all-small-field pair (§3), it is derived; (γ) is derived except the parts named at the end of the paragraph on (β) and (γ) below.

- (β) The shells of the plaquette and reference-configuration tests: LFR I (1.3), (1.4), (1.7), and (1.8) (p.178), CRE (2.17) (p.257), the small-field functions χ_k, χ_{k,Λ}, and χ_{h,1/2} of LFR II (1.70), the large-field functions χ^c_j(P_j) and χ′^c_j(Q_j) of LFR II p.380 l.31–46, and the factors of ζ(Ω^c_j) of CRE p.255 l.4–9 built from them. These test minimisers computed on the run's fine lattice, and at equal data the two runs' tested quantities differ by C(ε/a_j)^{1−2ε₀}ε_j; the thresholds ε_j and 2ϑ_j differ through the coupling by ≤ Ctg_j²|δ_j| at the threshold t. The tests of the scale j that are not equal to 1 on the support of the term's other characteristic functions lie in the term's large-field region or its collar (CRE (3.6)–(3.9) and (3.19)–(3.21); `WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §3(a)), so for a young pair the shells lie on the history's cubes, at scales ≥ k/2 − 1, and on the stopping-scale cubes of Ω_k.
- (γ) The large-field integrands: the action and the form of LFR II (1.70), the minimiser U₀ and the operators H_{1,k} and Δ₁(ζ₀) of the renormalisation denominators LFR II (1.1)–(1.2), the factors V(Y) of LFR II (1.91), the boundary terms 𝐁 generated at the steps at which a large-field region existed, and the large-field dressings of the loop at the R-operations with the loop.

The other run-dependent ingredients of these terms are derived. The coupling shells of the radial tests (1.5), (1.9), and χ^{(j−1)c}(R_j) are bounded in `WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §2(b). The denominators' coupling, normalisation constants, and cutoff χ({|B′| < M₀g_k^{−1}ε_k}), and the numerators' cutoff χ′ (|B| < g_k^{−1}δ′_k), are bounded in §8.6. VAR's multiscale condition (7) holds on the support of every term (that note's §1).

(β) is derived in that note's §3(d). There each plaquette and reference test is replaced, at every K and in both runs, by the soft test χ̄(f_□/t), linear from 1 at f_□ = t to 0 at f_□ = (1 + κ_s)t, κ_s := β/30. Every decomposition of unity stays exact, so F_K is unchanged, and every shell is bounded pointwise by δ^s_j/κ_s, δ^s_j := 2C(ε/a_j)^{1−2ε₀} + 4Cg_j²|δ_j|, times a run's own term with sharp tests at (1 + κ_s)t and t: the history's and the stopping-scale cubes' of a young pair, and the all-small-field pair's stopping-scale test. The reading (R-s) that this needs, that Bałaban's construction and bounds hold with the soft tests, is checked there on every page where a test stands: CRE §§1 and 3, LFR I §1, and LFR II §1. Lemma SH of `Balaban\DIFFERENCES_PROOFS.md` does not work for these shells (that note's §3(b)–(c)), and V4's lem:c5-5-5 bounds the shell of one fluctuation coordinate under a conditional Gaussian only. For (γ), the classical depth of the multiscale minimisers U_{𝐁″} of LFR II (1.50) at equal data is derived in that note's §4(b), as §9 with the multiscale bounds of VAR and PBG, in the layer-weighted norm of VAR's contraction and per point at the history's birth scale. The depth of U₀ of (1.3), a minimiser with a free region Λ, and its propagation through the data of U_{𝐁″}, which contain the block averages of U₀, are derived in its §4(c) from the lower bound (1.9) of LFR II at each run's minimum, under the coupling condition (T-Λ) of that section. The per-layer form of §10's (10.6), with H_{1,k}, Δ₁(ζ₀), V(Y), V(ζ₀, ·), and the linear term of (1.2) through it, is derived in that note's §4(d), using the gauge-fixing part of §10.4. The carried boundary terms 𝐁 of the R-terms of §8 are derived in its §4(e) under its condition (⋆), with no age factor, on the terms that are not deep in the sense of that section; the deep terms are bounded through the old histories of §8.6 and the old pairs of §6.2, and where they meet a young component through their crossing of the layers. For the young matched pairs of §6.2 the terms 𝐁 remain in (γ). The alignment of the backgrounds, here in §10.3 and in that note's §4(d), is derived through the pair class of §10.3, with no second-order control of the alignment fields. What is missing is the points (3), (6), (7), and (10) of that note's (LF-A). The first is the variation of the old terms V(Y) at the complex points of the class, the forms, the actions, d, and 𝐂_k − W_ext being derived on the class under (T-c), and the variation being one Cauchy estimate on V(Y) with Bałaban's bounds (1.68)–(1.69); what is missing there is that *Bałaban's Statements at the Reduced Thresholds* is a reading, not a check of every use of C₀ and C₁. The others are the interface multipliers, the per-Y comparison, and the carried boundary terms at the fluctuation step. The per-Y comparison includes the per-piece comparison of the localised 𝐂_k and the walk-factor difference (W-δ), which `WILSON_IT1_WALK_FACTOR_DIFFERENCE_2026_10_28.md` reduces to three open inequalities (Wδ-1)–(Wδ-3). The deep V(Y) in ν_j (§8.6) is also open. The large-field dressings of the loop are derived in that note's §5 except at the R-operations with the loop, which use these points through §8.6.

**Open: the near domains that meet a finer layer in R-Δ^c (§5, (R-Δ^c-T)).** The class bound (5.1c) at the points z with n > j whose near domain □₀ meets a finer layer m < n; at n = j it is derived by the direct bound of *Mixed radii.*. What is missing is that the restriction of a configuration of 𝒮_z to □₀ lies in RG I's space of Lemma 4 at the radii of the layer n. On a finer layer m ≥ 1 the bounds are weaker in the L^{−n}-scale, by L^{2(n−m)} in (2.34), L^{3(n−m)} in (2.36), and L^{n−m} or L^{2(n−m)} in (2.39); where □₀ reaches Ω₁^c (m = 0) the class (b) gives no bound at all. What closes it for m ≥ 1 is the bound at the age of the finest layer met, with the count of those points against the |Γ_m| of (2.43); the case m = 0 needs a bound on Ω₁^c, which that count does not cover. It enters the variation-group bound (5.1v) of §5, §8.2, §8.4, §8.6, §10.5, and the large-field note's §4(d) through the complex class.
**Open: the lift condition (§5, *The lift condition*).** The condition (a) of the class asks that the pair lift (ι_X𝐔, Q₁^{pl}(ι_X𝐔)*𝐉) lie in run B's U^c_{j+1}(X, r), and its current follows from the configuration conditions and run A's current bound r_{0,j}/C_{Q*}. At run A's real minimisers U^A_k(V) it is derived, with the localised lift below. At the complex data of the fluctuation integral and at the composites of RG I's Lemma 4 the configuration conditions are missing. What closes it there is §9.5–9.7 at complex data, with Bałaban's operator bounds at complex configurations (VAR p.309 l.2–4, RG I p.273 l.2–3), and, for the composites, the scale-j version of the last sentence of §9.7 with Lemma 4 for run B at its local minimiser of the same data. It enters (5.1c) for Δ, (5.1v), §8.2, and the large-field note's §4(d).
**Derived, unreviewed: the plane-resolved moment of G (§3, *The plane-resolved moment.*; §5).** CRE (2.29) holds only for the sum over X at one configuration (CRE p.260 l.2–4), and G evaluates each X at its own ι_X𝐔, so G is not known to be Euclidean covariant. Covariance is not used. CRE (3.49)–(3.56) need only gauge invariance, their first-moment terms vanishing by the Ward identity at linear gauge parameters (§5, *Evidence.*), and (3.57) gives G's whole tensor Π^G_{μν,κλ}(z) at each point z, which may depend on z, since its z-independence and (3.62)–(3.63) use the translation invariance of (2.29); its part off the isotropic coefficient is the plane-resolved term N_j, subtracted in irr^G and irr(Δ_j) through plane-pair actions. The global-lift term has run B's own tensor, isotropic by (3.60)–(3.61) for run B's sum, so every coefficient of N_j is a boundary term of the localised lift. N_j(U^A_k) joins the moment defect of (5.1v).

**Derived, unreviewed: the localised lift (§9.8, *The Localised Lift.*).** ι_XU := U(𝐁_1(X^∼), M^·(Q_1^{s*}U)) is Bałaban's local minimiser, with the collar data of the exact right inverse Q_1^{s*} of the averaging (CRE (1.2)–(1.3)), and VAR Theorem 1 and Proposition 9 give its existence, uniqueness, regularity, and analyticity. Its distance from ι on X is the tail C_ι(ε/a)(ε₁/a)e^{−δ₀dist(·, Ω₁^c)/ε}, with the gradient one factor ε^{−1} worse, through CRE (3.6)–(3.7) and VAR (190). The same decay, linearised at 1, gives the boundary layer of the moment, C_Π(j) (§5). It enters every use of G: (5.1c) for Δ, the pair line, (5.1v), the 𝐑-terms' young ages, §8.2, and the large-field note's §4(d).
**Open: the 𝐑 version of the class bound (5.1c) at complex configurations (§10.5).** The bound O(1)(L^jL^{−k})⁴g_j^{κ₀}exp(−κd_j(X)) of CRE p.283 l.34–38 for the 𝐑-terms at the complex configurations of 𝒮_z; Bałaban states it at the real regular configurations. It enters the old ages of the 𝐑-terms' variation at the fluctuation-dependent configurations of each step (§10.5). What closes it: §5's derivation of (5.1c) checked for the 𝐑-terms up to CRE (3.49), where p.283 l.34–36 stops Bałaban's; and the resulting per-step source ≤ C″_RK^{1−κ₀/2} carried in S_K, with Kμ_K in place of μ_K in w_K and κ₀ ≥ 9.

**The cube sizes at a crossing (§2) (derived).** CRE (2.5) (p.255 l.36–40) defines R_j as the smallest power of L with R_j ≥ (log g_j^{−2})^r, from each run's own coupling. The admissibility conditions of §2's bijection (CRE pp.255–256), the condition (i) of LFR I p.177, the cubes of the domains, and the localisation sets all depend on R_j, so they agree at the shared scale j only if R^A_j = R^B_{j+1}. That fails where a power of L lies between (log(g^A_j)^{−2})^r and (log(g^B_{j+1})^{−2})^r.
- *The choice.* Both runs use R_j := R̄(K − j), with K the run's number of steps and R̄(n) the smallest power of L with R̄(n) ≥ 2(log(ḡ^{−2} + bn))^r. Run B's scale j + 1 then has the same R̄(K − j) as run A's scale j. F_K does not depend on R, which only parametrises the decompositions of unity (§1).
- *Its properties.* By ONE_LOOP_PROOFS Lemma 2.1(i), g^{−2} + b(K − k) − C ≤ g_k^{−2} ≤ g^{−2} + b′(K − k) + C. So for ḡ small R̄(K − j) ≥ (log g_j^{−2})^r for either run, which is (2.5) as a lower bound, and R̄(K − j) ≤ C(log g_j^{−2})^r. R̄ is nonincreasing in j, changes by at most the factor L per step, and satisfies R̄_m/R̄_n ≤ L(1 + b)^{r/log ḡ^{−2}}(n − m)^{r/log ḡ^{−2}} ≤ (L + 1)(n − m)^{β₀} for n > m once r/log ḡ^{−2} ≤ β₀, which is CRE (2.9). Since the ratio is a power of L, (2.9) also gives R̄_m/R̄_n ≤ L^{(n−m)/2} for n − m ≥ 2.
- *The uses, checked.* Bałaban's proofs use R_j only through these properties.
  - CRE §§1–3 (pp.245–285): partitions into MR_j-, LM₂R_j-, and LMR_{k+1}-cubes compatible with the earlier ones, which needs R a power of L with R_{j+1} ∈ {R_j, R_j/L} (p.246 l.24–29, p.257 l.28–43, p.264 l.53–56); (2.9) (p.256 l.12–17); e^{−δ2M₂R_k} ≤ e^{−R_k} and e^{−δLM₂R_{k+1}} ≤ e^{−R_k} through R_k ≤ LR_{k+1} (p.266 l.18–19, p.268 l.41–51, p.269 l.44); and k₁ by L^{−(k−k₁)}MR ≤ L (p.270 l.49–50). The R₁ of (2.44) (p.263) is a constant, not a cube size.
  - LFR I pp.177–201: components in 100MR_k-cubes, with R_k decreasing by the factor L at some steps (p.177 l.19–26); N₀ with L^{−N₀+1}MR_{k−N₀+1} = M (p.179 l.8–9); R_{j−N+1} ≤ (L+1)N^{β₀}R_j (p.192 l.17–20); and e^{−R} small (pp.184–188, p.198, p.199 l.38–39, p.200 l.16–18 and l.33), with 10δMR_{j+1} ≥ R_j at p.188 l.22–23.
  - LFR II pp.356–390: the covers of the operation S and the scaling d′_n(Z^{(n−j)}) ≤ L^{−(n−j)/2}d′_j(Z), which need R_j/R_n ≤ L^{(n−j)/2} for n − j ≥ 2 (p.384 l.7–8 and l.35–36); R_{j−N+1} ≤ (L+1)N^{β₀}R_j (p.382 l.4); N = R_j (p.384 l.17); and the powers R^{d+1}, R^{d+2}, R^7, and R^{−8} inside a strict polylogarithmic dominance by p₀ or p₁ ((1.47), (1.79)–(1.88), p.383 l.28, p.389 l.15–18).
  - RG I and RG II use only a fixed R = R₁ (RG I p.262 l.42, p.273 l.41; RG II p.3 l.27–28), not R_j.
- R̄ satisfies each of these, with changed constants in the polylogarithmic bounds, the same in both runs.

**Status.** Derived: the matched decomposition (§2), the exact form of a matched pair's difference at the stopping scale (§3), the summable direct bound of the extra generation (§4.1), the summable old tail (§4.2), the contraction of the carried same-generation differences (§5, the reading R-Δ of CRE pp.279–283, and the class bound R-Δ^c of RG I §§3–4 on the regular subclass 𝒮_z, with its uniform constant C_α, its derivative version along families of local minimisers, and the variation-group bound (5.1v) with the current along the pair line through the pair lift, at the near domains satisfying (N), with the lift condition open where the proof evaluates the lifted terms at complex configurations), the age factor of the unmatched set and the comparison of the matched pairs with large-field regions (§6), the generated-difference inequality (G) of one step as the recursion (8.3) with the small constant ε_k^* (§8.2–8.3), the R-sector ((8.4), §8.4 and §8.6) except the localised pieces of 𝐂_k, the difference of the re-summing operators ((W-δ)), and the deep-V(Y) part of ν_j, which are open, with the 𝐑 version of (5.1c) at complex configurations open, the 𝐑-terms' variation group at the stopping scale (§5) and at the young ages of each step (§10.5), the solution of the recursion with its coupling and R-feedbacks (§8.7), the classical depth (§9), the Gaussian depth (§10), and the relative weight of the large-field terms in place of (Q) (§11). Four items are open: (LF) above, through its ingredient (γ), used by the young matched pairs (§6.2) and by the R-terms of the histories at the coarse scales (§8.6); (R-Δ^c-T) above, the near domains of R-Δ^c at layer boundaries; the lift condition above, at complex configurations; and the 𝐑 version of (5.1c) at complex configurations above. §5's R-Δ^c on 𝒮_z and its constant are reviewed and repaired, the repairs unreviewed; (5.1v) and the 𝐑-terms' variation group of §5 and §10.5 are unreviewed, and so are the pair lift and the current along the pair line (§3, §5), the plane-resolved moment of G (§3, §5), and the localised lift (§9.8). The ingredient (β) of (LF) and the stopping-scale shells of the all-small-field pair (§3) are derived through the soft tests, and the cube sizes at a crossing (§2) through the run-independent R̄. The alignment of §10.3, the bridge (B1)–(B5) of §10.1 between the plain block fields and PBG's double-bar averagings, the parts (10.6a) and (10.6b) of the defect in §10.4, with the gauge-fixing part's functional form (10.6c) for the large-field consumer, the π-part, which vanishes identically (β_π ≡ 0), and its Assembly through the two-sided bound (10.5′) are derived and not yet reviewed. The conditions are thresholds of Bałaban's kind on g_a, the coupling at the stopping scale (the two runs' inverse squared couplings there differ by δ_k, and every shared-scale coupling up to the stopping scale is ≤ (1+β₀)g_a by CRE (2.6)), on γ := sup_Kg_K, and on ḡ, each on its own line:

- (T1) The contraction and the feedbacks (§8.3, §8.4, §8.7): for every g ≤ (1+β₀)γ, ε^*(g)C_τ ≤ 1/2 and 4ε̂^*(g)N′(g/(1+β₀)) ≤ 1, ε̂^*(g) := sup_{g′≤2g}ε^*(g′), which cover every shared scale i ≤ K at its own coupling g_i ≤ (1+β₀)g_K; here ε^*(g) := C_XC_KPL⁴C_α[2³C_TC_Gc(g)μ(g) + 4a(g)] is the constant of (8.3) at the coupling g, μ(g) := (g/m(g))² ≤ 4c_m^{−2}(log g^{−2})^{−2min(q₀,q₁)} the square of the coupling over the analyticity margin m(g) := α₃(g)/2 of the frozen terms, α₃ at the radii r of the norm class (§8.2), a(g) ≤ gP_a(log g^{−2}) the bound of the interaction's activities on Bałaban's complex fluctuation domain, c(g) a double logarithm, N′(g) := log_L(MR(g)) + 3 the bound of the memory length of the recent R-terms (§8.4), and C_τ a constant of L, b, ḡ, and Bałaban's constants (§8.7); ε^*(g) → 0 as g → 0 as a negative power of log g^{−2} and N′(g) grows as a double logarithm, so both conditions hold for γ small, uniformly in K. The boundary-term recursion of `WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §4(e) adds 2ε^{*B}(g_i)W ≤ 1, with ε^{*B}(g) := 2C_XC_KPC_εε(g), C_ε Bałaban's constant in the factor O(ε_k) of CRE p.277 l.7–9, and W := Σ_{r≥1}w_rL^{dr} ≤ 2C_WL^d, finite under that section's condition (⋆) σ ≥ (d + 1)log L + 1 on Bałaban's R and M; ε(g) = gp₀(g) → 0, so this holds for γ small in the same way.
- The matched pairs with large-field regions (§6.2): a pair whose oldest alive component was born at least ⌊k/2⌋ steps before the stopping scale carries the factor exp(−C_P⌊k/2⌋) of its history volume, measured against each run's own all-small-field term (§6.1, §11(c)); for a younger pair the small-field ingredients differ per point of the history by the quantities of (8.6), (10.6), and the function (1.6) ((α) of §6.2), and the threshold shells enter through the derived ingredient (β) of (LF), the large-field integrands through its open ingredient (γ).
- The classical depth, |A_{ε/L}(U^B_{k+1}(V)) − A_ε(U^A_k(V))| ≤ 2C_bL^{−k}ε₁²N_s⁴, is derived in §9 ((9.10), σ = 1): the blocking artifact at the regular field U^B is first order in ε/a by Taylor expansion with the C^{1,1} regularity of VAR Theorem 1 ((9.4), the weights of the linearised averaging computed in §9.8), and the gap between A_ε(MU^B) and the minimum A_ε(U^A) is second order ((9.9)), by VAR's contraction (116)–(121) at the reference configuration MU^B, whose residual is the propagator 𝔊 applied to the first variation of the blocking artifact in divergence form ((9.5), (9.7)), bounded through PBG (3.42), (3.44); the cube-gauge regularity (3.35)–(3.36) of MU^B is derived in §9.8. The minimiser half of the configuration depth follows ((9.8), §9.7, §9.8 for the localised lift): the two stopping-scale minimisers differ on T_{ε/L} by a field of VAR-norm ≤ C(ε₀)(ε/a)^{1−2ε₀}ε₁.
- The Gaussian depth (configuration depth, covariance half) is derived in §10 (the alignment (10.3), the action and constraint parts (10.6a), the gauge-fixing part (10.6b), the π-part, which vanishes identically (β_π ≡ 0), and the Assembly through (10.5′), with the plain-to-double-bar bridge (B1)–(B5) of §10.1, not yet reviewed): the step covariances RG I (1.4)–(1.5) of the two runs on the shared lattice T_{εL^{j−1}}, in the backgrounds U^A_j on T_ε and U^B_{j+1} on T_{ε/L}, differ by a kernel bounded by r_j times the covariance's own bound (3.132), r_j ≤ C(ε₀, γ_H)[L^{−γ_H(j−1)} + ε₁L^{−(1−ε₀)j}/α_*] ((10.6)), from the penalty form of the Schur complement, the chain rule for the averagings, a bilinear defect that is the second variation of the blocking artifact, and the C^{1,γ_H} regularity PBG (3.133) of the minimal lifts. The defect enters §8.1 as a quadratic insertion and is bounded in §10.5.
- (T2) The large-field weight (§11): (T2-i) ĉ₀ := c₀M^dR_{k+1}^{d+1}(3·2^{d−1})^{−1}(2L²)^{−d} ≥ log C_d + 2; (T2-ii) p₀(g_a)/4 ≥ (log C_d + 1)100^d + 1; (H) 3·2^{d−1}L^d(C_ℰ + C_P) ≤ R_n and 2^dL^dM^dR_n^d(c_n + C_P) ≤ ⅛γ₀A₁²p₀²(g_n) at every scale n ≤ k, c_n := C_ℰ + C_λ(1 + log g_n^{−2}) (the history volume and cost, (11.2)), C_P := max(2, 2C_M); Bałaban's three conditions for (1.80) with the gain halved and the cost raised by λ_n := 1 + 3·2^{d−1}L^dC_λ(1 + log g_n^{−2}) (§11(d)); and β₀ ≤ 1/7. All hold for ḡ small, uniformly in the loop, with p₀ > r(d + 2) + 1, Bałaban's "p₀ large" (LFR II p.385 l.37, p.387 l.28), a choice that CRE p.246 l.14's p₀ ≥ 5r allows: R_n ≥ (log g_n^{−2})^r and p₀(g) = A₀(log g^{−2})^{p₀} (CRE (2.4)–(2.5)). It is compatible with Bałaban's other conditions on the exponents: p₁ < p₀ (LFR I p.183 l.2), ½ν ≤ p₀ − p₁ − 1 with a positive integer ν (LFR I p.198 l.55–56), and 2p₁ − (d+5)r₀ > p₀ (LFR II p.383 l.28) admit an integer p₁ once p₀ ≥ (d+5)r₀ + ν + 4, since ν and r₀ do not depend on p₀; and T₀ ≥ 2 of §8.2 holds with the integers q₀, q₁ > p₀ of CRE (2.28), which are bounded below only. They give the relative weight δ_LF := e^{2C_K}(e^s − 1) of (11.3), C_K := C_fsN_s⁴(1 + log g_a^{−1}) the final-scale constant of (11.1), bounded uniformly in K, with w_{ℓ₀} ≥ (1 + δ_LF)^{−1}; the Cauchy estimate uses only this. The smallness δ_LF ≤ ½ needs in addition (T2-v) 8e^{2C_K}N_s⁴(MR_k)^{−4}e^{−(3/2)p₀(g_a)} ≤ 1, the continuum note's third (C-LF) condition with 8e^{2C_K} in place of 2; it depends on the loop through N_s and holds for ḡ ≤ ḡ₀(q, T_ph/u) when p₀ > 4r + 1 (§11(c)).
- (T-Λ) The depth of U₀ (`WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §4(c)), used through (LF): ε₁ = C(M)R_kε_k below the smallness that VAR Sect. G needs for the constant C₁ := max(1, C_X(M)C_Φ(M)/κ_Λ) of that section, which depends only on M and Bałaban's constants, and the smallness conditions on ε₁ of the alignment of that note's §4(d), listed there. It holds for γ small, uniformly in K.
- (T-c) The complex class (`WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §4(d), *The complex class.*): the conditions of (T-Λ) with 26ε₁′ in place of ε₁, where ε₁′ := C(M)R_k(ε_k + 60(α̂₀ + α̂₁) + 25g_kb_k) and α̂_i := max_{m≤k}α_{i,m}, and 1560(α̂₀ + α̂₁) ≤ min(α₁, α_J). It holds for γ small, uniformly in K.
- Result (§7): |F_{K+1}(C) − F_K(C)| ≤ a_K with a_K := (the direct bounds of §4.1–4.2) + C_aℓ_KN_s⁴[(K+1)θ̃^{K−j(C)} + (j(C)+2)w_K] + b^{(S)}_KB_W + δ_LF(a^{(U)}_K + b^{(M)}_K)B_W, b^{(S)}_K the stopping-scale shell bound of §3 (soft tests), ℓ_K := (log(bK + ḡ^{−2}))^{rd+2p₀} the polylogarithm collecting the cube counts (100MR_j)^d, the shells' p₀(g_j)², and c(g_j) at the shared scales, μ_K := K^{1−κ₀/2}ℓ_K, w_K := (K + 1)(θ̃^{k_s/8} + 2^{−k_s/16}) + μ_K with the stopping scale k_s := K − j(C) of §8, b^{(M)}_K the bound of §6.2 on the change of F by the matched large-field pairs, summable in K since κ₀ ≥ 7 (CRE p.263 l.50), given the open ingredient (γ) of the input (LF) for the young matched pairs (§6.2) and the coarse R-terms (§8.6): item (9) of the continuum note.
- Review status: §11, §6.1, §6.2, §8.6, §8.7, §9, the rest of §8, and the condition (T2) reviewed and repaired, the repairs unreviewed; the boundary-term additions to §8.2(a), §8.4, §8.6, §8.7, and the constants line unreviewed; §§1–5, §7, §10, and the conditions (T1) and (T-c) of this block unreviewed.

## 1. Setting

Run A: lattice spacing ε = uL^{−K}, bare coupling g^A_0 = g_0(ε, ḡ), k := K − j(C) steps to the stopping lattice T_a, a = uL^{−j(C)}, with N_s⁴ points. Run B: spacing ε/L, g^B_0 = g_0(ε/L, ḡ), k + 1 steps to the same T_a. Both are pinned at the unit scale, g^A_K = g^B_{K+1} = ḡ (RG I Theorem 2; ONE_LOOP_PROOFS Corollary 8.2). Run B's step j + 1 acts on the same pair of lattices as run A's step j, j = 1, …, k; the shared scale index is j, and run B's step 1 is the extra step. Couplings at the shared scale j: g^A_j and g^B_{j+1}, with δ_j := (g^B_{j+1})^{−2} − (g^A_j)^{−2}, δ_K = 0.

F_K(C) = N_K/(κ_K Z_K) with κ_K = κ^{(k)} the run's own product of per-step perimeter and corner coefficients (map l.264), so that at the stopping scale F_K is the expectation of the normalised dressed loop 𝒲^A_k = W(C^{(k)}; V) + (dev insertions), leading coefficient 1, against the stopping-scale density ρ^A_k, plus the remainder r_K (continuum note (3.2), (6.3)). The same for run B with 𝒲^B_{k+1} and ρ^B_{k+1}. The comparison is made at T_a with each run's κ divided out; no product of per-step coefficients is compared across runs.

*Why not on the common fine lattice.* After run B's first step both runs are densities on T_ε, and F_{K+1} − F_K can be written exactly through ρ^B_1/ρ^A_0 and the leading word W_ε(C; V). That identity splits into a covariance term and a normalisation term (κ^B_1κ_K/κ_{K+1} − 1)F_K, and the latter carries the two runs' perimeter coefficients at every scale, each multiplied by the perimeter P_{j−1}(C) = 4ℓ_C L^{k−j+1} at its scale; the two terms cancel a factor exp(±O(g⁴L^k)) between them. The stopping-scale comparison has no such cancellation to control.

## 2. The Matched Decomposition (Derived)

Both stopping-scale densities have the form CRE (2.18): ρ = Σ_{{Ω_j},{Λ_j}} χ(Ω_last) 𝐓 exp A, summed over admissible sequences of domains, one domain pair per step. Run B's sequences have k + 1 entries, run A's k. The R-operation acts on a component Z′ of the large-field region by replacing ρ(Z, V) with ρ(Z″, V) times a quotient of integrals (LFR I (0.3)), ρ(Z″, V) being the term of the same sum with Z′ removed from the sequence, the quotient entering as an R-term (LFR I (0.5)–(0.6)); a component is renormalised once it satisfies LFR I p.177's conditions (i) (contained in a cube of size 100MR_k) and (ii) (no new large field inside during the preceding N steps). So every term of run B's stopping-scale representation is one of two kinds:

- (M) its step-1 pair is (T, T) (no large-field region alive from run B's extra step; its renormalised step-1 regions survive only as R-terms); dropping the step-1 entry gives an admissible run-A sequence, and this is a bijection between (M) and run A's terms, since the admissibility conditions (CRE pp.255–256) are the same at the shared scales, with R_j := R̄(K − j) in both runs, so that R^A_j = R^B_{j+1} (the paragraph on the cube sizes at a crossing);
- (U) its step-1 pair is not (T, T): a component born at run B's step 1 is alive at the stopping scale.

So ρ^B_{k+1} − ρ^A_k = Σ_{τ} (ρ^B[τ⁺] − ρ^A[τ]) + Σ_{(U)} ρ^B[·], and the same for the numerators with the dressed loops.

## 3. A Matched Pair at the Stopping Scale (Exact)

Fix a matched pair τ⁺, τ, and the all-small-field term first (every Ω_j = Λ_j = T). On the last domain the position-dependent coupling of CRE (2.24) is constant, and (2.23) with (2.25), (2.30) gives the exponent of each run as −g^{−2}_{stop}A_fine(U(V)) + Σ_j irr_j(U(V)) + Σ_j[𝐑^{(j)}(U) − 𝐑^{(j)}(1)] − E, where irr_j := 𝐄^{(j)}(U) − 𝐄^{(j)}(1) − β_j A(φ_j, U) is the quantity bounded by CRE (2.43) and the coupling renormalisations β_j have been absorbed into g_stop by (2.24). For run A, g_stop = g^A_k, A_fine = A_ε, U = U^A_k(V), the minimiser of CRE (2.12) on T_ε with M^kU = V; for run B, g_stop = g^B_{k+1}, A_fine = A_{ε/L}, U = U^B_{k+1}(V) on T_{ε/L} with M^{k+1}U = V.

*The lift.* For a field U on T_ε regular at scale k let ι(U) be the one-step minimiser of A_{ε/L} with block data U (CRE (2.12) at one step; existence, regularity, analyticity in U, and gauge covariance from VAR Theorem 1 and RG I p.262 (iv), p.263 (1.17)); M ι(U) = U exactly, so M^{k+1}ι(U^A_k(V)) = V. For a localisation domain X let ι_X be the localised one-step minimiser of §9.8, *The Localised Lift.*, a function of U on X^∼ with Mι_XU = U on X^∼ and ι_X1 = 1. Let Q₁^{pl}(W) := dM(W) be the plain linearised one-step averaging at a configuration W on T_{ε/L} (§10.3, *The Adjoint Averaging.*). The pair lift of a pair (𝐔, 𝐉) on X^∼, 𝐉 a current variable as in RG I (1.14), is (ι_X𝐔, Q₁^{pl}(ι_X𝐔)*𝐉).

The difference of the two exponents at the same V is then exactly

(3.1) D_τ(V) = −δ_k A_{ε/L}(U^B_{k+1}) − (g^A_k)^{−2}[A_{ε/L}(U^B_{k+1}) − A_ε(U^A_k)] + irr^B_1(U^B_{k+1}) + Σ_{j=1}^{k} irr(Δ_j)(U^A_k) + Σ_{j=1}^{k}[irr^B_{j+1}(U^B_{k+1}) − irr^G(U^A_k)] + (the same three groups for the R-terms) − (E^B − E^A),

with Δ_j(X, ·, z) := G(X, ·, z) − 𝐄^{A,(j)}(X, ·, z) and G(X, (𝐔, 𝐉), z) := 𝐄^{B,(j+1)}(X, (ι_X𝐔, Q₁^{pl}(ι_X𝐔)*𝐉), z), run B's term at the pair lift. Both are evaluated at the diagonal 𝐉 = J(U), J(W) the current (I.1.8) of the configuration W, so Δ_j is a functional of fields on T_ε. It is written here through its irrelevant part irr(Δ_j) := Δ_j(U) − Δ_j(1) − Π^Δ_{22,11}A(h_z, U) − N_j(U), with Δ_j's own coefficient Π^Δ_{22,11} = Π^G_{22,11} − β^A_j and N_j the plane-resolved part of G's moment (*The plane-resolved moment.* below). The coefficient differs from Δβ_j := β^B_{j+1} − β^A_j by at most C_Π(j)E₀e^{−δ₀M₁L^j}, C_Π(j) := C′_Π(8B₃/α_{2,j})² (§5, *The moment of the lifted term.*). The vacuum constants E^B − E^A are pure numbers for this term and cancel in F.

In the variation group, irr^G(U^A_k) := G(Λ_j, U^A_k, z) − G(Λ_j, 1, z) − Π^G_{22,11}A_ε(h_z, U^A_k) − N_j(U^A_k), with G at the diagonal and the Wilson action of T_ε at U^A_k. Since ι_X1 = 1 and J(1) = 0, G(Λ_j, 1, z) = 𝐄^{B,(j+1)}(Λ_j, 1, z). So the telescoping is exact: irr(Δ_j)(U^A_k) = irr^G(U^A_k) − irr^A_j(U^A_k), and the group adds irr^B_{j+1}(U^B_{k+1}) − irr^G(U^A_k), where irr^B_{j+1}(U^B_{k+1}) carries A_{ε/L}(h_z, U^B_{k+1}). The sum of the two is the same for every coefficient in place of Π^G_{22,11} and every N_j; G's own marginal part makes each piece the irrelevant part of one term.

*The plane-resolved moment.* For the coordinate planes P = (κ, μ), κ < μ, and P′ = (λ, ν), λ < ν, let Φ_P(x) := (1/iε²)log U(∂p_P(x)) be the plaquette field of the plaquette p_P(x) with the corner x spanned by e_κ and e_μ, and let A_{PP′}(h_z, U) := ½Σ_{x∈T_ε}ε⁴h_z(x)tr Φ_P(x)Φ_{P′}(x), the plane-pair action with the weights of A_ε(h_z, ·). It is gauge invariant, since both loops start at x, and holomorphic where the plaquettes are small; at real configurations Φ_P and Φ_{P′} are Hermitian, so the trace is real. Let Π^G_{μν,κλ}(z) be the tensor of CRE (3.57) for G at the point z, the sum over X extended to the localisation domains of the infinite lattice as there. Its independence of z uses the translation invariance ("This function is translation invariant", CRE p.281 l.59), which G is not known to have, so it is taken at each z, and every subtraction below is at that z. Then

N_j(U) := Σ_{κ<μ, λ<ν}(Π^G_{μν,κλ} − Π^G_{22,11}δ_{μν}δ_{κλ})A_{(κμ)(λν)}(h_z, U),

the part of G's marginal term off its isotropic coefficient. For a family with the Euclidean covariance (2.29), CRE (3.60)–(3.61) give N = 0. G is not known to be covariant: (2.29) holds only for the sum over X at one configuration (CRE p.260 l.2–4), and G evaluates each X at its own ι_X𝐔. N_j is what this costs, and its coefficients are boundary terms of the localised lift (§5, *The moment of the lifted term.*). The functions h_z are CRE's tents on T_{L^{−j}}, h(t) = max{1 − |t|, 0} (CRE p.283 l.12–15), the same for both runs since both use the blocks T^{(j)}, with Σ_zh_z = 1 on each fine lattice.

The two all-small-field terms also differ in their supports. Each carries the stopping-scale test χ_k(T) of (2.18), built on its own fine lattice, and the fluctuation cutoffs (3.16), whose shells are §8.2(b); its other plaquette, axial, and reference tests are equal to 1 on the support of its other characteristic functions (`WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §3(a)). With the plaquette and reference tests soft at every K and in both runs (that note's §3(d)), the shells of χ_k(T) change F_{K+1} − F_K by at most b^{(S)}_KB_W, b^{(S)}_K := 4κ_s^{−1}e^{2C_K}N_s⁴[2C(ε/a)^{1−2ε₀} + 4Cg_a²|δ_k|], κ_s := β/30 the width of the soft tests, by the reading (R-s) (that note's §3(d)). b^{(S)}_K is summable in K given the birth-scale form of the open input (LF): its coupling part 4Cg_a²|δ_k| is summable by (8.6), which uses (LF) through h_m.

The five groups are of four kinds. (a) The coupling difference δ_k times the action, with |A_{ε/L}(U^B_{k+1}(V))| ≤ C ε_k² N_s⁴ for V regular at scale k, and δ_k = Σ_{m>k}(β^B_{m+1} − β^A_m) by (2.24) and the pin (the continuum note's (9.4)(d)). (b) The difference of the two constrained minima, bracketed by two one-step artifacts: by the minimality of U^B_{k+1} and of U^A_k among the competitors in VAR's convexity ball (115), which contains MU^B_{k+1} for run A's problem (§9.1) and ι(U^A_k) for run B's (§9.7, the distance σ_k),

(3.2) A_{ε/L}(U^B_{k+1}) − A_ε(MU^B_{k+1}) ≤ A_{ε/L}(U^B_{k+1}) − A_ε(U^A_k) ≤ A_{ε/L}(ι(U^A_k)) − A_ε(U^A_k),

since MU^B_{k+1} and ι(U^A_k) are admissible competitors. The difference Δ itself is bounded in §9, (9.10), for fields regular at scale k (CRE (3.12), VAR's B₃). (c) The extra generation irr^B_1 and 𝐑^{B,(1)}: §4.1. (d) The same-generation differences, split as the frozen difference Δ_j at run A's own configuration plus the variation of run B's frozen term between the two configurations U^B_{k+1}(V) and ι_X(U^A_k(V)): §5 and the configuration-depth item.

For matched terms with large-field regions the same split applies to the small-field exponent on the last domain; in addition the characteristic functions χ_k (CRE (2.17)), the operations 𝐓_k (CRE (2.19)–(2.21)), the boundary terms 𝐁 (CRE (2.40)–(2.42)), and the relative constants E are each run's own, built on its fine lattice. Their differences are sorted in §6.2: the old pairs are measured against each run's own Z_{ℓ₀}, and for the young pairs the small-field ingredients are (α), the shells of the tests that are not identically 1 are (β), and the large-field integrands and 𝐁 are (γ).

## 4. The Direct Bounds (Derived)

### 4.1 The Extra Generation

On the all-small-field term, Γ_{k+1} = T_a in run B's indexing. CRE (2.43) at j = 1 with Ω = T_a gives |irr^B_1(U^B_{k+1}(V))| ≤ E₁ L^{−βk} N_s⁴ (checked: the sum Σ_{n=1}^{k+1}(L^{1−n})^β|Γ_n ∩ Ω| has the single term n = k + 1). CRE (2.44) at j = 1 gives |𝐑^{B,(1)}(U) − 𝐑^{B,(1)}(1)| ≤ R₁ (g^B_1)^{κ₀} N_s⁴, and (g^B_1)^{−2} ≥ ḡ^{−2} + bK − C by ONE_LOOP_PROOFS Lemma 2.1(i) at the shared scale 1 (K steps below the pin), so this is ≤ R₁ N_s⁴ (bK − C)^{−κ₀/2}, summable for κ₀ ≥ 3 (CRE p.263 l.50 has κ₀ ≥ 7). On terms with large-field regions, run B's step-1 boundary terms 𝐁^{(1)} are localised at Z_1^∼ (CRE (2.41)), which is empty for (M) after the R-operations, so there are none. The extra generation's direct contribution to D_τ is therefore ≤ (E₁L^{−βk} + R₁(bK − C)^{−κ₀/2}) N_s⁴, summable in K.

### 4.2 The Old Tail

For the same-generation differences of ages k − j ≥ m, the triangle inequality with CRE (2.43), (2.44), (2.46) and Lemma 2.1(i) gives Σ_{j ≤ k−m}|irr^B_{j+1} − irr^A_j| ≤ 2E₁N_s⁴L^{−βm}/(1 − L^{−β}) and Σ_{j ≤ k−m}|Δ𝐑_j| ≤ C N_s⁴(1 + m)^{1−κ₀/2} (the continuum note's (9.2); the cutoff comparison note's (6)–(8)). This is summable in K along m_K = ⌊√K⌋ and not along m_K ∝ log K; it bounds the two runs' differences at the old ages without any information on d_j, and is the fallback where §5 is not used.

## 5. Theorem 2 Read for a Difference (Reading R-Δ, Bałaban's Proof Applied to Δ_j)

*Statement.* Let 𝐄^A and 𝐄^B be two families of generation-j terms on the same lattices, each with CRE p.259's properties (i)–(iv) (localisation in X, analyticity on U^c_j(X, α₀, α₁), gauge invariance, the bound RG I (1.18) with E₀) and, for 𝐄^A, the Euclidean covariance (2.29). For the pair-lift term G of §3 covariance is not used: CRE (3.57) holds for G with its own tensor Π^G_{μν,κλ}, and the part off its isotropic coefficient is the N_j of §3 (*The plane-resolved moment.*). For G, (i) holds with X^∼ in place of X, and (ii) holds on the common polydisc 𝒫_j(X; α′) of *Complex domain.* below. Let their difference Δ satisfy |Δ(X, (𝐔, J(𝐔)), z)| ≤ d E₀ exp(−κd_j(X)) on the norm class 𝒫_j(X; r). Then for z ∈ Λ_j⁰ ∩ (Ω_n ∖ Ω_{n+1}) and U_k regular,

(5.1) |Δ(Λ_j, U_k, z) − Δ(Λ_j, 1, z) − Δβ A(h_z, U_k) − N(U_k)| ≤ d · E₁′ (L^jL^{−n})^{5−β}, Δβ := Π^Δ_{22,11} (Δ's own coefficient, not the Δβ_j = β^B_{j+1} − β^A_j of §3),

with N := N_j for the frozen difference and N := 0 for two covariant families, E₁′ the constant of CRE (3.67) for one term with the 36 plane pairs of N included, and summing over z, the bound (2.43) with E₁ replaced by d E₁. Here β is the small positive exponent of (3.67) (p.283 l.31: "with 1−β, β>0, instead of β<1"), written β_E where it meets other exponents; (2.43)'s β is the complementary number. The age exponent of this note is β′ := ½min(β_H, 1 − β_E), β_H the Hölder index of the class bound (5.1c) below. The two indices are fixed with β_H ≥ 19/20 and β_E ≤ 1/20, so β′ ≥ 19/40: RG I states its Hölder bounds for 0 ≤ β ≤ β^H_0 < 1, β^H_0 RG I's β₀ there (p.276 l.42, p.285 l.23), on VAR (9), which holds up to β = 1 (§9.2), and (3.67) holds for every β > 0. Since 4 + β′ < 5 − β_E and L^{j−n} ≤ 1, (5.1) holds with the exponent 4 + β′ in place of 5 − β.

*Complex domain (reading R-Δ^c).* For z ∈ Λ_j⁰ ∩ (Ω_n ∖ Ω_{n+1}) let □ ∈ π_n be the cube containing z, and let the near domain of z be □₀ := □̃⁵, of the side 11M in the L^{−n}-scale, 11MLⁿη in the units of T_η (RG I p.257 l.7, p.273 l.40). In the first case of CRE p.280 l.14–22, the localisation domains X ⊂ □̃² of p.279 l.45–48, RG I's analysis uses the configuration on □₀ only: the gauges v and u_{k+1} of (I.3.26)–(I.3.27) and the localisation of p.280 l.44–47 live there, the background bounds (I.3.27) hold on □̃⁴, and the gauge transformations are "explicitly given analytic functions of 𝐔, depending on 𝐔 restricted to the cube □₀" (RG I p.276 l.14–15). Let irr(Δ)(U) := Δ(Λ_j, U, z) − Δ(Λ_j, 1, z) − Δβ A(h_z, U) − N(U). The radii are reduced: α′_{i,m} := α_{i,m}/(4B₃) on the layer m, i = 0, 1, and r_{i,m} := ½α′_{i,m}.

The common polydisc of X at radii s ≤ α′ is the set 𝒫_j(X; s) of pairs (𝐔, 𝐉) on X^∼, 𝐉 a current variable as in RG I (1.14), with two properties. The restriction to X lies in run A's U^c_j(X, s_{0,j}, s_{1,j}), with its current at most s_{0,j}/C_{Q*}. The lifted configuration ι_X𝐔 satisfies, on X, the configuration conditions of run B's U^c_{j+1}(X, s_{0,j}, s_{1,j}).

The lifted pair (ι_X𝐔, Q₁^{pl}(ι_X𝐔)*𝐉) then lies, on X, in run B's U^c_{j+1}(X, s_{0,j}, s_{1,j}), its current included. A column of Q₁^{pl}(W)* has L straight terms and at most 2dL^d contour terms of weight L^{−1}, and the term of weight L^{d−1} (§10.3, *The Adjoint Averaging.*), with transports along at most (2d + 1)L bonds. So |Q₁^{pl}(W)*𝐉| ≤ C_{Q*}|𝐉|, C_{Q*} := 2(1 + (2d + 1)L^{d−1}). The factor 2 is the room for the transports and for the operators dlog, dexp, Ad(e^{iY_c}), and Ad(h_{c,x}) of that paragraph at the configurations W of the class, which are within O(s_{1,j}L^{−j}) of real ones on each bond, for γ small. Each transport is a product of at most (2d + 1)L bond factors, and each of the four operators is analytic in the logarithms of such products near their real values, so at W each is within the factor exp(O((2d + 1)Ls_{1,j}L^{−j})) of its value at a real configuration, and that factor is at most 2 for γ small.

Δ(X, ·, z) is analytic on 𝒫_j(X; α′). For 𝐄^A(X, ·) this holds since α′ < α. For G(X, ·) it holds since ι_X is analytic in 𝐔 (§9.8, *The Localised Lift.*), Q₁^{pl}(W)* is analytic in W, and α′_{i,j} ≤ ½α_{i,j} ≤ α^B_{i,j+1}. The last inequality holds where |δ_j| ≤ ½(g^A_j)^{−2}. δ_j and the norms d_i are joint unknowns of the fixed point of §8.7, whose solution (8.6) gives that bound for γ small, so it is not a loop.

The norm class is 𝒫_j(X; r), on which §8.2 takes the norm d_j. Δ depends on 𝐔 on X^∼, not on X alone. The proof below uses the localisation only to split the domains into the near ones, X ⊂ □̃², and the far ones. A near X has X^∼ ⊂ □̃³: X^∼ exceeds X by one layer of M₁-cubes of the scale j (CRE (2.13), p.257 l.9–11), M₁ < M (CRE p.245 l.35–37), and the layers of □̃³ have the width ML^n ≥ ML^j. RG I's output conditions hold on □̃³ (p.277 l.55), "hence on X".

The class of (5.1c) is the set 𝒞_k of pairs (𝐔, J(𝐔)) on T_η with two properties.
- (a) For every localisation domain X ∈ 𝐃_j of the sum Δ(Λ_j, ·, z), the restriction to X^∼ lies in the norm class 𝒫_j(X; r).
- (b) The pair satisfies the bounds (2.34)–(2.39) of Bałaban's multiscale complex class (CRE p.261), with the top index k as in (3.43), on the whole lattice T_η, with r_{0,m}/(2B₃) in place of α_{0,m} and r_{1,m} in place of α_{1,m}.

Condition (a) is the domain of every term of the sum, and (b) is what RG I's analysis on □₀ uses. The radii in (b) are of the form (2.28) with C_i divided by at most 16B₃², and the current bound r_{0,j}/C_{Q*} of (a) is of that form with C₀ divided by 8B₃C_{Q*}. So Bałaban's statements hold at them, by `WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §4(d), *Bałaban's Statements at the Reduced Thresholds*, once C₀ and C₁ are at least λ_Q := 16B₃²C_{Q*} times his lower bounds, which dominates the divisors 16B₃² and 8B₃C_{Q*} since B₃, a constant of upper bounds, is taken ≥ ½ and C_{Q*} ≥ 1. Run A's part of (a) and (b) at the configurations of the callers is then Bałaban's: at real minimisers by his statements at his own thresholds and the current bound of *The lift condition.*, and at complex points by that reading, which is not a check of every use of C₀ and C₁ (header, *Open (LF)*). He evaluates the terms at U_k on every X (CRE p.259 l.40–44), and he places the configuration of (3.15) in the class (3.43). The lift part of (a) is *The lift condition* below. Below, α_{·,m} stands for r_{·,m}, and Lemma 4 is run at these radii.

*The regular subclass.* 𝒮_z is the set of pairs (𝐔, J(𝐔)) of the class, J(𝐔) the current of 𝐔, whose restriction to □₀, in a G^c-valued gauge on □₀, is the local minimiser of its own block data at the scale of the layer, 𝐔 = U_n(□₀, M^·(𝐔)). Here U_n(□₀, ·) is RG I's local function U_j(□₀) of p.273 l.39–42 built at the scale n: a sequence of cubes □₀ ⊃ □₁ ⊃ ⋯ ⊃ □_n satisfying (1.3)–(1.6) [14] in the L^{−n}-scale, with the averages M^p(𝐔) of the scales p ≤ n as data, extended as equal to 𝐔 outside □₀ (RG I p.275 l.4–7). This is CRE's reading of RG I's construction: U_k "restricted to Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1} coincides with a U_n-type configuration", and (I.3.18)–(I.3.28) are applied with η replaced by L^{−n} (CRE p.280 l.14–20, p.276 l.9–14) and with η𝐇_k in place of L^{−1}η𝐇_{k+1} (p.280 l.17–19). Bałaban writes the same representation at the scale j in his second proof of Theorem 2, U_k = U_{j,□₀}(M^·(U_k)) (p.283 l.47–48). On □₀ ∩ Ω_{n+1} the data of the scale n determine the configuration's own data of the scale n + 1, since M^{n+1} = M∘M^n, so they are finer constraints there. The literal U_k(□₀, ·) doesn't work: for k ≫ n its cube sequence does not fit in □₀, and its constraints are not those of the layer. These are the configurations at which RG I's Lemma 4 evaluates a term in CRE's reading, with 𝐀 = 0 in (I.3.30): the composite is then the configuration itself, and its C^{1,β_H} regularity is (I.3.27), at a fixed Hölder index 0 < β_H < 1 not above RG I's Hölder limit there, written β^H_0 here (RG I's β₀ of (I.3.27) and (I.4.18), not CRE's β₀ of (2.6)–(2.7)). 𝒮_z contains the pairs (U_k(𝐕), J(U_k(𝐕))) of the class at complex data 𝐕: a minimiser is the local minimiser of its own block data, as in (I.3.18) (RG I p.273 l.44–46), which CRE applies at the layer n (p.280 l.14–16), and at complex data the identity holds by analytic continuation, both sides being analytic in the data. It also contains the fluctuation-dependent configuration of CRE (3.15), the minimiser at complex data. A family t ↦ U_n(□₀, 𝐕(t)), extended as equal to a fixed configuration of the class outside □₀, at holomorphic complex data stays in 𝒮_z as long as its pairs stay in the class, since M^·U_n(□₀, 𝐕) = 𝐕 on the constrained bonds. The reading R-Δ^c is the bound

(5.1c) |irr(Δ)(U)| ≤ d · E₁(α) (L^{j−n})^{4+β′}, E₁(α) := C_αE₀,

for every U in 𝒮_z whose near domain □₀ satisfies the condition (N) below. The constant C_α is that of *The constant.* below, uniform in j, n, k, K, and the volume.

On the whole class (5.1c) doesn't work: the class bounds the configuration's first derivatives and its current, not a Hölder norm of ∇A, and the power beyond (L^{j−n})⁴ comes from the C^{1,β_H} regularity of the background ((I.3.27), (I.3.32), (I.4.18)); Bałaban states Theorem 2, (2.43), "for β<1, and sufficiently regular configurations U_k=U_k(V)" (CRE p.263 l.19).

On 𝒮_z the bound is uniform and carries no power of the size of U's background. irr(Δ) vanishes to second order at U = 1 and not to fifth: its first variation vanishes by (I.4.14), A(h_z, ·) being quadratic at 1, and its quadratic part, the irrelevant part of Π^Δ left after (3.49)–(3.66), does not vanish in general. Schwarz's lemma along the family that scales the background therefore improves (5.1c) at most by a factor quadratic in the background's size over the class radius.

*Bałaban's analysis on complex configurations.* RG I Lemma 4 (p.280 l.24–32) takes 𝐔 ∈ U′^c_{k+1}(□₀, (1+2β_I)α₀, (1+2β_I)α₁), the space of p.277 l.34 (there with the fourth argument α₀), together with a 𝔤^c-valued 𝐀 satisfying (I.3.31) and a complex B′ with |B′| < α₃. It states (I.3.53) and "The functions in (3.53) are analytic on the above spaces". Here β_I is RG I's class parameter, not the exponent β of (3.67). RG I p.281 l.3–4 states that the remaining terms are irrelevant, "i.e. they satisfy bounds of the form (0.29) on their domains of analyticity".

In CRE's proof of Theorem 2, 𝐀 = 0 in (I.3.30), and L^{−1}η𝐇_{k+1} is replaced by η𝐇_k (p.280 l.17–19). The background is therefore the field A of (I.3.30), built from U_k: "The function L^nηA, where A is defined by (I.3.30), satisfies the bound (I.3.32), but in the L^{−n}-scale instead of the η-scale" (l.19–20). That bound is stated with the class parameters, the background obeying the bounds of (I.3.31) "with α₂ replaced by B₃O(1)Mα₀, and □₀ by □̃⁴" (RG I p.276 l.42–43). So the real proof already measures the background's amplitude in the units of the class; its Hölder regularity is a property of the composite minimiser, which is why the bound is stated on 𝒮_z.

The field U_k "may depend on the fluctuation field, as in the exponential in (3.15)" (CRE p.279 l.43–44). The expressions needed in the fluctuation integral come from the derivative in t_□ (p.283 l.23–25), the parameter multiplying the fluctuation field inside the configuration (RG I (3.28)). RG I p.280 l.47–49 bounds that derivative by "a factor O(1)α₂^{−1}ε₁".

*The near domain (derived under (N) and at n = j; open for n > j otherwise).* The bounds below use that the restriction of U to □₀ satisfies, in the L^{−n}-scale, the conditions of RG I's space U′^c_{k+1}(□₀, (1+2β_I)α_{0,n}, (1+2β_I)α_{1,n}, α_{0,n}) on □₀, with α = r the radii of the norm class. That space asks (i)–(iii) "on the whole lattice T_η" (RG I p.275 l.20–21). The global conditions enter RG I's proof only through the fluctuation field 𝐇_k(B′) of (I.3.25). It is built from the propagators of the whole lattice: its operators are those of VAR (179)–(180), with 𝐔, 𝐉 in place of U_{k+1}, J_{k+1} (RG I p.273 l.2–3). In CRE's reading 𝐇_k(B′) = 0 (p.280 l.17–18). The remaining objects of (I.3.25)–(I.3.32) are functions of 𝐔 restricted to □₀: the averages M^·(𝐔) with their axial gauge v of (I.3.26) and the gauge u_{k+1} of (I.3.27) ("explicitly given analytic functions of 𝐔, depending on 𝐔 restricted to the cube □₀", p.276 l.14–15), and the local minimiser's field 𝐇(□₀, ·) of (I.3.27) and the composite of (I.3.30) at 𝐀 = 0. The proof states its input bounds on □₀ and □̃⁴ ((I.3.26)–(I.3.27)) and its output conditions on □̃³ (p.277 l.55). So its bounds hold for configurations that satisfy (i)–(iv) on □₀, and no extension outside □₀ is used.

The proof does not use the cube condition (I.1.12). Its inputs on □₀ are the averages M^·(𝐔) with the axial gauge v of (I.3.26), the gauge u_{k+1} and the field 𝐇(□₀, ·) of (I.3.27), and the composite of (I.3.30). (I.3.26) repeats VAR Sect. F (RG I p.275 l.21–26), and VAR derives its axial-gauge bounds from the plaquette condition and Proposition 2 of [4] (VAR p.300 l.38–40), so it uses (I.1.11) for U, (I.1.14) for 𝐔, and |A′| < α₁ of (ii). VAR Sect. F concerns real minimal configurations, and RG I gives no separate proof of (I.3.26) at complex 𝐔. So its complex version is a reading: the G^c-valued gauge freedom puts M^·(U) in the axial gauge through the plaquettes alone, and v absorbs the complex factor U′. The VAR minimiser and (I.3.27) need only |V(b) − 1| < O(1)Mα₀ on the bonds of the averages. The condition (iv) comes from (1.17), which RG I derives from the plaquettes of the averages and VAR Proposition 9 (p.263 l.10–17). The outputs come from (I.3.37) and (I.3.50) for (i)–(ii), from (I.3.41)–(I.3.47) and (I.3.51)–(I.3.52) for (iii), and from the identity (I.3.38) for (iv) (p.277 l.55, p.278 l.12–13, p.280 l.6 and l.21); the output's real factor is 1, so its (I.1.12) holds with A = 0. The statement of p.275 l.6–7 that "all necessary theorems are valid" concerns the global operations, which enter only through 𝐇_k(B′) = 0. So the membership needed is that of (I.1.11), (I.1.13), (I.1.14), and (iv) on □₀, and CRE's cube condition (2.38) is not among the inputs.

The condition (N) is that □₀ lies in Ω_n, so that it meets no finer layer. Under (N) the membership follows from the condition (b) of the class:
- On the layer n the radii are those of the layer, and the factor 1 − β_C(1 − 2^{−(k−n)}) ≤ 1 of (2.34)–(2.39) leaves the room 1 + 2β_I. Here β_C is CRE's class constant ("e.g., we can take β=1/4", p.261 l.42), taken as β_C = 1/8; CRE's uses of its β are counted against 1/8 in `WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §4(d), *The choice β = 1/8, checked.*, and nothing below depends on the choice.
- (2.34) gives the plaquettes of (I.1.11) and (I.1.14) at α_{0,n}/(2B₃). (2.36) gives the 𝐉-bound of (I.1.14) with γ₀ = α_{0,n}/(2B₃) ≤ α_{0,n}, and (2.39) gives (ii) at α_{1,n}.
- The condition (iv) on □₀ follows by RG I (1.17) at ξ = L^{−n}, with □₀ as Ω₀. The conditions (i)–(iii) hold at α′₀ := (1+2β_I)α_{0,n}/(2B₃), so (1.17) gives (I.1.16) at 2B₃α′₀ = (1+2β_I)α_{0,n}, the radius of the space, for the configuration and for its real part.
- On the layers n + p, p ≥ 1, the bounds of (2.34), (2.36), and (2.39), rescaled to the L^{−n}-scale, are ≤ (1+β₀)²p^{1/2}L^{−p} ≤ 1 times those of the layer n, compared directly with the room at the layer n, by CRE (2.6)–(2.7) at the distance p, for L ≥ 2 and β₀ ≤ 1/7: at p = 1 it is (64/49)·½ < 0.66, and p^{1/2}L^{−p} decreases in p. The power L^{−p} is the worst over the conditions (L^{−2p} in (2.34), L^{−3p} in (2.36), L^{−p} and L^{−2p} in (2.39)). (2.35) and (2.37) are not used, since (iv) comes from (1.17).

*Mixed radii (derived).* Lemma 4 maps its space at the radii (α₀, α₁) into U^c_j(X, α₀, α₁) at the same radii, while here the input carries the layer-n radii and the term is analytic at the scale-j radii (CRE p.259 l.41–42). At n = j Lemma 4 does not apply: its margin (1 + 7β_I)L^{−2} ≤ 1 rests on L^{j−1}η ≤ L^{−1} (RG I p.279 l.28), and the replacement factor is L⁰ = 1, so (I.3.47) gives (1 + 7β_I)α_{0,j}ξ² > α_{0,j}ξ². There (L^{j−n})^{4+β′} = 1, and irr(Δ) is bounded directly. Σ_X|Δ(X, U, z)| ≤ O(1)dE₀, since by the condition (a) of the class U lies in the norm class 𝒫_j(X; r) of every X. The same holds at U = 1, where ι_X1 = 1, and |Δβ A(h_z, U)| ≤ O(1)dE₀c₂^{−2}, and the same holds for N(U), a sum of 36 plane-pair actions with coefficients at most 2 sup|Π^Δ|, so (5.1c) holds at n = j with C_α ≥ O(1)(2 + c₂^{−2}), the O(1) counting these 36 pieces. For n > j, α_{i,n} ≤ (1+β₀)²(n−j)^{1/2}α_{i,j} (*The constant.*), and in CRE's reading the inequalities (I.3.37)–(I.3.52) carry a factor at most L^{j−n} in place of RG I's L^{j−1}η, squared in (I.3.41) and (I.3.43)–(I.3.47) and cubed in (I.3.42). Since (1+β₀)²m^{1/2}L^{−m} ≤ 1 and (1 + 7β_I)(1+β₀)²m^{1/2}L^{−2m} ≤ 1 for m ≥ 1, L ≥ 2, β₀ ≤ 1/7, and β_I ≤ 1/4, each conclusion of the proof holds with the scale-j radii on its right-hand side. The steps (I.3.51)–(I.3.52), with the factors 1 + 6β_I and 1 + 8β_I and 2B₃α_{3,j} = β_IL^{−2}α_{0,j}, hold the same way. The α₃-term 2B₃α_{3,j} = β_IL^{−2}α_{0,j} is fixed at the scale j and carries no factor m^{1/2}L^{−2m}, so the bound is (1 + 7β_I)(1+β₀)²m^{1/2}L^{−2m} + β_IL^{−2} ≤ (11/4)(64/49)/4 + 1/16 < 0.97 at m = 1, L = 2, and it decreases in m. In particular |𝐇_j| < B₃²O(1)Mα_{0,j} in (I.3.37), which with B₃²O(1)Mα_{0,j} < ½α_{1,j} gives (i)–(ii) at the scale j, and the bound (1 + 7β_I)α_{0,j} of (I.3.47) holds. The smallness conditions O(1)Mα₁ ≤ β_I and (4B₃²O(1)M)²α₀ ≤ β_I (RG I p.278 l.46 and l.64) are taken at the radii of the layer n, and the restrictions on α₃ of (I.3.51) at the scale j.

This is the derivation for run A's term. For Δ, Lemma 4's output must also lie in run B's space through the pair lift, which is the lift condition at the composites, open (*The lift condition.*).

**Open (R-Δ^c-T):** the points z with n > j whose near domain □₀ does not satisfy (N), that is those whose □₀ meets a finer layer m < n; at n = j the direct bound of *Mixed radii.* below covers every □₀. On a finer layer m ≥ 1 the bounds are weaker in the L^{−n}-scale, by L^{2(n−m)} in (2.34), L^{3(n−m)} in (2.36), and L^{n−m} for |A′| and L^{2(n−m)} for |∇A′| in (2.39), each with the ratio α_{·,m}/α_{·,n} of CRE (2.6)–(2.7); so the restriction is not in the space at the radii of the layer n. Where □₀ reaches Ω₁^c, the case m = 0, the class (b) gives no bound at all, since (2.34)–(2.39) bound only the layers n ≥ 1 and Ω_j (CRE p.261 l.24, l.40). For real configurations the points are covered by the statements of (2.43) and (3.67). CRE p.276 l.9–11 states that the regularity properties of U_{k+1} "on a neighborhood of Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1}" "are the same as for U_n-configurations", for the bounds of RG I §§3–4 with η replaced by L^{−n}; it does not give the width of the neighbourhood, so it does not decide the points z within 5ML^n of the finer layers. What closes it for m ≥ 1: the bound at the age of the finest layer met, the layer of smallest index, with the count of such points z against the |Γ_m| of (2.43); the width of the layers in units of the near domain enters only through that count. For m = 0 it needs a bound on Ω₁^c, which the count of (2.43), starting at m = 1, does not cover.

*The constant (derived).* The radii come first. With α_{i,m} = g_mC_i(log g_m^{−2})^{q_i} (CRE (2.28)), CRE (2.6)–(2.7) (p.255) give α_{i,n}/α_{i,j} ≤ (1+β₀)²(n−j)^{1/2} for n > j. RG I requires B₃²O(1)Mα₀ < ½α₁ (p.277 l.54). CRE takes q₀ and q₁ "integers greater than 1" and C₀ and C₁ "sufficiently large" (p.259 l.50), with no other condition on them in CRE; the reading takes q₀ ≤ q₁ and C₁ > 2B₃²O(1)MC₀, so that B₃²O(1)Mα_{0,m} < ½α_{1,m} at every m. CRE writes C₁ also in (3.43), the bound |A| < C₁p₁(g_k) of the fluctuation field, p₁(g) = (log g^{−2})^{p₁} as in LFR I p.183 l.2 (CRE p.276 l.29–31); the print does not say whether it is the constant of (2.28). Either way the conditions here are compatible with it: they are lower bounds on C₀ and C₁, as CRE's "sufficiently large" is (p.259 l.50), and the pages read put no upper bound on C₁. If it is the same constant, the fluctuation domain |A| < C₁p₁(g_k) must still lie inside the margin α_{3,k}, which is proportional to g_kC₀(log g_k^{−2})^{q₀}, the fluctuation entering the configuration as g_kA. That holds for γ small, since q₀ > p₀ > p₁ (LFR I p.183 l.2), and then γ depends on C₁/C₀. The radius α₂ of (I.3.14) and (I.4.4) depends "on α₀ and on some absolute constants" (RG I p.272 l.38–39). It is fixed by the room between the space U^c_j(X, ½α₀, ½α₁, α₀) of (3.13) and the domain U^c_j(X, α₀, α₁) of the term. The substitution (3.13) moves the conditions (i)–(ii) by O(1)max(|𝐀|, |∇𝐀|) and the current by O(1)max(|𝐀|, |P₁𝐀|, |∇𝐀|, |Δ𝐀|), since F₁ is a local operator in 𝐔, ∂𝐔, 𝐀, and ∇𝐀 that vanishes at 𝐀 = 0 (p.272 l.19–22). The rooms are ½α₀, ½α₁, and (1 − L^{−3})α₀, so α_{2,m} := c₂min(α_{0,m}, α_{1,m}) = c₂α_{0,m}, with c₂ depending at most on d, L, and B₃, is admissible. The room in (iv) under the shift exp iξ𝐀 is paid through Proposition 9, hence the B₃.

The fifth-order term of (I.3.34) is bounded by (I.3.54) (RG I p.280 l.35–42). This is a Cauchy integral over |σ| = r of 𝐄 itself, ≤ E₀α₃^{−5}|B|⁵exp(−κd_j(X)), and the print continues "|B|<O(1)L^jη, e.g. |B|<α₁L^jη" (l.44). CRE replaces η by L^{−n} (p.280 l.20–22). On 𝒮_z the background bound (I.3.27) at the layer n then gives |B| ≤ O(1)B₃Mα_{0,n}L^{j−n}. RG I requires B₃α₃ < ½α₁ and 2B₃α₃ ≤ β_IL^{−2}α₀ (p.280 l.6 and l.16), so α_{3,j} := β_IL^{−2}α_{0,j}/(2B₃) is admissible. So the remainder is ≤ E₀C_r⁵max(1, n−j)^{5/2}(L^{j−n})⁵exp(−κd_j(X)), with C_r := O(1)B₃²ML²β_I^{−1}(1+β₀)². It holds on □̃⁴. The localisation of the inner product in □̃⁴ and its complement is Bałaban's statement. The parts with at least one factor localised in (□̃⁴)^c "can be estimated by an arbitrary power of L^jη using the exponential decay properties", by considerations "similar to those done on several occasions, e.g. (3.6), (3.21)", which he does not repeat (RG I p.280 l.45–47). In CRE's reading this is a bound C_loc E₀(L^{j−n})⁵exp(−κd_j(X)) at the power 5. C_loc is the constant of that statement, an equal-scale constant of Bałaban's like those in C_{WT}; the statement is Bałaban's, sketched and accepted as presented. In its transfer to the mixed radii, a background of the layer n and a term of the scale j, the radii enter through the ratio α_{0,n}/α_{2,j} ≤ c₂^{−1}(1+β₀)²max(1, n−j)^{1/2}, as in ρ below, so the transfer costs at most a power of max(1, n−j), which C_{β′} absorbs below.

The terms n = 2, 3, and 4 of (I.3.34) are n-linear in B. Their coefficients are the Taylor coefficients at U = 1, which (I.4.3) writes as Cauchy integrals of 𝐄^{(j)}(X, exp iξ𝐀) on the domain (I.4.4). This gives the bound (I.4.5), (2n²B₃/α₂)ⁿE₀exp(−κd_j(X)) times the product of the sup norms of the arguments (RG I p.282 l.10–17), with the radius α_{2,j} of the term. After the Ward–Takahashi identities the irrelevant parts carry derivatives of B. The bounds on these are (I.4.17), |∂B| < O(1)(α₂ + B₃O(1)Mα₀)(L^jη)², and (I.4.18), |∂∂B| < α₁(L^jη)^{2+β} for 0 ≤ β ≤ β^H_0 < 1, which comes from the Hölder norms of (I.3.32) (RG I p.285 l.14–24). The α₂ of (4.17) comes from the field 𝐀 of (I.3.31)–(I.3.32), and 𝐀 = 0 in CRE's reading, so it is absent. In CRE's reading every factor B, ∂B, and ∂∂B then carries the amplitude O(1)B₃Mα_{0,n} of (I.3.27) at the layer n, times L^{j−n}, (L^{j−n})², and (L^{j−n})^{2+β_H} respectively. An irrelevant part has the power (L^{j−n})^{4+β_H} or a higher one, the least power belonging to the parts with one factor ∂∂B; this is (3.67)'s 5 − β at β = 1 − β_H. The parts with a factor |Γ_{x,x₃}| are summed with the exponential decay as for (I.4.22) (p.286 l.2–21). (4.22) itself is stated after the differentiation in t_□ (p.282 l.24–25) and is used here only for that summation. A part of order r is r-linear in B. The commutator terms produced by the Ward–Takahashi identities carry fewer factors α_{2,j}^{−1} than factors B, and α_{2,j} ≤ 1, so the bound of a part of order r is at most the r-th power of the ratio ρ := O(1)B₃²Mα_{0,n}/α_{2,j} times

C_{WT}(L^{j−n})^{4+β_H}exp(−⅓κd_j(X))E₀.

Here C_{WT} collects Bałaban's equal-scale constants of RG I Sect. 4: the factors 2n², c₀(δ₁)c₁(δ₁) with δ₁ = O(M^{−1}), and the like, as in (4.22). The two factors B₃ of ρ are the 2n²B₃/α₂ of (I.4.5) per order and the amplitude B₃ of each factor B. Since α_{0,n}/α_{2,j} ≤ c₂^{−1}(1+β₀)²max(1, n−j)^{1/2}, ρ ≤ ρ̄max(1, n−j)^{1/2} with ρ̄ := O(1)B₃²M(1+β₀)²/c₂.

(I.4.14)–(I.4.15) (RG I p.284 l.13–37) are identities for the Taylor coefficients at B = 0, "for an arbitrary gauge function λ, and arbitrary gauge fields B₁, B₂, B₃" (l.37). They are multilinear, so they hold for complex B with no continuation, and they hold for Δ because they hold for each term.

The far domains are bounded by (3.48). It holds on the class, since the condition (a) places U in the norm class of every X, where Δ has the bound of the *Statement*, and their sum is ≤ O(1)exp(−κL^{n−j}) ≤ O(1)(L^{j−n})⁵ (p.280 l.4–8).

Every factor max(1, n−j)^{1/2} enters at most to the fifth power, and (L^{j−n})⁵ ≤ (L^{j−n})^{4+β_H}. Let C_{β′} := sup_{m≥0}max(1, m)^{5/2}L^{−β′m}, which is finite. Since β_H − β′ ≥ β′, max(1, n−j)^{5/2}(L^{j−n})^{4+β_H} ≤ C_{β′}(L^{j−n})^{4+β′}. Two further pieces enter. The irrelevant part of Δβ A(h_z, U) + N(U), by (3.66) for A(h_z, ·) and for each of the 36 plane-pair actions of N, ≤ O(1)E₀c₂^{−2}ρ²(L^{j−n})^{4+β_H} with the O(1) counting these pieces, has the form of the parts of orders 2 to 4 and is counted with C_{WT} + c₂^{−2} in place of C_{WT}. The localisation remainder outside □̃⁴ is Bałaban's bound C_locE₀(L^{j−n})⁵exp(−κd_j(X)) above. With ρ^r ≤ (1+ρ̄)⁴max(1, n−j)² for r ≤ 4, and with the sum of exp(−⅓κd_j(X)) over the domains X ∋ z bounded by O(1), (5.1c) holds with C_α := O(1)C_{β′}[C_r⁵ + C_loc + (C_{WT} + c₂^{−2})(1+ρ̄)⁴ + 1], the O(1) that sum and the 1 the far domains. Since C_{β′} ≥ 1, this is at least the value O(1)(2 + c₂^{−2}) of *Mixed radii.* at n = j, for the O(1) large enough. C_α depends on L, M, B₃, β_I, β₀, β_H, and c₂ and on Bałaban's equal-scale constants. It does not depend on j, n, k, K, the volume, or the regularity ε_n of real configurations. For a difference every step is linear in the term, and E₀ is replaced by dE₀.

*Derivative version (derived from R-Δ^c).* Let t ↦ U(t), |t| < R, be a holomorphic family of pairs of the class that lie in 𝒮_z, at a point z whose near domain satisfies (N). Two kinds occur. One is the local minimisers U(t) = U_n(□₀, 𝐕(t)) of complex block data 𝐕(t) holomorphic in t, extended outside □₀ as in *The regular subclass.*. The other is the minimisers U_k(𝐕(t)) at holomorphic complex data, which vary outside □₀ and lie in 𝒮_z pointwise in t (*The regular subclass.*); the §8.2 family U_k(exp i[g_kCtA − hD̃(g_kCtA)]V^{(k)}) is of this kind. Then t ↦ irr(Δ)(U(t)) is analytic on |t| < R and bounded there by (5.1c), and Cauchy's estimate on the circles |t| = R′ < R, as R′ → R, gives

the derivative bound |∂_t irr(Δ)(U(t))|_{t=0}| ≤ R^{−1}·d·E₁(α)(L^{j−n})^{4+β′}.

This is Bałaban's mechanism for the derivative in t_□ (p.283 l.23–25, RG I p.280 l.47–49): the configuration is a holomorphic function of t_□ through its data, and the factor O(1)α₂^{−1}ε₁ is the size ε₁ of the direction over the radius α₂. §8.2 uses it along the fluctuation, with R the ratio of the margin m_k to the size of the fluctuation. A version with the relative size measured against the background's own size doesn't work, since irr(Δ) is only of second order at U = 1. Along a family that leaves the local minimisers, (5.1c) is not available.

*The lift condition (derived at real minimisers; open otherwise).* Condition (a) asks, besides run A's membership with the current at most r_{0,j}/C_{Q*}, that the lifted configuration ι_X𝐔 satisfy on X the configuration conditions of run B's U^c_{j+1}(X, r_{0,j}, r_{1,j}). The lifted current Q₁^{pl}(ι_X𝐔)*𝐉 then lies in run B's space (*Complex domain.*). The proof of (5.1c) and its callers need this at three kinds of configurations:
- the minimisers U^A_k(V) at real data V;
- the minimisers at the complex data of the fluctuation integral (§8.2, CRE (3.15));
- for the near domains, the composites U_j(□₀, exp i(τB + B′)) of Lemma 4 at complex τ and B′.

A lift statement for the whole class doesn't work. At one step, VAR's bounds (2), (9), (10), and (19) control the lifted field's derivatives only at the scale ε of the data. That is a factor of order L^j above (I.1.12)–(I.1.14) at the scale j, and the class allows data that are rough at the scale ε.

At the real minimisers the conditions are derived, with the localised lift of §9.8. Let U^A := U^A_k(V), U^B := U^B_{k+1}(V), a := εL^k, and a_j := εL^j, the unit of the scale j.
- §9.7 gives ι(U^A) = e^{i(ε/L)𝐒}U^B, with 𝐒 := ℒA′* + A″ up to the BCH term O((ε/L)|A″||ℒA′*|) of the composition e^{i(ε/L)A″}e^{i(ε/L)ℒA′*}, sup|𝐒| ≤ σ_k/a, and sup|∇𝐒| ≤ σ_k/a². In the units of a_j the two bounds are σ_kL^{j−k} and σ_kL^{2(j−k)}.
- By *The Localised Lift.* (§9.8), ι_XU^A = e^{i(ε/L)𝐒_X}U^B on X, with 𝐒_X − 𝐒 at most C(ε₀)ε₁e^{−δ₀M₁L^j}/a and its gradient at the one-step scale ε. In the units of a_j the tail moves the cube-gauge field by C(ε₀)ε₁e^{−δ₀M₁L^j}L^{j−k} and its gradient by at most C(ε₀, δ₀M₁)ε₁e^{−δ₀M₁L^j/2}L^{j−k}. So in these units 𝐒_X and its gradient are at most σ̂_{k,j}L^{j−k}, with σ̂_{k,j} := σ_k + C(ε₀)ρ_k + C(ε₀, δ₀M₁)ε₁e^{−δ₀M₁L^j/2}.
- U^B is real. By VAR Theorem 1 at k + 1 steps (§9.2, (9.2)) its plaquettes lie within C₀ε₁L^{2(j−k)}ξ² in the units of a_j. By VAR (9) on the cubes of side 2M′a, M′ := O(1)LM/2 ≤ M(ε₁) taken a multiple of R₁M₁ as VAR Theorem 1 asks (VAR p.279 l.3), it has on the cubes of (I.1.12) a gauge with |A|, |∇A| ≤ O(1)LMB₃ε₁L^{j−k} in these units.
- The move by e^{i(ε/L)𝐒_X} changes the plaquettes by O(1)σ̂_{k,j}L^{j−k}ξ² and the cube-gauge field by at most σ̂_{k,j}L^{j−k} with its gradient.
- So ι_X(U^A) satisfies (i), (ii) (with U′ = 1), and the plaquette half of (iii) on X at radii ≤ r_{·,j}/(2B₃), for γ small. Here ε₁ ≤ C_{ε₁}ε_k, σ_k ≤ C(ε₀)ε₁, and ε_kL^{j−k}/α_{0,j} ≤ (ε_k/α_{0,k})(1+β₀)²(k−j)^{1/2}L^{j−k} → 0 uniformly, since q₀ > p₀, a choice of this note (CRE p.259 l.50 asks only "integers greater than 1").
- (iv) follows by RG I (1.17), which uses only the plaquettes of the configuration and of its real part.
- The current. By §10.3 at the scale k, J(U^A) = J′ + δ𝐉 with a³|J′| ≤ 2B_λε₁ and a³|δ𝐉| ≤ x_J(ρ_k + (ε/a)ε₁) ≤ x_J(C_G(ε₀) + 1)ε₁. So in the units of a_j, |J(U^A)| ≤ B′_λε₁L^{3(j−k)} < r_{0,j}/C_{Q*} for γ small, B′_λ := 2B_λ + x_J(C_G(ε₀) + 1), since r_{0,j} ≥ r_{0,k}L^{j−k} and ε₁/r_{0,k} ≤ C_{ε₁}ε_k/r_{0,k} → 0 (q₀ > p₀). The lifted current Q₁^{pl}(ι_XU^A)*J(U^A) is then at most C_{Q*}|J(U^A)| < r_{0,j}. These limits, and C_Π(j)ε₁² ≤ max(1, k − j) and r_{0,k}ε₁ ≤ 1 below, use ε₁ ≤ C_{ε₁}ε_k with the constant C_{ε₁} of CRE (3.12). At the ε₁ = C(M)R_kε_k of (T-Λ) they need q₀ > p₀ + r, since R_k ≤ C(log g_k^{−2})^r. CRE p.259 l.50 asks only that q₀ be an integer greater than 1, and CRE, LFR I, and LFR II print no upper bound on q₀, so this is a choice of q₀, as q₀ > p₀ is.

The current of the lifted configuration, J(ι_XU^A), doesn't work in the current slot: no bound of J(ι_XU^A) − J(U^B) at the scale of the age is available, since PBG Theorem 3.13 gives first differences only and excludes the Laplacian entry for 𝔊 (p.426 l.29–32). This is why G is evaluated at the pair lift (§3).

**Open (complex configurations):** the configuration conditions of the lifted configuration at the complex data of the fluctuation integral and at the composites of Lemma 4. Its current then follows by *Complex domain.*, run A's current bound r_{0,j}/C_{Q*} being Bałaban's through the reading of *The class*. What closes it has two parts. The first is §9.5–9.7 at complex data, with the operator bounds of PBG and VAR at complex configurations (VAR p.309 l.2–4, RG I p.273 l.2–3), giving the same representation ι(U^A_k(𝐕)) = e^{i(ε/L)𝐒(𝐕)}U^B_{k+1}(𝐕). The second is, for the composites, the scale-j version of the last sentence of §9.7: ι(U^A_j(□₀, ·)) against run B's U^B_{j+1}(□₀, ·) at the same data, with Lemma 4 for run B at its local minimiser of that data.

Until these close, (5.1c) for the lifted term G, and so for Δ, is open where its proof evaluates the lift, at the composites of the near domains and at the far domains. For one term on its own run it is Bałaban's (3.67).

*The moment of the lifted term (derived).* For the global lift ι the whole tensor is isotropic, with Π^Δ_{22,11} = Δβ_j = β^B_{j+1} − β^A_j of §3 and N_j = 0; for the localised lift both differ by the boundary term below. By (3.57)–(3.64) and RG I (1.20)–(1.22), the coefficient of a generation-j term 𝐄 along a lift ℓ (a map from the block fields exp iB on T^{(j)} to fine fields with M^{(j)}∘ℓ = id) is β = −∂²_{p_μp_ν}Π̂_{μν}(0), Π the kernel of the quadratic part of B ↦ 𝐄(ℓ(exp iB)) at B = 0 (RG I (1.20), "the vacuum polarization tensor"). The first variation of 𝐄 at the trivial configuration is zero: it is invariant under the constant gauge transformations (RG I p.264 l.9–10), so each of its values is an Ad-invariant vector of 𝔤, which is zero since the gauge group is compact and simple, as for ∂_J𝐄 below. So Π = (dℓ)*H(dℓ) with H the Hessian of 𝐄 in the fine field at 1, and in Fourier variables Π̂(p) = Σ_n d̂ℓ_n(p)*Ĥ(p + 2πn/a_j)d̂ℓ_n(p), the sum over the aliases n of the block lattice. The Ward identity (I.4.15) with the covariance (3.58)–(3.60) gives Ĥ(q) = O(q²) at q → 0 and Ĥ(0) = ∂Ĥ(0) = 0, and a lift that reproduces affine block fields has d̂ℓ_0(0) = 1, d̂ℓ_0(p) = 1 + O(p), and d̂ℓ_n(p) = O(p²) for n ≠ 0; so Π̂(p) = Ĥ(p) + O(p³) and β = −∂²Ĥ(0), the same for every such lift. The Fourier form uses the block-translation covariance of the global lift; the next paragraph identifies the tensor without it. U^B_{j+1} is a linearised Landau-gauge minimiser of the Wilson form under block constraints, with an exponentially decaying kernel, and ι∘U^A_j is the composition of two such lifts; each reproduces affine fields, so the composition does too. An affine fine field A_aff has constant curl and averages to an affine block field. For every φ of compact support Σ_{p′}(Dφ)(p′) = 0 for each orientation, so ⟨DA_aff, Dφ⟩ = 0, and A_aff solves the linearised constrained Euler–Lagrange equations with zero multiplier. The lift ℓ of its block field is defined, since the kernel of ℓ decays exponentially and A_aff grows linearly, and φ := ℓ(MA_aff) − A_aff, put in the lift's gauge, has zero block data and, with the multiplier λ of ℓ(MA_aff), solves the homogeneous gauge-fixed constrained system 𝒜(φ, λ) = 0, the block constraint included; φ and λ grow at most linearly. The inverse 𝒢 of that system, data to the lift (VAR (190)) and sources to the constrained propagator (PBG (3.42)), has an exponentially decaying kernel, and 𝒜 is local, so 𝒢𝒜 = I, which holds on pairs of compact support, extends to pairs of polynomial growth by cutting off and dominated convergence. Hence (φ, λ) = 𝒢𝒜(φ, λ) = 0 in the lift's gauge, the lift of the block field of A_aff is A_aff modulo gauge, and H annihilates the gauge differences by the Ward identity. The argument is local, as β is defined in infinite volume (RG I p.264 l.21–22). Hence Π^{B∘ι}_{22,11} = β^B_{j+1} for the global lift ι.

The affine fields give the whole tensor. The tensor Π_{μν,κλ}(z) of (3.57) is the second-order term at z of the affine field B_μ(x) = Σ_κa_{μκ}(x_κ − z_κ), and by (3.55) it depends on a only through the curl. The lift of an affine block field is A_aff modulo gauge, the first variation of 𝐄^B at 1 is zero, and H annihilates the gauge differences, so B ↦ 𝐄^B(ι(exp iB), z) has run B's own tensor, Π^{B∘ι}_{μν,κλ} = Π^{B,(j+1)}_{μν,κλ}. CRE (3.60)–(3.61) for run B's sum reduce it to β^B_{j+1}δ_{μν}δ_{κλ} for κ < μ, λ < ν. The covariance used is (2.29) for run B's sum at one configuration, as CRE p.260 l.2–4 states it. No covariance of ι, of Q₁^{pl}, or of G enters.

The pair lift does not change the moment. Its current slot carries Q₁^{pl}(ι𝐔)*J(𝐔) in place of the lifted configuration's own current. Both are O(p²)B̂(p) at the block momentum p, on every alias, since the linearised current D*D vanishes on affine fields and Q₁^{pl}(1)* is bounded. The mixed Hessian Ĥ_{AJ}(q) of 𝐄 in the configuration and the current at the trivial pair (1, 0) is O(q). The gauge invariance (iii) at (1, 0), in the direction of a gauge parameter ω and a current j, gives H_{AJ}(Dω, j) + ∂_J𝐄(1, 0)[[ω, j]] = 0. The first variation ∂_J𝐄(1, 0) is invariant under the constant gauge transformations, so it vanishes: the gauge group is compact and simple, so 𝔤 has no nonzero Ad-invariant vectors. So q_μĤ_{AJ,μν}(q) = 0 for every q, and Ĥ_{AJ}(0) = 0 by the continuity that the exponential decay gives. The current slot therefore changes Π̂ by O(p)·O(p²) = O(p³) on the alias n = 0 and by O(p⁴) on the others, and β = −∂²Ĥ(0) also at the pair lift. At an affine field the linearised current vanishes, so the current slot adds nothing to the second-order term there, and the whole tensor is unchanged.

For the localised lift the coefficient differs by a boundary term. On the support of the term Π^G − Π^{B∘ι} = (dι_X)*H dι_X − (dι)*H dι, and the moment tests it on affine block fields. The linearisations of ι and ι_X at 1 agree on constant block fields. On an affine block field their difference is sourced near the boundary of X^∼ by its constant curl, not by the field itself, so the unboundedness of affine fields enters only through the term's weight exp(−κd_j(X)). By the decay of the localised lift's linearisation at 1 (§9.8, *The Localised Lift.*), the difference on X is at most C″_Πe^{−δ₀M₁L^j} times the curl. RG I (4.5) at order 2 bounds the Hessian by (8B₃/α_{2,j})²E₀exp(−κd_j(X)). The argument tests the quadratic form in the curl and never uses the plane, so for all κ < μ, λ < ν, |Π^G_{μν,κλ} − β^B_{j+1}δ_{μν}δ_{κλ}| ≤ C_Π(j)E₀e^{−δ₀M₁L^j}, C_Π(j) := C′_Π(8B₃/α_{2,j})², C′_Π := O(1)C″_Π, the weights of the moment absorbed by exp(−κd_j(X)) and the polarisation by the O(1). So the coefficients of N_j are at most 2C_Π(j)E₀e^{−δ₀M₁L^j}. C_Π(j) is not uniform in j, since α_{2,j} = c₂α_{0,j} carries g_j. It enters only through C_Π(j)ε₁², and α_{0,j} ≥ α_{0,k}(1+β₀)^{−2}max(1, k−j)^{−1/2} with ε₁ ≤ C_{ε₁}ε_k gives C_Π(j)ε₁² ≤ C′_Π(8B₃)²C_{ε₁}²c₂^{−2}(1+β₀)⁴max(1, k−j)(ε_k/α_{0,k})² ≤ max(1, k − j) for γ small. G is gauge invariant (§9.8), so the Ward identities hold for G and for Δ exactly. The frozen difference and the variation group are therefore renormalised with G's own coefficient. Their sum, the two runs' difference at the generation j, is the same for every coefficient. With Π^G_{22,11} and N_j the frozen difference is irr(Δ_j) of §3, with Δ's own coefficient Π^Δ_{22,11} = Π^G_{22,11} − β^A_j. Its marginal part is removed in full, since Δ_j's tensor is Π^G_{μν,κλ} − β^A_jδ_{μν}δ_{κλ}, run A's tensor being isotropic by (3.61); so (5.1) bounds it. The marginal parts of the two runs are their own coefficients, which §3 absorbed into the couplings, and the defect (Π^G_{22,11} − β^B_{j+1})A_ε(h_z, U^A_k) + N_j(U^A_k) enters only the second bound (5.1v) of the group.

*Evidence.* CRE pp.279 l.43–283 l.34. Every step of the proof is linear in the term: the far-domain bound (3.48) is RG I (1.18) with half the decay rate, exp(−½κd_j(X)) (p.280 l.4); the near-domain analysis applies the expansion (I.3.34) with remainder (I.3.54), a Taylor expansion on the analyticity domain whose remainder is a Cauchy estimate proportional to the sup bound (p.280 l.14–22), and whose terms of orders 2 to 4 are bounded through (I.4.17)–(I.4.18) and (I.4.3), proportional to E₀; the Ward–Takahashi identities (I.4.14)–(I.4.15) are consequences of the gauge invariance (iii), which holds for both terms and hence for Δ; (3.49)–(3.56) are identities linear in the term, which use the gauge invariance and not the covariance. RG I removes the first-moment terms of (I.4.34) by the Euclidean symmetries (p.290 l.13–15), but in CRE's version all factors are B, moved to z (p.280 l.22–25), so each first-moment term has a slot at the constant B(z). The Ward identity at the linear gauge parameter λ = cx_κ, c ∈ 𝔤, with the first variation at 1 zero, gives Σ_x𝐄^{(2)}_{κν}(X, x, y, z) = 0 for every κ, ν, and y, so these terms vanish, by the symmetry of (3.50) also where the constant slot is the second; (3.57) holds for each term with its own tensor at each z, whose z-independence uses the translation invariance (p.281 l.59), so for G the tensor is taken at each z; (3.58)–(3.61) use the covariance (2.29) to reduce that tensor to Π_{22,11}, which is Bałaban's for 𝐄^A, while for G the part off Π^G_{22,11}(z) is subtracted as N_j; (3.62)–(3.64), which also use the translation invariance (p.282 l.44), identify the quadratic coefficient Π_{22,11} for 𝐄^A, and for G the coefficient is identified at each z through the lift (*The moment of the lifted term.*); (3.65)–(3.66) concern A(h_z, U_k), and the same analysis gives (3.66) for each plane-pair action A_{PP′}(h_z, U_k), with the elementary tensor ½tr F_PF_{P′}(z) in the normalisation of (3.66), computed at the affine fields, where every fine plaquette of the plane P carries the curl F_P (A_{PP′}(h_z, ·) is gauge invariant and analytic where the plaquettes are small, and (3.66) uses the covariance of A(h_z, ·) only to identify its tensor); (3.67) collects constants proportional to E₀. The derivative version is the mechanism of p.283 l.23–25 ("we differentiate these terms with respect to the parameter t_□ multiplying the fluctuation field"), applied along a holomorphic family of local minimisers in 𝒮_z. The common polydisc 𝒫_j(X; α′) is by definition the set where run A's domain conditions and run B's conditions for the lifted pair both hold, so both terms are analytic on it and bounded there by E₀ (*Complex domain.*). Condition (a) puts every pair of the class in the norm class 𝒫_j(X; r) of every X, and the proof runs there with E₀ the bound on the larger polydisc. At the configurations where the proof evaluates the lift, condition (a) is *The lift condition.*, which is open in part. Every radius of the proof is a fixed multiple of the common radius α_{0,·}: α_{1,·} by the choice of C₁, α_{2,·} = c₂α_{0,·}, α_{3,j} = β_IL^{−2}α_{0,j}/(2B₃), and the background amplitude O(1)B₃Mα_{0,n}. So the ratios ρ, C_r, and α_{0,n}/α_{2,j} are unchanged by the reduction, the smallness conditions O(1)Mα₁ ≤ β_I and (4B₃²O(1)M)²α₀ ≤ β_I are weaker at the reduced radii, and C_α is unchanged.

*What it gives (derived except the inputs of the class bound: the lift condition and (R-Δ^c-T)).* The frozen difference Δ_j of a matched pair, renormalised with Π^Δ_{22,11} = Π^G_{22,11} − β^A_j and N_j (*The moment of the lifted term.*) and carried from its generation j to the stopping scale, is evaluated at run A's minimiser U^A_k, a configuration of 𝒮_z on T_ε. It contributes ≤ d_j E₁(α) L^{−β′(k−j)} N_s⁴ to D_τ, where d_j is the analytic norm of the difference at its generation.

The variation group of (3.1) compares irr^B_{j+1}(U^B_{k+1}) with irr^G(U^A_k) := G(U^A_k) − G(1) − Π^G_{22,11}A_ε(h_z, U^A_k) − N_j(U^A_k), G run B's term at the pair lift on T_ε (§3), whose configuration ι_XU^A_k is not a local minimiser of run B. Each of the two values has the age bound of (5.1c) for one term, with d = 1 and the other term zero:
- irr^B_{j+1}(U^B_{k+1}) on T_{ε/L}, where U^B_{k+1} is in run B's 𝒮_z;
- irr^G(U^A_k) on T_ε, where U^A_k is in 𝒮_z, and (5.1c) for G bounds G(U) − G(1) − Π^G_{22,11}A_ε(h_z, U) − N_j(U). G depends on U on X^∼, where the condition (a) of the class places the lifted pair (*Complex domain.*). At U^A_k that is the lift condition, derived there. The proof of (5.1c) for G also evaluates G at the composites of Lemma 4 at complex τ and B′, through (I.3.34), (I.3.54), and (I.4.3), and at the far domains, where the lift condition is open (*Open (complex configurations)*). It holds at the near domains satisfying (N), and the others are (R-Δ^c-T).

So, given these inputs, the group is ≤ 2E₁(α)(L^{j−k})^{4+β′} per scale-(j+1) point.

*The pair line (derived).* Let a := εL^k.
