By §9.7 and the localised lift of §9.8, ι_XU^A_k = e^{i(ε/L)𝐒_X}U^B_{k+1} on X and within 2L fine spacings of it, with sup|𝐒_X| ≤ σ̂_{k,j}/a, and in the units of a_j 𝐒_X and its gradient are at most σ̂_{k,j}L^{j−k}, σ̂_{k,j} := σ_k + C(ε₀)ρ_k + C(ε₀, δ₀M₁)ε₁e^{−δ₀M₁L^j/2} (*The lift condition.*). The localisation enters the distance, not an additive tail. The terms are functions of pairs (𝐔, 𝐉) with the current 𝐉 an independent variable (RG I p.262, (1.14)), and the condition (iv) involves only the currents of the averages of 𝐔, which are functions of 𝐔 (RG I (1.15)–(1.16)). So the difference is split with the current frozen. Let W(s) := e^{is(ε/L)𝐒_X}U^B_{k+1}, ΔJ := Q₁^{pl}(ι_XU^A_k)*J(U^A_k) − J(U^B_{k+1}), the difference of the two current slots, and F(s, u) := 𝐄^B(W(s), J(U^B_{k+1}) + uΔJ; z) for s, u ∈ ℂ. Then G(U^A_k; z) − 𝐄^B(U^B_{k+1}; z) = [F(1, 0) − F(0, 0)] + [F(1, 1) − F(1, 0)]. The bounds below are per localisation domain X, with E₀ standing for E₀exp(−κd_j(X)); per point they carry the domain sum C_X of §8.2.
- *The configuration part.* In the units of a_j the line s ↦ W(s) moves the plaquettes by at most 4|s|σ̂_{k,j}L^{j−k}ξ², the cube-gauge field and its gradient by at most |s|σ̂_{k,j}L^{j−k}, and the data of (iv) through (1.17). These are measured against r_{0,j}, the smallest room, and r_{0,j} ≥ r_{0,k}(1+β₀)^{−2}(k−j)^{−1/2} ≥ r_{0,k}L^{j−k} (*The constant.*). So for |s| ≤ T := r_{0,k}/(16σ̂_{k,j}) they stay within ¼r_{0,j} of U^B_{k+1}, whose own plaquettes and cube-gauge field are within O(1)LMB₃C₀ε₁L^{j−k} ≤ ¼r_{0,j} for γ small (§9.2, *The lift condition.*). The current stays at J(U^B_{k+1}), and |J(U^B_{k+1})| ≤ O(1)C₀ε₁L^{3(j−k)} ≤ ¼r_{0,j} by (9.2), for γ small. So the disc lies in the domain of 𝐄^B, since 2B₃·½r_{0,j} < α^B_{0,j+1}.
- *Its bound.* For T ≥ 2 Cauchy's estimate on the disc |s| ≤ T gives |F(1, 0) − F(0, 0)| ≤ 2 sup|F|/T ≤ 32(σ̂_{k,j}/r_{0,k})E₀. For T < 2 both endpoints lie in the domain, the endpoint s = 1 by the configuration conditions of *The lift condition.*, and |F(1, 0) − F(0, 0)| ≤ 2E₀ < 64(σ̂_{k,j}/r_{0,k})E₀. So the configuration part is ≤ C_σ(σ̂_{k,j}/r_{0,k})E₀ at every k, C_σ := 64, and no bound on the current enters it.
- *The current part.* At the fixed configuration W(1) = ι_XU^A_k, which satisfies the configuration conditions (*The lift condition.*), the line u ↦ J(U^B_{k+1}) + uΔJ moves only the current. For |u| ≤ T_J := r_{0,j}/(4 sup|ΔJ|) the current stays within ½r_{0,j}. With the endpoint u = 1 in the domain by the lift condition, the same two cases give |F(1, 1) − F(1, 0)| ≤ 16(sup|ΔJ|/r_{0,j})E₀.

*The current along the pair line (derived).* At every age, sup_X|ΔJ| ≤ C_Jσ̂_{k,j}L^{3(j−k)} in the units of a_j, C_J := 2x_J + 1, under (16dc_br + 2c_Dc_v)L^{d−1}B_λε₁ ≤ 1.

The averagings compose, M^{k+1} = M^k∘M, so 𝒬(U^B_{k+1}) = 𝒬(Ũ)Q₁^{pl}(U^B_{k+1}) with Ũ := MU^B_{k+1}. Hence J(U^B_{k+1}) = 𝒬(U^B_{k+1})*λ^B = Q₁^{pl}(U^B_{k+1})*J′, with J′ := 𝒬(Ũ)*λ^B the intermediate current of §10.3 at the scale k (*The Multipliers.*, *The Intermediate Current.*). With δ𝐉 := J(U^A_k) − J′,

ΔJ = Q₁^{pl}(ι_XU^A_k)*δ𝐉 + [Q₁^{pl}(ι_XU^A_k) − Q₁^{pl}(U^B_{k+1})]*J′.

- *The first term.* By §10.3 at the scale k, a³|δ𝐉| ≤ x_J(ρ_k + (ε/a)ε₁) and a³|J′| ≤ 2B_λε₁. On X^∼, Mι_XU^A_k = U^A_k, so *The Adjoint Averaging.* (ii) applies at W := ι_XU^A_k with W̄ = U^A_k, and c_QL^{d+1}κ_W ≤ 1 for γ small by the plaquette bound of *The lift condition.*. Its divergence term is small. D*_{U^A_k}J(U^A_k) = 0 (PBG (3.117)) and D*_ŨJ′ = 0 by D_V*λ^B = 0 (*The Multipliers.*), so D*_{U^A_k}δ𝐉 = −(D*_{U^A_k} − D*_Ũ)J′. U^A_k = e^{iεA′*}Ũ with sup|A′*| ≤ 4ρ_k/a (§9.7), so the transports of the 2d bonds at a point differ in Ad by at most c_brε·4ρ_k/a, and ε sup|D*_{U^A_k}δ𝐉| ≤ 8dc_br(ε/a)ρ_k sup|J′|. So a³ sup_X|Q₁^{pl}(W)*δ𝐉| ≤ 2x_J(ρ_k + (ε/a)ε₁) + 16dc_brL^{d−1}B_λε₁(ε/a)ρ_k.
- *The second term.* On X and within 2L fine spacings of it, ι_XU^A_k = e^{iη𝐒_X}U^B_{k+1}, η := ε/L. The columns of Q₁^{pl}(·)* at the bonds of X use the transports along paths of at most (2d + 1)L bonds there. These change by at most c_vη sup|𝐒_X| per bond, and with them the three operators and Ad(h_{c,x}) of *The Adjoint Averaging.*. As in *(b) The Difference.* of §10.3 at the level i = 0, the second term is at most c_DL^{d−1}c_vε sup|𝐒_X| sup|J′|, and a³ times it is at most 2c_Dc_vL^{d−1}B_λε₁(ε/a)σ̂_{k,j}.

With ρ_k + (ε/a)ε₁ ≤ σ̂_{k,j}, ρ_k ≤ σ̂_{k,j}, and ε/a ≤ 1, a³ sup_X|ΔJ| ≤ [2x_J + (16dc_br + 2c_Dc_v)L^{d−1}B_λε₁]σ̂_{k,j} ≤ C_Jσ̂_{k,j}, and a_j³ = L^{3(j−k)}a³ gives the statement. So the current part is ≤ 16C_J(σ̂_{k,j}/r_{0,k})L^{2(j−k)}E₀ ≤ 16C_J(σ̂_{k,j}/r_{0,k})E₀ at every age, since r_{0,j} ≥ r_{0,k}L^{j−k}. No second differences of the displacement enter: at both minimisers the current is the lift of a multiplier, J = 𝒬*λ, so the current slot moves through the multipliers and, in the configuration, only through the transports of Q₁^{pl}*. The size of the lifted current alone doesn't work: |ΔJ| ≤ Cε₁L^{3(j−k)} leaves the current part at 16C(ε₁/r_{0,k})L^{2(j−k)}E₀, and with it (5.1v) is only polylogarithmically small in K and not summable.

*The action part (derived).* The group's action part is −β^B_{j+1}[A_{ε/L}(h_z, U^B_{k+1}) − A_ε(h_z, U^A_k)], at two real regular configurations, so no lift enters it. It splits through MU^B_{k+1}, and the support of h_z has the volume (2a_j)⁴.
- U^A_k = e^{iεA′*}MU^B_{k+1} with sup|A′*| ≤ 4ρ_k/a and sup|∇A′*| ≤ 4ρ_k/a² (§9.7), and both plaquette fields are at most 2C₀ε₁/a² ((9.2)–(9.3)). So |A_ε(h_z, U^A_k) − A_ε(h_z, MU^B_{k+1})| ≤ C_Aρ_kε₁L^{4(j−k)}.
- The localised blocking artifact A_ε(h_z, MU^B_{k+1}) − A_{ε/L}(h_z, U^B_{k+1}) is (9.3)–(9.4) with the weight h_z. The terms of (9.4) give 16C_b(ε/a)ε₁²L^{4(j−k)}. The weight changes by at most 4ε/a_j between a coarse plaquette and the fine plaquettes of its cell, whatever point of a plaquette h_z is evaluated at, since h_z is Lipschitz in ℓ¹ with the constant 1/a_j and the ε-cube of the coarse plaquette, which contains these fine plaquettes, has ℓ¹-diameter 4ε. The fine action on the support of h_z is at most 192C₀²ε₁²L^{4(j−k)}, so this adds at most 768C₀²(ε/a)ε₁²L^{3(j−k)}.
- β̄ := sup_j|β^B_j| is finite. The hypothesis (W) of ONE_LOOP_PROOFS (l.85–86), which holds on 𝓗_γ by its Remark 2.3, from the zero-coupling (W) of its Theorem 8.1 (Corollary 8.2 for simple G) and (P2) of its Proposition 2.4, gives b − C ≤ β_{m+1} ≤ b′ + C at every step (n = m + 1), so β̄ ≤ b′ + C. RG I p.264 l.32 gives the bound on [0, γ] at each j, not uniformly in j.
- With (ε/a)ε₁ ≤ σ_k and ρ_k ≤ σ̂_{k,j}, the action part is at most C_A′β̄σ̂_{k,j}ε₁ per point, C_A′ := C_A + 16C_b + 768C₀².
- The moment defect (Π^G_{22,11} − β^B_{j+1})A_ε(h_z, U^A_k) + N_j(U^A_k) is at most 2496C_XC₀²E₀C_Π(j)ε₁²e^{−δ₀M₁L^j} per point (*The moment of the lifted term.*). A_ε(h_z, U^A_k) ≤ 192C₀²ε₁²L^{4(j−k)} by the plaquette bound of the first bullet. Each plane-pair action is at most ½(2C₀ε₁/a²)²ε⁴(2a_j/ε)⁴ = 32C₀²ε₁²L^{4(j−k)}, by the same bound, and the 36 coefficients of N_j are at most 2C_Π(j)E₀e^{−δ₀M₁L^j}, which adds 2304C₀².

So the group has the second bound (σ̂_{k,j}/r_{0,k})(C_X(C_σ + 16C_J)E₀ + C_A′β̄) + 2496C_XC₀²E₀C_Π(j)ε₁²e^{−δ₀M₁L^j} per point, since r_{0,k}ε₁ ≤ 1, with no age factor: the configuration and current parts of the pair line, the action part, and the moment defect. The smaller of two bounds is at most their geometric mean with the weights 1 − θ_V and θ_V, θ_V := β′/(2(4+β′)), and (x + y)^θ ≤ x^θ + y^θ. Since (4+β′)(1−θ_V) = 4 + β′/2, per scale-(j+1) point

(5.1v) |irr^B_{j+1}(U^B_{k+1}) − irr^G(U^A_k)| ≤ E_V[(σ̂_{k,j}/r_{0,k})^{θ_V} + (C_Π(j)ε₁²e^{−δ₀M₁L^j})^{θ_V}](L^{j−k})^{4+β′/2}, E_V := (2E₁(α))^{1−θ_V}(C_X(C_σ + 16C_J + 2496C₀²)E₀ + C_A′β̄)^{θ_V}.

It is derived given the inputs of the class bound listed in *What it gives.*. Counting the L^{4(k−j)} scale-(j+1) points per stopping-scale point, the group contributes ≤ E_V[(σ̂_{k,j}/r_{0,k})^{θ_V} + (C_Π(j)ε₁²e^{−δ₀M₁L^j})^{θ_V}]L^{−(β′/2)(k−j)}N_s⁴ per generation. Since r_{0,k} ≥ ε_k, C_Π(j)ε₁² ≤ max(1, k − j) (*The moment of the lifted term.*), and e^{−δ₀M₁L^j} ≤ e^{−δ₀M₁L^j/2}, the sum over j is ≤ E_VN_s⁴[((σ_k + C(ε₀)ρ_k)/ε_k)^{θ_V}/(1 − L^{−β′/2}) + ((C(ε₀, δ₀M₁)ε₁/ε_k)^{θ_V} + 1)Σ_{j≤k}max(1, k−j)^{θ_V}e^{−θ_Vδ₀M₁L^j/2}L^{−(β′/2)(k−j)}]. With C′_{β′} := sup_{m≥0}max(1, m)L^{−β′m/4}, the last sum is ≤ C′_{β′}(L^{−β′k/8} + e^{−θ_Vδ₀M₁L^{k/2}/2})/(1 − L^{−β′/4}), split at j = k/2. With σ_k + C(ε₀)ρ_k ≤ C(ε₀)(1 + C_G(ε₀))L^{−(1−2ε₀)k}ε₁ and ε₁ ≤ C_{ε₁}ε_k every part is summable in K. A bound linear in σ_k, by the derivative version along a family from U^B_{k+1} to ι_XU^A_k, doesn't work: the family leaves the local minimisers of run B, where (5.1c) is not available.

*The 𝐑-terms' variation group (derived except the lift condition).* The group Σ_j[𝐑^{B,(j+1)}(U^B_{k+1}) − 𝐑^{B,(j+1)}(pair lift of U^A_k)] of (3.1) has no age factor. At a regular configuration each term is O(1)(L^jL^{−k})⁴g_j^{κ₀}exp(−κd_j(X)) (CRE p.283 l.34–38), which offsets the L^{4(k−j)} points and no more, so the geometric mean above leaves the factor L^{4θ_V(k−j)} and doesn't work. The group is split at the age m_K := ⌊√K⌋ of §4.2.
- At the ages k − j < m_K, the 𝐑-terms have the properties (i)–(iii) of the 𝐄-terms and the bound (2.31), g_j^{κ₀}exp(−κd_j(X)), on the class (CRE p.260 l.18–25). So the frozen-current split of the pair line above gives ≤ (C_σ + 16C_J)(σ̂_{k,j}/r_{0,k})(g^B_{j+1})^{κ₀}exp(−κd_{j+1}(X)) per localisation domain, given the lift condition, as for the 𝐄-terms; the 𝐑-terms carry no coupling coefficient, so no moment enters. At these ages j > k − m_K, so e^{−δ₀M₁L^j/2} ≤ L^{−(1−2ε₀)k} and σ̂_{k,j} ≤ C_σ̂L^{−(1−2ε₀)k}ε₁, C_σ̂ := C(ε₀)(1 + C_G(ε₀)) + C(ε₀, δ₀M₁), for K ≥ K₀(L, δ₀M₁, j(C)), finitely many K excepted. With the domain count C_X of §8.2 and the L^{4(k−j)} points, these ages contribute ≤ C_X(C_σ + 16C_J)(sup_{k−j<m_K}σ̂_{k,j}/ε_k)L^{4m_K}N_s⁴/(1 − L^{−4}). With ε₁ ≤ C_{ε₁}ε_k and k = K − j(C), this is ≤ C_σ̂C_{ε₁}C_X(C_σ + 16C_J)L^{(1−2ε₀)j(C)}L^{4√K−(1−2ε₀)K}N_s⁴/(1 − L^{−4}), summable in K.
- At the ages k − j ≥ m_K the group is not split. With the frozen 𝐑-differences of these ages it is the same-generation difference 𝐑^{B,(j+1)}(U^B_{k+1}) − 𝐑^{A,(j)}(U^A_k), each run at its own minimiser, and §4.2 bounds the sum of these by CN_s⁴(1 + m_K)^{1−κ₀/2}, summable in K for κ₀ ≥ 7.

So the 𝐑-terms of (3.1) are bounded by the extra generation (§4.1), the frozen 𝐑-differences of the ages < m_K, which S_k bounds, the young part of the group above, and §4.2's tail. The fluctuation-dependent version is in §10.5.

So the carried differences contract geometrically in age with no information beyond their size at birth, and the stability statement S of the continuum note's (9.4)(e) reduces to the size at birth, d_{k+1}, of the generation created by one step from inputs differing by d_1, …, d_k: the generated-difference inequality, treated in §8, where it becomes (8.3) with the small constant ε_k^* and the contraction θ := L^{−β′} + ε^*.

## 6. The Large-Field Terms: The Unmatched Set and the Matched Pairs (Derived except for the Young Pairs, Which Use the Open Input (LF))

### 6.1 The Unmatched Set

A term of (U) has a component Z alive at the stopping scale with first large field at run B's step 1, j(Z) = 1. The large-field regions grow and merge until renormalised ((1.84); LFR I (0.3)), so Z's history meets the large-field region at every scale n = 1, …, k + 1 of run B, and its history volume P(Z) of §11(c), the number of points of its history counted at their own scales, is ≥ k + 1. The sum (11.3) for run B admits the weight ω(Z) := exp(C_PP(Z)) ≥ e^{C_P(k+1)} on that component and ω = 1 on the others, so

(6.1) Σ_{(U)}Z^B_ℓ ≤ Σ_{(U)}M^B_ℓ ≤ e^{−C_P(k+1)}δ_LFZ^B_{ℓ₀} ≤ ½a^{(U)}_Kδ_LFZ^B_{ℓ₀}, a^{(U)}_K := 2e^{−C_P(K−j(C))},

measured against run B's own all-small-field term. Since |𝒲^B| ≤ B_W pointwise in V (the continuum note's (8.1) is assembled from pointwise bounds of the loop's terms, its (6.3), (5.6), and the B_LF of (4.5)) and Z^B ≥ Z^B_{ℓ₀}, removing the (U) terms from F_{K+1} = N^B/Z^B changes it by at most 2B_WΣ_{(U)}Z^B_ℓ/Z^B ≤ δ_LFa^{(U)}_KB_W, summable in K; no comparison of Z^B_{ℓ₀} with Z^A_{ℓ₀} is used. δ_LF is the bound (11.3) of §11, which replaces (Q). The entropy of the alive components is inside (11.3).

### 6.2 The Matched Pairs with Large-Field Regions

A matched pair τ⁺, τ with large-field components has the same sequence of domains at the shared scales, and its two terms are the same operations applied to run-dependent ingredients. By CRE (2.20)–(2.21) the large-field operation 𝐓 is an ordered product of one-step operations, each an integral over the block variables V_i on Ω^c_{i+1} with the constraint δ(V̄_iV_{i+1}^{−1}) and the large-field functions ζ, and over the fluctuation variables A_i on Z_{i+1} ∩ Ω_{i+1} with the functions χ and the Gaussian form of Δ^{(i)}; the integration variables and the constraint are the same in both runs, and the fine lattice enters through the step covariance Δ^{(i)} (§10: relative difference r_i, and the same for the Gaussian normalisations z^{(i)}), through the characteristic functions built on the local minimisers U_{i,□}(V_i) (LFR I (1.3)–(1.4), CRE (2.17)), which differ on threshold shells; through the functions (1.5)–(1.7) of LFR I p.178, which depend on the run's coupling: (1.6), (1/z)exp[−g_i^{−2}[1 − Re tr V_i(y,x)]], differs relatively by at most e^{4|δ_i|} − 1 per variable, since 0 ≤ 1 − Re tr V ≤ 2 for the normalised trace and the same bound holds for z, while (1.5) and (1.7), with the thresholds ε_i and 2ϑ_i (ϑ_i := (A₁/A₀)ε_i is Bałaban's δ_i, renamed here to keep δ_i for the coupling difference), differ on threshold shells; through the large-field operations' own integrands, the fine-lattice action A(1/(g″_i)²ζ₁, U″(exp ig_iB, V″)) and the form ⟨DH″B, ζDH″B⟩ of LFR II (1.70); and through the effective action on the small-field regions, whose exponent difference is (3.1) layer by layer: on Ω_n ∖ Ω_{n+1} the frozen terms have age n − j and their differences are bounded per scale-n point by D_n + S_n, with the coupling term Cε_n²|δ_n|, and the depth terms e_n; the boundary terms 𝐁 generated at the steps at which a large-field region existed are outputs of those steps' large-field operations, and their two-run differences belong to (γ). Let j₀ be the birth scale of the pair's oldest alive component, so that Ω_i = Λ_i = T for i < j₀ and the ingredients at the scales i < j₀ are those of the all-small-field pair. Two cases, with m := ⌊k/2⌋.

If k − j₀ ≥ m, the oldest component X meets the large-field region at every scale from j₀ to k, so its history volume is P(X) ≥ m + 1, as in §6.1. The sum (11.3) with the weight ω(X) := exp(C_PP(X)) on it bounds each run's terms of this kind by e^{−C_P(m+1)}δ_LF times that run's own Z_{ℓ₀}, and removing them from each run's F changes it by at most 2B_We^{−C_P(m+1)}δ_LF, as in §6.1; for the two runs together this is ≤ 3e^{−C_Pm}δ_LFB_W, since 4e^{−C_P} < 3.

If k − j₀ < m, every component of the pair was born at a scale > k − m ≥ k/2, so the ingredients at the scales i < j₀ are the all-small-field pair's, and at the scales i ≥ j₀ the two terms differ in three ways.

- (α) On the common support of the two runs' characteristic functions the small-field ingredients differ as follows.
  - The frozen exponents, the coupling term, and the depth terms differ per point by D_i + S_i + Cε_i²|δ_i| + e_i ≤ C_aℓ_K[(i+1)θ̃^i + w_K], by (8.6).
  - The function (1.6) differs relatively by e^{4|δ_i|} − 1 ≤ 8|δ_i| per variable.
  - The covariance differs by r_i ≤ C(ε₀, γ_H)[L^{−γ_H(i−1)} + L^{−(1−ε₀)i}], by (10.6), so the Gaussian form changes by ≤ Cr_i|A_i(b)|² per variable, and the normalisations z^{(i)} relatively by r_i per variable (§10). On the fluctuation cutoff this is ≤ Cr_ip₀(g_i)² per variable, with p₀(g_i)² ≤ ℓ_K (§8.7). Where a large-field function χ^c leaves A_i unbounded, the factor e^{Cr_i|A_i|²} is absorbed by the form (C^{(i)})^{−1} ≥ C_G^{−1} (C_G the bound of the covariance used in §8.2(b)). This changes Bałaban's positivity bound of the A_i-integral, the gain of χ^c included, by at most the factor exp(2CC_Gr_i(1 + p₀(g_i)²)) per variable.
  - The history-localised constants differ per point in two parts. The vacuum-energy counterterms are subtractions of one-step small-field vacuum energies (CRE p.262 l.16–20), so they differ by the class norm d_i of their step, ≤ C_aℓ_K[(i+1)θ̃^i + w_K] by (8.6). The normalisation constants (−½d(𝐠)log g_i^{−2} + log σ₀) per variable (LFR II (1.70), p.380 l.24–28) differ by ½d(𝐠)|log(1 + g_i²δ_i)| ≤ d(𝐠)g_i²|δ_i| ≤ |δ_i|.

  Each of these enters per point of the component's history at its own scale: the layers Γ_i, the block variables on Ω^c_{i+1}, and the fluctuation variables on Z_{i+1} ∩ Ω_{i+1}, all inside Z_{i+1}. So on the common support the two integrands, measured by Bałaban's bounds, differ relatively by at most exp(Σ_Xφ′_X) − 1, φ′_X := C_Ms′_KP(X), with C_M the number of these ingredients per point (a constant of d, L, and G) and s′_K := 2C_aℓ_K[(k+1)θ̃^{k−m} + w_K] + C(ε₀, γ_H)ℓ_K[L^{−γ_H(k−m−1)} + L^{−(1−ε₀)(k−m)}] + 10sup_{i>k−m}|δ_i| ≤ 1 for k large.
- The threshold shells of the characteristic functions, where one run's function is 1 and the other's 0. The shells of the radial tests (1.5), (1.9), and χ^{(j−1)c}(R_j) are derived (`WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §2(b)); the shells of the plaquette and reference-configuration tests are (β) of the Open line.
- The large-field integrands, the boundary terms 𝐁 of the steps with large-field regions, and the large-field dressings of the dressed loop (the continuum note's (9.3)): (γ) of the Open line; the dressings are derived in `WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §5 except at the R-operations with the loop.

(β) and (γ) are the input (LF), derived for (β) (`WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §3(d)) and open for (γ). In the form (LF-1) it asks that, for a history born at a scale > k/8, their run-dependence change the term by at most (exp(Σ_XΣ_iC_Ms^{LF}_{n(X)}L^d|Z_i ∩ X|_i) − 1) times its majorant M_τ of (11.1), with s^{LF}_n ≤ C_hθ̃^n. Since Σ_i|Z_i ∩ X|_i ≤ P(X) by the definition of the history volume (§11(c)) and every component of a young pair is born at a scale n(X) > k − m ≥ k/2, this exponent is ≤ C_Ms^{LF}_KL^dP(X) with s^{LF}_K := C_hθ̃^{k/2}. Given (LF), let s_K := s′_K + L^ds^{LF}_K and φ_X := C_Ms_KP(X). The three-piece split of `WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §2(b) puts each shell term on one run's own support. The triangle inequality, (α) on the common support (where the difference is ≤ (e^{Σφ′_X} − 1) times Bałaban's bound of ρ^A, the densities being positive on the real integration region, LFR I p.176 l.20), the radial shells and the coupling part of the plaquette shells (each ≤ Cp₀(g_i)^{d_G}g_i²|δ_i| ≤ |δ_i| or (4/κ_s)Cg_i²|δ_i| ≤ |δ_i| per variable or test relative to Bałaban's bound, inside the term 10sup_{i>k−m}|δ_i| of s′_K), and (LF) on the rest give, integrating over V,

(6.2) ∫|ρ^B[τ⁺] − ρ^A[τ]| dV ≤ s_KΠ_{X⊂Z(τ)}ω(X)·M^A_τ + (the difference of the small-field exponents on Λ_k, bounded as for the all-small-field pair), ω(X) := (1 + C_MP(X))e^{C_Ms_KP(X)} ≤ e^{C_PP(X)},

since Z^A_τ ≤ M^A_τ, (e^a − 1) + (e^b − 1) ≤ e^{a+b} − 1 for a, b ≥ 0, e^{Σφ_X} − 1 ≤ (Σ_Xφ_X)e^{Σφ_X}, Σ_XC_MP(X) ≤ Π_X(1 + C_MP(X)), and C_P ≥ 2C_M. Hence, by (11.3) for run A with these weights, for the young pairs,

(6.3) Σ_{(M), young}∫|ρ^B[τ⁺] − ρ^A[τ]| dV ≤ δ_LFs_K Z^A_{ℓ₀} + (the small-field differences).

Since Z^A ≥ Z^A_{ℓ₀}, the young pairs change F by ≤ B_Wδ_LF(2s_K + s^{W,tot}_K) through the numerators and the normalisation together, with s^{W,tot}_K of `WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §5(f). The old pairs are not compared: they change each run's F by the amount computed above, ≤ 3e^{−C_P⌊k/2⌋}δ_LFB_W for the two runs. So the matched pairs with large-field regions change F_{K+1} − F_K by at most δ_LFb^{(M)}_KB_W, b^{(M)}_K := 3e^{−C_P⌊k/2⌋} + 2s_K + s^{W,tot}_K,

summable in K: the first term is geometric; in s_K the terms w_K and, through |δ_i| ≤ C[kθ̃^{k/2} + Kw_K] for i > k/2 by (8.6), Kw_K are summable, since their parts μ_K = K^{1−κ₀/2}ℓ_K and Kμ_K are summable for κ₀ > 6 (κ₀ ≥ 7), as before, and the rest, at most K(K + 1)(θ̃^{k_s/8} + 2^{−k_s/16}) with k_s = K − j(C), decays exponentially in K at fixed j(C); the others are geometric in k, s^{LF}_K = C_hθ̃^{k/2} is geometric, given (LF), and s^{W,tot}_K is summable by that note's §5(f). The callers integrate against the dressed loop: ∫|𝒲^Bρ^B − 𝒲^Aρ^A| ≤ B_W∫|ρ^B − ρ^A| + ∫|𝒲^B − 𝒲^A|ρ^A, and the second term is bounded in the same way, the small-field dev insertions by the recursion of §8.3 (inside s′_K) and the large-field dressings by `WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §5, (5.1)–(5.2), so (6.3) holds for the young pairs' numerators with B_W(s_K + s^{W,tot}_K) in place of s_K, which is the numerator part of the change B_Wδ_LF(2s_K + s^{W,tot}_K) above.

## 7. What Remains, Collected

The Cauchy estimate |F_{K+1} − F_K| ≤ a_K follows from (3.1)–(3.2), §4, §5, §6, §8, §9, §10, and §11, and from the open inputs of the Open lines; from nothing else. (LF) enters only through the young matched pairs of §6.2 and through h_n in the 𝒟_{[j]} of §8.4 and §8.6, in both through its open ingredient (γ). (R-Δ^c-T) and the lift condition enter through (5.1c) and (5.1v) of §5, and the 𝐑 version of (5.1c) through §10.5. The groups of (3.1): the carried frozen differences of the all-small-field matched pairs are D_k + S_k per stopping-scale point, the coupling term is ≤ Cε_k²|δ_k|, the variation group is ≤ the sum over j of (5.1v), summable in K ((5.1v) of §5, §10.5), its 𝐑-terms' group is split at the age ⌊√K⌋ (§5, *The 𝐑-terms' variation group*, with §4.2's tail), and the classical group (b) is (9.10), with (3.2) reducing it to one-step statements about regular fields; by (8.6) of §8.7, D_k + S_k + Cε_k²|δ_k| ≤ C_aℓ_K[(K+1)θ̃^{K−j(C)} + (j(C)+2)w_K], w_K = (K + 1)(θ̃^{k_s/8} + 2^{−k_s/16}) + μ_K, μ_K = K^{1−κ₀/2}ℓ_K, ℓ_K = (log(bK + ḡ^{−2}))^{rd+2p₀}, summable in K, given the birth-scale form s^{LF}_n ≤ C_hθ̃^n of the open input (LF). The supports of the all-small-field pair differ by the shells of the stopping-scale test, which change F_{K+1} − F_K by ≤ b^{(S)}_KB_W (§3), by (R-s), summable given (LF) through (8.6). The matched pairs with large-field regions change F_{K+1} − F_K by ≤ δ_LFb^{(M)}_KB_W (§6.2: the old pairs each run against its own Z_{ℓ₀}, the young pairs by (6.3)), summable, the young pairs given (LF). (8.3) also covers the dev insertions of the dressed loops (the project's class D(k) at equal leading words) and the per-step coefficients β_{k+1}, hence δ_k through |Δβ_{k+1}| ≤ C_βd_{k+1} (§8.3); the matched large-field pairs are §6.2; the unmatched set is §6.1 with the δ_LF of §11. Apart from the open inputs, none of these is a new item: together they are S of the continuum note's (9.4)(e), with the carried part derived in §5, the generated part in §8, and the depth source split into its classical, configuration, and Gaussian parts in §§9–10.

## 8. The Generated Difference of One Fluctuation Integral

### 8.1 The Interpolation Identity (Derived)

At the shared scale k the two runs apply the same step CRE (3.1)–(3.15) to inputs on the same lattice T_{εL^{k−1}} with the same block data, and the fluctuation field A_k of (3.11) is the same variable in both runs; the characteristic functions χ^{(k)} of (3.16) cut the fluctuation at Bałaban's δ_k = (A₁/A₀)ε_k, written ϑ_k in this note since δ_k is the coupling difference of §1, a function of the run's coupling at the shared scale, so they differ on a threshold shell, treated in §8.2(b). The background U_{k+1}(V) of (3.10) is the fine-lattice minimiser, on T_ε in run A and on T_{ε/L} in run B (§9.7 bounds their distance), and the Gaussian form −½⟨A_k, C*Δ^{(k)}CA_k⟩ is RG I (1.5) built from the fine-lattice propagators in that background, so it differs between the runs; §10 bounds the difference δΔ^{(k)} := Δ^{(k+1)}_B − Δ^{(k)}_A by r_k times the form's own bound ((10.6)). The runs differ further in the interaction 𝐏^{(k)} of (3.15): the curly bracket collects, for every frozen term, its fluctuation part 𝐄^{(j)}(U_k(exp i[g_kCA_k − hD̃(g_kCA_k)]V^{(k)})) − 𝐄^{(j)}(U_{k+1}), and the same for 𝐑 and 𝐁, and in the fluctuation parts of the fine actions g_k^{−2}A_ε(U^A_k(…)) and g_k^{−2}A_{ε/L}(U^B_{k+1}(…)), whose difference at the fluctuation-dependent configuration is the classical depth of §9 at that configuration (the same (9.1)–(9.10) with V replaced by the fluctuated block field, which is regular by (3.14)). Put 𝐏_t := 𝐏^A + t(𝐏^B − 𝐏^A) and Δ_t := Δ^{(k)}_A + tδΔ^{(k)}, t ∈ [0, 1], Z_t := ∫dA_k χ^{A,(k)} exp[−½⟨A, C*Δ_tCA⟩ + 𝐏_t], with run A's cutoff in both; run B's own integral is Z_1 plus the shell term of §8.2(b). Then, exactly,

(8.1) log Z_1 − log Z_0 = ∫₀¹ [⟨𝐏^B − 𝐏^A⟩_t − ½⟨⟨A, C*δΔ^{(k)}CA⟩⟩_t] dt,

the left side being the whole logarithm of the step's output (𝐄_0^{(k+1)} of (3.37) together with the R″, B, and constant parts of (3.26), (3.38)); the second term is the expectation of a quadratic insertion whose kernel is bounded by (10.6), of the class the marked expansion of §8.5 treats, and

⟨·⟩_t the expectation of the fluctuation measure with interaction 𝐏_t, which for every t is a measure of the kind Bałaban integrates, since 𝐏_t is a convex combination of two admissible interactions and the inductive bounds (RG I (1.18), CRE (2.31), (2.42)) define convex sets. The localised terms of (3.47) for the difference are the localised terms of the right side of (8.1): the marked expansion with one marked activity, the piece of 𝐏^B − 𝐏^A on Y. The marked-cluster bound (G′) of §8.5 is the statement that this marked expansion is bounded by the marked activity times C_KP, with Bałaban's decay in d_{k+1}(X), and by the marked activity's Gaussian mean where it is connected to no activity of 𝐏_t.

### 8.2 The Interaction Difference (Derived)

Δ𝐏 := 𝐏^B − 𝐏^A has four parts, and the step's output differs further by the threshold shell. (a) *The frozen differences' fluctuation parts.* For each shared generation j ≤ k, [irr(Δ_j)(U_k(…A…)) − irr(Δ_j)(U_{k+1})] + Δβ_j[A(h_z, U_k(…A…)) − A(h_z, U_{k+1})], and the same for the frozen R-differences ΔR_j of §8.4 (𝐑_k′ of (3.26) is included into 𝐄_k, and 𝐑_k″ enters by the s-interpolation of (3.26), p.271 l.3–30, whose measure is of the kind §8.5 expands). The frozen boundary-term differences enter the same way, as marked activities of the t-interpolation of (3.26) and of its measure (3.27), and their output goes into 𝐁^{(k+1)} only (p.271 l.29–31, p.279 l.16); they are carried by `WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §4(e) and enter (8.3) only through §8.6. Let k_s := K − j(C) denote the stopping scale in the thresholds of §§8.2–8.7, where k is the generic step. At the steps > k_s/8 the norms d_j, D, S, and those of the boundary terms are taken over the terms that are not deep in the sense of that section: a non-deep output has non-deep inputs only, and the deep terms are bounded in §8.6 and §6.2 (that section's "Deep terms"). (b) *The coupling and the cutoff.* The coupling enters 𝐏 through g_kCA_k in the backgrounds and through the coefficients g_k^{n−2} of the action's terms of order n ≥ 3 in the fluctuation (the quadratic part is the g-free Gaussian form of (3.15)); the two runs' values g^A_k and g^B_{k+1} differ relatively by τ_k := g^B_{k+1}/g^A_k − 1, |τ_k| ≤ g_k²|δ_k|, and the marginal parts Σ_jΔβ_jA(h_z, ·) of (a) are absorbed in δ_k by (2.24) and §3. The cutoff (3.16) is sup_b|g_kCA_k(b)| < ϑ_k, i.e. the rescaled Gaussian variable A_k of (3.15) (the scaling A_k′ = g_kA_k of p.267 l.49, A_k of covariance O(1) by RG I (1.4)–(1.5) and PBG (3.132)) is cut at r_k := (A₁/A₀)p₀(g_k); this is the reading of (3.16) that p.268 l.28–30 uses, where the cutoff implies the Q-test (3.3) at 2ϑ_k because V^{(k)}(V^{(k)}_{□′})^{−1} − 1 is "much smaller than δ_k" (Bałaban's δ_k = ϑ_k) ((3.17)–(3.18): ≤ 44d²B₃²(1+β₀)e^{−R_k}ε_k). The two runs' radii r^A_k, r^B_k differ relatively by ≤ C|δ_k|g_k² (p₀ is smooth in g), so χ^{B,(k)} − χ^{A,(k)} is supported on the shell between the two radii in the sup over a cube's bonds, a set of Gaussian measure ≤ #bonds(□)·C|δ_k|g_k²r_ke^{−r_k²/(2C_G)} ≤ C|δ_k|g_k²e^{−c(A₁/A₀)²p₀(g_k)²}, and the shell's contribution to the step's output per scale-(k+1) point is at most this measure times e^{O(1)} (the activities on the cube are bounded by Bałaban's bounds on (3.43)): a term ≤ C|δ_k|g_k²e^{−cp₀(g_k)²}, absorbed in the coupling term of (8.3). The device is V4's lem:c5-5R-2(b), the marked difference of two cutoffs paid by the large-field reserve. (c) *The R-differences' fluctuation parts,* in (a) and §8.4. (d) *The extra generation,* run B's generation-1 terms, which run A lacks: an input difference d_1 ≤ E₀ + R₁g_1^{κ₀} ≤ 2E₀ per scale-1 point at the shared scale 1, carried by (8.3) like every other.

Three facts bound (a); they are V4's three mechanisms (α)–(γ) of sec:c5-5R and its Lemmas lem:c5-5R-1(b) and lem:c5-5R-2(a), (c), restated for Bałaban's step. The norm throughout is the class norm of R-Δ: d_j is the smallest number with |Δ_j(X)| ≤ d_jE₀exp(−κd_j(X)) on the norm class 𝒫_j(X; r) of §5 (*Complex domain.*), r_{i,j} = α_{i,j}/(8B₃) with α_{i,j} the radii of the inductive complex class of CRE (2.28) (p.259 l.40–50). C₀ and C₁ are at least 16B₃² times Bałaban's lower bounds, so that his statements and inclusions hold at the reduced thresholds (`WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §4(d), *The room at complex points.*, *Bałaban's Statements at the Reduced Thresholds*). R-Δ^c is run at the radii r (§5), and the radii α₂ and α₃ and the margin m_k below are those of that run. The real backgrounds of the support lie in the class 𝒞_k of §5, its condition (a) being the lift condition, open in its current. This is the hypothesis of R-Δ (§5) and the quantity the step's output is measured in: the output difference is analytic on the class of the next scale with the better decay (1+4β)κ (p.262 l.26–30) and satisfies the inductive assumption (p.279 l.22–23), so d_{k+1} is defined with the same E₀. First, the age factor: reading R-Δ^c (§5) applies at the fluctuation-dependent configuration, which is a U_k-type configuration of 𝒮_z (CRE p.279 l.43–44, "The field U_k may depend on the fluctuation field, as in the exponential in (3.15)"; p.280 l.15–17), so with F_j(A) := irr(Δ_j)(U_k(exp i[g_kCA − hD̃(g_kCA)]V^{(k)})), the class bound is |F_j(A)| ≤ b^c_j := d_jE₁(α)L^{−(4+β′)(k−j)}exp(−κd_j(X)) per scale-j term on Bałaban's analyticity domain: Δ_j/d_j satisfies the hypothesis (I.1.18) with E₀, so reading R-Δ^c (§5) bounds its value at the scale k on the complex class by E₁(α)L^{−(4+β′)(k−j)}exp(−κd_j(X)), with E₁(α) := C_αE₀ and C_α of §5 (*The constant.*), uniform in j and k (Theorem 2's real constant E₁ carries the smaller factor (ε/α₃)^{5−β} instead). The analyticity domain is RG I Lemma 4 (p.280, (3.53)), here with α₃/2, α₃ at the radii r: for a background in the space U′^c_{k+1}(□₀, (1+2β)α₀, (1+2β)α₁) of (3.40), a fluctuation 𝐀 with the bounds (3.31) (p.276), and an additional field B′ with |B′| < α₃/2, the composed configuration lies in U^c_j(X, α₀, α₁) and the composed function is analytic; CRE's fluctuation domain (3.43) (p.276 l.26–31) has the background in Ũ^c_{k+1}(Y, (1+β)α̃₀, (1+β)α̃₁), inside Lemma 4's space, and the fluctuation g_kCA_k of (3.15) is in the role of B′, so F_j is analytic in A on the ball sup_b|g_kCA(b)| < α_{3,k}/2. The margin m_k := α_{3,k}/2 is half of α_{3,k}, which is a fixed fraction of min(α_{0,k}, α_{1,k}) (RG I p.280, the restrictions B₃α₃ < ½α₁ before (3.51) and 2B₃α₃ ≤ βL^{−2}α₀ after it, which are homogeneous), so α_{3,k} ≥ c_mg_k(log g_k^{−2})^{min(q₀,q₁)} by (2.28). With ϑ_k = (A₁/A₀)ε_k and ε_k = g_kA₀(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀} (CRE (2.4)), α_{3,k}/ϑ_k ≥ (c_m/A₁)(log g_k^{−2})^{min(q₀,q₁)−p₀} ≥ 4 for γ small, since q₀, q₁ > p₀, a choice that (2.28) allows ("integers greater than 1", p.259 l.48–50). So the cutoff ball sup_b|g_kCA_k(b)| < ϑ_k of (3.16) lies inside the ball of radius m_k with the ratio T₀ := m_k/ϑ_k ≥ 2: this is Bałaban's inclusion of the fluctuation cutoff in his analyticity domain, which his expansion on (3.43) and his Cauchy estimate (3.54) (p.280) use, and in CRE's parameters it is a threshold of Bałaban's kind on A₁/A₀ (p.266 l.42, "for A₁/A₀ sufficiently small") and on the exponents q₀, q₁ against p₀ (of the kind LFR II p.383 l.28). Second, the split and the evenness (lem:c5-5R-1(b), lem:c5-5R-2(a)): F_j(A) − F_j(0) = L_j(A) + R_{2,j}(A) with L_j(A) := DF_j(0)A linear (the term hD̃ is of second order and belongs to R_{2,j}) and R_{2,j} the two-term Taylor remainder. The Gaussian measure of (3.15) with the symmetric cutoff (3.16), after the removal of the gauge-fixing δ-functions by C (p.267 l.47–49), is even in A_k, and L_j is odd, so in the expansion of §8.5 the cluster consisting of L_j alone vanishes, and L_j enters only through clusters in which it is connected to at least one activity of 𝐏_t (the interaction is not even: the cubic vertices of the action and the odd parts of 𝐄_k(U_k(…)) − 𝐄_k(U_{k+1}) are in it, (3.28)). Each such cluster carries the activity's small factor a_k, the bound of 𝐏_t's activities on (3.43): the action's vertices of order n ≥ 3 are ≤ g_kO(p₁(g_k)³) there, and the frozen terms' fluctuation parts are bounded on (3.43) by the terms' own class bounds, which are the outputs of the earlier steps, ≤ g_{j−1}O(p₀³(g_{j−1})) in the form of (2.42) for the generation j (p.278 l.32–35: the terms of (3.47) "satisfy the bounds (2.42), with the constant B₀ replaced by O(p₀³(g_k))", and "the multiplication by g_k yields small bounds"), times L^{−β′(k−j)} per scale-k point by Theorem 2; so a_k ≤ g_kP_a(log g_k^{−2}) for a polynomial P_a. This is V4's mechanism (β) and lem:c5-5R-2(c), the Cauchy parameter on the activities: with every activity multiplied by ζ, the terms with at least one activity vanish at ζ = 0, and the expansion converges for |ζ| up to the ratio of the Kotecký–Preiss bound ā it tolerates to a_k, so each such term carries a_k/ā. Third, the second-order part and the Gaussian mean (mechanism (γ)): for A in the cutoff ball, with f(t) := F_j(tA) analytic for |t| ≤ T := m_k/sup_b|g_kCA(b)| ≥ T₀ and bounded by b^c_j there, the remainder is bounded by its Taylor tail,

R_{2,j}(A) = f(1) − f(0) − f′(0), |R_{2,j}(A)| ≤ Σ_{n≥2}T^{−n} sup_{|t|=T}|f(t)| ≤ C_Tb^c_j(sup_b|g_kCA(b)|/m_k)², C_T := (1 − 1/T₀)^{−1},

and under the cutoff Gaussian in the real background, whose mass is ≥ 1/2, ⟨sup_b|g_kCA(b)|²⟩ ≤ 2g_k²‖C‖²⟨max_{b∈Y}|A(b)|²⟩_{Gauss} ≤ 4C_Gg_k²(1 + log #bonds(Y)) ≤ 4C_Gg_k²(c_k + d_k(Y)), c_k := 1 + log(2d(LM₂R_k)^d), by the diagonal bound (3.132) of the covariance in the rescaled variable (‖C‖ = O(1), p.267 l.47–49, absorbed in C_G) and the bound on the maximum of Gaussians; in the complex background the same mean is bounded by a constant times this one (§8.5, the Gaussian-mean part of the statement), absorbed in C_G. The factor 1 + d_k(Y) is absorbed in the localisation exp(−κd_k(Y)) at the cost of κ → κ′, which the output's better decay (1+4β)κ affords (p.262 l.26–30), through the constant C_X := sup_X Σ_{Y∩X≠∅}(1 + d_k(Y))exp(−(κ − κ′)d_k(Y)), the domain count of the localisation. So the cluster consisting of R_{2,j} alone is ≤ 8C_TC_Gc_k(1 + d_k(Y))μ_kb^c_j with μ_k := (g_k/m_k)² ≤ 4c_m^{−2}(log g_k^{−2})^{−2min(q₀,q₁)}, the factor 4 from m_k = α_{3,k}/2, a fraction of the age-decayed class bound that is a negative power of log g_k^{−2}; the clusters connecting R_{2,j} or L_j to activities are ≤ C_KP·4b^c_j·a_k by the bound on (3.43) (|L_j(A)| = |f′(0)| ≤ b^c_j/T ≤ b^c_j by Cauchy, so |L_j|, |R_{2,j}| ≤ 3b^c_j there). Counting the L^{4(k+1−j)} scale-j points per scale-(k+1) point, L^{4(k+1−j)}L^{−(4+β′)(k−j)} = L⁴L^{−β′(k−j)}, and dividing by E₀, the generated difference from (a) per scale-(k+1) point in the class norm is

(8.2) d^{(a)}_{k+1} ≤ ε_k^*(D_k + S_k), D_k := Σ_{j≤k}L^{−β′(k−j)}d_j, S_k := Σ_{j≤k}d^R_j, ε_k^* := C_XC_KPL⁴C_α[2³C_TC_Gc_kμ_k + 4a_k],

with S_k the carried R-differences of §8.4 (no age factor, so the normalisation 1 in place of L^{−β′(k−j)} and the same ε_k^*, the R-terms' class bounds R₁g^{κ₀} of (2.31) in place of E₀ being the smaller), and ε_k^* → 0 as g_k → 0: μ_k is a negative power of log g_k^{−2}, c_k a double logarithm, and a_k = g_kP_a(log g_k^{−2}). Every step is taken on Bałaban's analyticity domain, so the bound holds in the class norm with no conversion between real sizes and class radii.

The coupling part (b): at g^B the frozen terms' fluctuation parts are F_j((1+τ_k)A) in place of F_j(A), and G_j(A) := F_j((1+τ_k)A) − F_j(A) = Σ_{n≥1}a_n[(1+τ_k)^n − 1] with f(t) = Σ_na_nt^n and |a_n| ≤ b^c_jT^{−n} by Cauchy has G_j(0) = 0, the odd linear part τ_ka_1 = τ_kDF_j(0)A, and the remainder Σ_{n≥2}, bounded by |τ_k|b^c_jΣ_{n≥2}n(1+|τ_k|)^{n−1}T^{−n} ≤ |τ_k|C_T′b^c_j(sup_b|g_kCA(b)|/m_k)² with C_T′ := (9/2)(1 − 3/(2T₀))^{−2} (|τ_k| ≤ 1/2 and Σ_{n≥2}nx^n ≤ 2x²(1 − x)^{−2}); the action's own terms of order n ≥ 3 change by |τ_k| times their bounds, a_k for the odd cubic part (connected clusters: a_k²) and a Gaussian mean ≤ C_Gg_k²p₀(g_k)²-type for the quartic part, both below the bracket of ε_k^*. With the frozen terms' own class bounds C_αL^{−(4+β′)(k−j)} in place of b^c_j/E₀ and Σ_jL^{−β′(k−j)} ≤ (1 − L^{−β′})^{−1}, the coupling part is ≤ C_gε_k^*g_k²|δ_k| with C_g := (1 − L^{−β′})^{−1}max(C_T′/(2C_T), 1), and the shell term of (b) is smaller still.

### 8.3 The Recursion (Derived, with §8.5–8.6)

With (8.1), (8.2), the coupling and shell parts of §8.2(b), and the marked-cluster bound of §8.5, the generated difference per scale-(k+1) point in the class norm is

(8.3) d_{k+1} ≤ ε_k^*(D_k + S_k) + C_gε_k^*g_k²|δ_k| + e_{k+1},

with ε_k^* and C_g of (8.2) and §8.2(b), e_{k+1} the depth terms (classical: (9.10) at the fluctuated block field, §8.1; Gaussian and configuration: §10.5), and |Δβ_{k+1}| ≤ C_βd_{k+1} by Cauchy's estimate (the quadratic coefficient Π^Δ_{22,11} of (3.61)–(3.64)), so that the coupling difference is δ_j = Σ_{i=j+1}^{K}Δβ_i from the pinned value δ_K = 0 and |δ_j| ≤ C_βΣ_{i>j}d_i (the per-step coefficient β_i is the marginal part of the step's output, whose two-run difference is the marginal part of the generated difference, and its own dependence on the coupling is inside d_i through §8.2(b)). For the carried sum this gives D_{k+1} ≤ θ_kD_k + ε_k^*S_k + C_gε_k^*g_k²|δ_k| + e_{k+1} with θ_k := L^{−β′} + ε_k^*. C_α, C_T, C_X, C_G, and C_KP are independent of j and k (Theorem 2's E₁ is independent of j, k; C_KP depends on the activity bounds, which are uniform in k), and ε^*(g) increases with g for g small, so ε_k^* ≤ ε^* := sup_{i≤K}ε^*(g_i) ≤ sup_{g≤(1+β₀)γ}ε^*(g) at every shared scale (g_i ≤ (1+β₀)g_K by CRE (2.6)), and θ_k ≤ θ := L^{−β′} + ε^* < 1 under (T1): the contraction is a threshold on the couplings, not an inequality between Bałaban's constants. The delay before the age decay sets in is absorbed in E₁, which does not depend on R_j (it is uniform in j while R_j varies with j). The same recursion holds for the dev insertions of the dressed loops (the project's class D(k), whose per-step map is the same fluctuation integral with the loop's words as marked activities) and for the perimeter coefficients, which are the marginal parts of the loop's generated terms: the loop's words are additional marked activities in the same fluctuation measure, with the same localisation and analyticity, and their two-run difference is the frozen difference of the dev insertions, so (8.2)–(8.3) hold for them with the dev insertions' class norms in place of d_j. The recursion is solved in §8.7.

### 8.4 The R-Sector (Derived, with §8.6)

The R-terms have no age decay beyond the volume factor (CRE p.283 l.35–39: O(1)(L^jL^{−n})⁴g_j^{κ₀}exp(−κd_j(X)) per scale-j point), so their differences enter (8.3) as S_k = Σ_{j≤k}d^R_j without the factor L^{−β′(k−j)} of (8.2). Their fallback bound is the triangle inequality with (2.44), d^R_j ≤ 2R₁g_j^{κ₀}, whose sum over j ≤ k is O(ḡ^{κ₀−2}), uniform in K and not small; so the R-sector needs its own two-run bound. A term of the generation k+1 has two sources. (i) The renormalisation of the large-field components at the step k+1, as the logarithm of the quotient LFR I (0.5), with the bound (2.31), g_{k+1}^{κ₀}exp(−κd_{k+1}(X)): by (8.4) of §8.6 its two-run difference is ≤ c_{k+1}𝒟_{[k+1]} + c^B_{k+1}𝒟^∗_{k+1} + c^V_{k+1}𝒟^V_{k+1} + ν_{k+1}, c_j := C_R(100MR_j)^dg_j^{κ₀}, c^B_j := C^B_R(100MR_j)^dg_j^{κ₀}, c^V_j := C^V_R(100MR_j)^dg_j^{κ₀}, 𝒟^∗ the boundary terms' source of `WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §4(e), 𝒟^V the old terms' own differences in the V(Y) (§8.6, *The Own Differences of the V(Y).*), ν_j the fine-scale and old-history bounds of §8.6 with Σ_{j≤K}ν_j ≤ C_νw_K, 𝒟_{[j]} := max_{k_s/8−1<m≤j}e^{−½C_P(j−m)}𝒟_m the memory-weighted maximum over the component's history (§8.7's sums treat it as a scale-j quantity: in the weighted norm there max_{m≤j}e^{−½C_P(j−m)}m_m ≤ m_j, since e^{−½C_P} ≤ e^{−1} < ½ ≤ θ̃, and S_{m−1} ≤ S_{j−1}), 𝒟_m := D^♭_m + S_{m−1} + C|δ_m| + e_m + h_m, h_m := C_hθ̃^m, D^♭_m := Σ_{i≤m}q^{m−i}d_i, q := L^{−1/3}e^{−1/12}. Its first four terms bound the relative difference of the two runs' integrands per point of the history at the scale m: the frozen small-field exponents, through D_m ≤ D^♭_m, which holds since L^{−β′} ≤ q, that is (β′ − 1/3)log L ≥ 1/12, true for β′ ≥ 19/40 (§5) and L ≥ 2, with D^♭ also carrying the coupling part of the exterior part W_ext of 𝐂_j (§8.6), the carried R-differences, the coupling, which enters the action as Cε_m²|δ_m|, the function (1.6) of LFR I as 8|δ_m| per variable (§6.2), and the normalisation constants, the radial shells, the coupling part of the plaquette shells, and the denominators of §8.6 as ≤ C|δ_m| per point, and the depth terms. The open input (LF) bounds the rest, the threshold shells and the large-field integrands, by h_n per point at every scale of a history born at the scale n > k_s/8, and §8.6 carries it through the term m = n of 𝒟_{[j]}. (ii) The fluctuation integral's own R-output: by (3.26) the frozen R-terms of the recent generations, 𝐑″_k = Σ_{j=k₁+1}^{k}𝐑^{(j)}, are not merged into 𝐄_k but carried through the step by the s-interpolation (p.271 l.1–30), and the step's output contains 𝐑″^{(k+1)}, their fluctuation parts integrated (p.279 l.17–20); k₁ is the largest integer with L^{−(k−k₁)}MR_j ≤ L (p.270 l.48–51), read with j = k₁. The inequality fails at k₁ + 1, so the memory N′_k := k − k₁ satisfies N′_k < log_L(MR_{k₁+1}), and CRE (2.9) (p.256 l.12–17), R_m ≤ L(1 + g_n²b₂(n − m))^{β₀}R_n for m ≤ n, with b₂ Bałaban's coefficient β′ of (2.6) and (2.9), renamed here since β′ is the age exponent of §5 in this note, gives R_{k₁+1} ≤ 2LR_k, since g_k²N′_k is small for γ small. Hence N′_k ≤ log_L(MR_k) + 2 ≤ N′(g_k), N′(g) := log_L(MR(g)) + 3, a double logarithm of g_k^{−1}. The two-run difference of 𝐑″^{(k+1)} is the generated difference of §8.2 with the frozen R-differences of the window as the marked activities, bounded by (8.2) with the window sum in place of D_k + S_k, together with the measure difference Δ𝐏 acting on run A's 𝐑″_k, whose fluctuation parts are activities of size ≤ 2R₁N′_kg_k^{κ₀}/E₀ connected to the marked activity: d^{R,(ii)}_{k+1} ≤ 2ε_k^*Σ_{j=k−N′_k+1}^{k}d^R_j + C_KP(2R₁/E₀)N′_kg_k^{κ₀}ε_k^*(D_k + S_k). The R-differences of the generations j ≤ k₁ are merged into 𝐄_k and carried with the weight 1 inside S_k. Together,

d^R_{k+1} ≤ c_{k+1}𝒟_{[k+1]} + c^B_{k+1}𝒟^∗_{k+1} + c^V_{k+1}𝒟^V_{k+1} + ν_{k+1} + 2ε_k^*Σ_{j=k−N′_k+1}^{k}d^R_j + c′_{k+1}(D_k + S_k), c′_{k+1} := C_KP(2R₁/E₀)N′_kg_k^{κ₀}ε_k^*, ε_k^* := ε^*(g_k),

and the window lemma: if u_{i+1} ≤ s_{i+1} + η_iΣ_{j=i−N′_i+1}^{i}u_j for all i, with Σ_{i: i−N′_i<j≤i}η_i ≤ 1/2 for every j, then Σ_{j≤k}u_j ≤ 2Σ_{j≤k}s_j (summing over i, Σ_{i≤k}u_i ≤ Σ_is_i + Σ_ju_jΣ_{i: i−N′_i<j≤i}η_i). With η_i = 2ε_i^*: the i with i − N′_i < j ≤ i satisfy 0 ≤ i − j < N′_i, so g_i ≤ (1 + g_i²b₂N′_i)^{1/2}g_j ≤ 2g_j and g_i ≥ g_j/(1+β₀) by CRE (2.6). Since the memory bound N′(g) ≥ N′_i above decreases in g (R grows as g decreases), there are at most N′(g_j/(1+β₀)) such i, each with η_i ≤ 2ε̂^*(g_j). The condition is therefore 4ε̂^*(g_j)N′(g_j/(1+β₀)) ≤ 1, which is part of (T1), and S_k ≤ 2Σ_{j≤k}[c_j𝒟_{[j]} + c^B_j𝒟^∗_j + c^V_j𝒟^V_j + ν_j + c′_j(D_{j−1} + S_{j−1})]. The terms S_{m−1} inside 𝒟_{[j]}, 𝒟^∗_j, and 𝒟^V_j and S_{j−1} in c′_j are a discrete Gronwall feedback, since S_{m−1} ≤ S_{j−1} for m ≤ j: S_k ≤ Σ_{j≤k}(2c_j + 2c^B_j + 2c^V_j + 2c′_j)S_{j−1} + Σ_{j≤k}[2c_j𝒟′_{[j]} + 2c^B_j𝒟^{∗′}_j + 2c^V_j𝒟^{V′}_j + 2c′_jD_{j−1}] + 2C_νw_K, 𝒟′_m := D^♭_m + C|δ_m| + e_m + h_m, and 𝒟^{∗′}_j and 𝒟^{V′}_j the maxima 𝒟^∗_j and 𝒟^V_j with the terms S omitted. Here 2c′_jD_{j−1} ≤ 2c_jD_{j−1}, since c′_j/c_j = (2/E₀)(C_KPR₁/C_R)N′_{j−1}ε^*_{j−1}(g_{j−1}/g_j)^{κ₀}(100MR_j)^{−d} ≤ 1 for γ small: ε^*_{j−1} ≤ ε^*_γ is small (§8.7), N′_{j−1} ≤ (100MR_j)^d, and g_{j−1}/g_j ≤ 1 + β₀ by CRE (2.6). So

(8.5) S_k ≤ C_S[Σ_{j≤k}c_j(𝒟′_{[j]} + D_{j−1}) + Σ_{j≤k}c^B_j𝒟^{∗′}_j + Σ_{j≤k}c^V_j𝒟^{V′}_j + C_νw_K], C_S := 2exp(2C_Σ), C_Σ := sup_KΣ_{j≤K}(c_j + c^B_j + c^V_j + c′_j),

with C_Σ finite and uniform in K: (100MR_j)^d is a power of log g_j^{−2}, N′_j a double logarithm, and g_j^{κ₀} ≤ (ḡ^{−2} + b(K − j) − C)^{−κ₀/2} by Lemma 2.1(i), summable over j for κ₀ > 2. The feedback of S into (8.3) is through ε^*S_k, and (8.5) feeds D back into S with the weights c_j, which at the fine scales are ≤ (bK/2)^{−κ₀/2} times a polylogarithm; with the R-sector source Σ_jν_j ≤ C_νw_K of §8.6, whose fine-scale part is a sum over at most K fine scales, this is the origin of the term μ_K = K^{1−κ₀/2}ℓ_K inside the weight w_K of the solution of §8.7.

### 8.5 The Marked-Cluster Bound (Reading R-Mark, Bałaban's Construction Applied to the Insertion Δ𝐏)

*Statement.* For the step-(k+1) fluctuation integral with an admissible interaction 𝐏_t and a localised insertion I(Y), Y ∈ 𝐃_k, analytic on the spaces (3.43) with |I(Y)| ≤ ι(Y) exp(−κd_k(Y)) there, the expectation ⟨I(Y)⟩_t has the localised expansion (3.47), ⟨I(Y)⟩_t = Σ_{X ∈ 𝐃_{k+1}, X ∩ Y ≠ ∅} 𝐈(X), with |𝐈(X)| ≤ C_KP[ι_G(Y) + a_kι(Y)] exp(−κ′d_{k+1}(X)), where ι_G(Y) is the bound of the Gaussian expectation of I(Y) on its cube in the complex background, the term of the expansion in which the insertion is connected to no activity of 𝐏_t, and a_kι(Y) bounds every term in which it is connected to at least one activity (the Cauchy parameter on the activities, lem:c5-5R-2(c)); ι_G(Y) ≤ C_G″ times the mean of |I(Y)| under the real-background cutoff Gaussian, since the imaginary part of the quadratic form on the class is a relative O(α̃_{1,k}) perturbation of Δ^{(k)} (RG I (1.5) with PBG (3.132) on the complex class), so that for a quadratic function of A the complex-background mean is the real one up to a constant factor; the constants C_KP, C_G″, and κ′ ≥ (1+4β)κ are those of Bałaban's bound for his own insertion (p.262 l.26–30 for the decay), independent of k, and the output 𝐈(X) is analytic on the class U^c_{k+1} of the next scale, as Bałaban's output is (p.262 l.26–28, p.279 l.22–23).

*Evidence.* CRE (3.37), p.274: 𝐄_0^{(k+1)}(Λ, U, b) is defined through ∫₀¹dt ⟨tr 𝐕′_k(tg_k, A, b)(CA)(b)⟩_t, an interpolation in the coupling of the interaction with the localised insertion tr𝐕′(CA)(b) under the interacting measure ⟨·⟩_t; p.277 l.33–47 and p.278 l.1–11 expand "the last expectation value in (3.37)": the nominator is (3.44) with one factor H′(Z₀) "given by an integral with the integrand including, as one of the factors, a localized term from the expansion of the integrated function", and "Although different, it satisfies the same bound as the other factors"; (3.45)–(3.47) exponentiate and resum into terms 𝐄_0^{(k+1)}(Λ, X, b) which "satisfy the bounds (2.42), with the constant B₀ replaced by O(p₀³(g_k))", the insertion's own size, "and the multiplication by g_k yields small bounds". The only properties of the insertion used are its localisation, its analyticity on (3.43), and its bound, each entering linearly; so the output bound is the insertion's bound times a constant of the expansion. For the insertion Δ𝐏(Y) of §8.2, localisation and analyticity hold because both runs' frozen terms have them (§5), and the bound is (8.2). The measure ⟨·⟩_t with 𝐏_t = 𝐏^A + tΔ𝐏 is of the kind expanded on p.277, since 𝐏_t satisfies the inductive bounds (§8.1).

*What it gives.* (8.1) with the reading: 𝐄_0^{B,(k+2)} − 𝐄_0^{A,(k+1)} = Σ_Y ∫₀¹⟨Δ𝐏(Y)⟩_t dt has localised terms bounded by C_KP[the Gaussian mean of Δ𝐏(Y) + a_k‖Δ𝐏(Y)‖] exp(−κ′d_{k+1}(X)), analytic on the class, which is the inequality the Status called (G′). With the three facts of §8.2 this is (8.2), and with the coupling and shell parts (8.3).

### 8.6 The Renormalisation Quotient (Derived except at the Coarse Histories, Which Use the Open Input (LF), and at the Per-Polymer Pieces, Which Are Open)

*Lemma (real backgrounds).* Let f^A, f^B be positive functions on a compact group power with |log(f^B/f^A)| ≤ φ_* pointwise. Then ∫f^B/∫f^A ∈ [e^{−φ_*}, e^{φ_*}]. For quotients q^{A/B} := ∫f^{A/B}_1/∫f^{A/B}_2 of two such pairs, |log q^B − log q^A| ≤ 2φ_* and |q^B − q^A| ≤ (e^{2φ_*} − 1)q^A ≤ 4φ_*q^A for φ_* ≤ 1/2. Proof: ∫f^B = ∫f^Ae^{log(f^B/f^A)} and the pointwise bound; divide; exponentiate. This is the statement at real backgrounds, where the densities are positive (LFR I p.176 l.19–20); the class norm needs the complex version below, which uses absolute integration in place of positivity.

*Application (the complex class).* Bałaban bounds the renormalised terms by uniform bounds on the integrands followed by integration over the real variables: LFR I p.191 l.49–52, "They are easily obtained estimating the integral (1.60), after the renormalization and the localization. All the expressions in the exponential in this integral can be uniformly bounded in the usual way, except possibly expressions connected with 𝔹_k^{(n)}" (the cumulation of those boundary expressions is controlled on p.191 l.52–p.192 l.6 by their factors g_j), on the complex spaces Ũ^{(n)c}_k of pp.190–191 built for this purpose (p.190 l.2–4, "to gain a larger domain of analyticity for the effective action, in order to perform the basic step in the R-operation"), with the result (1.70) (p.192), |ℂ^{(n)}_k(X, (𝕌, 𝕁))| ≤ C₀exp(−(1+3β)κd_m(X)), analytic on the space. The renormalisation of a component Z′ at step j replaces ρ(Z, V) by ρ(Z″, V) times the quotient q(V) := ∫dV|_{Z′}ρ(Z, V)/∫dV|_{Z′}ρ(Z″, V) (LFR I (0.3)–(0.5), p.177 l.1–5), whose numerator n is the large-field integral over the component and whose denominator d is the small-field expression localised near Z′, the logarithm of which is given by the convergent small-field expansion, the same expansion that produces the step's small-field output, analytic on the class. At the same complex V the two runs' numerators n^A, n^B are the same operation applied to run-dependent ingredients, of the three kinds of §6.2: (α) the small-field ingredients of the component's history, which on the common support of the characteristic functions give integrands I^B = I^Ae^{D}, together with the function (1.6), whose two runs' measures differ by ≤ e^{4|δ_m|} − 1 ≤ 8|δ_m| per variable relative to the measure itself (§6.2), the localised constants, and the radial shells (§6.2); the shells of the plaquette and reference-configuration tests, (β) of the Open line; and the large-field integrands, among them the term V(X^∼) of LFR II (1.71) for the component X, the terms localised in its component of Z^∼ (p.379 l.24–25), and the boundary terms 𝐁, (γ) of the Open line. The factors V(Y) of (1.91), with Y ∖ Z^∼ ≠ ∅, lie outside the quotients and are compared in *From the quotients to the R-terms* below. (β) and (γ) are the input (LF) in the form (LF-2), with h_n = C_hθ̃^n per point of the history at every scale, n its birth scale. The boundary terms 𝐁 enter instead through `WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §4(e). Those that are not deep are ≤ 2(2B₁ + C_XB₀C_w)C_B(𝒟^∗_j + C_hθ̃^{k_s/8}) per point of a layer of the history, with no age factor. The deep ones that meet the component when its history lies at the layers > k_s/8 are ≤ 2C_Dk̄Ē₀2^{−k_s/16} per point of the component at the scale j. Here Ē₀ := max(E₀, B₀, R₁), since the deep terms are frozen 𝐄-, 𝐑-, and 𝐁-terms, and k̄ := max(k_s, j) ≤ K bounds the number of generations summed at the step j, which exceeds k_s at the steps beyond the stopping scale. The other deep terms are those of "Fine scales and old histories" below. So |n^B − n^A| ≤ (e^φ − 1)n̄, with φ the total exponent difference below and n̄ the majorant of ∫|I^A| that Bałaban's uniform bound controls. The denominators' logarithms differ by ψ := |log d^B − log d^A|. The denominator is LFR II (1.1)–(1.2) at the step j of the renormalisation (Bałaban's k), d = ∫dV′|_Λ δ_{G₀}(V′)χ exp[−g_j^{−2}A(ζ₀, U_{j,Z}(V′V_Λ))] over a parallelepiped Λ inside a cube of side 100M. By (1.10) (p.358), log d = −g_j^{−2}A(ζ₀, U₀) − I(U₀) + E_j(Λ), with E_j(Λ) = (−½d(𝐠)log g_j^{−2} + log σ₀)|Λ^{(j)}∖G₀| and −I(U₀) the logarithm of the B′-integral of (1.2). These are analytic on the class, with ε_j replaced by O(α_{0,j} + α_{1,j}) in the bounds (p.358 l.27–33). With x := g_j^{−2} and |x^B − x^A| = |δ_j|, the run-dependence is the following.
- E_j(Λ) changes by ½d(𝐠)|log(1 + g_j²δ_j)| ≤ d(𝐠)g_j²|δ_j| per bond of Λ^{(j)}.
- The coupling of the action changes it by |δ_j||A(ζ₀, U₀)| ≤ 3|δ_j| per plaquette of Λ^{(j)}, since 0 ≤ ζ₀ ≤ 1 and |1 − Re tr U₀(∂p)| ≤ 3 on the class.
- The coupling inside I, at fixed fine-lattice objects, enters through the exponent and through the threshold of χ (the soft form of |V′ − 1| < M₀ε_j, i.e. χ̄(|B′(b)|/t) per bond, t := M₀p₀(g_j)).
  - For the exponent, ∂_x[log d + xA(ζ₀, U₀)] is minus the average of A(ζ₀, U_{j,Z}(V′V_Λ)) − A(ζ₀, U₀) under d's integrand. On the support of χ, x times this difference is the exponent of (1.2): the form, bounded per bond by the boundedness of the quadratic form (p.358 l.23–24) at |B′| < t; the linear term, bounded by (1.6); and V, bounded by O(g_j^{1−β})|Λ| (p.357 l.9–10). So the difference is ≤ Cg_j²p₀(g_j)² per bond. The average is taken against |d|, which is ≥ exp(−O(1)log M|Λ|) times the integral of the integrand's modulus (the lower bound of p.358 l.24–25 with (1.11), and the upper bound from (1.9)), so ∂_x is ≤ C(M)g_j²p₀(g_j)² per bond and the change is ≤ C(M)g_j²p₀(g_j)²|δ_j| per bond.
  - For the threshold, the other run's radius t′ has |t′ − t| ≤ Ctg_j²|δ_j|, since p₀(g) is a power of log g^{−2}. The cutoff χ of (1.2) is the soft test of LFR I (1.101) (`WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §3(d)), a product over the bonds b of Λ of χ̄(|B′(b)|/t). Per bond |χ̄(f/t′) − χ̄(f/t)| ≤ (1 + κ_s)|t′ − t|/(κ_s min(t, t′)) ≤ 4Cg_j²|δ_j|/κ_s, and both factors vanish where f ≥ (1 + κ_s)max(t, t′). Telescoping over the bonds, with every other factor at most 1, the change of d is ≤ |Λ^{(j)}|(4Cg_j²|δ_j|/κ_s) times the integral of the modulus of d's integrand with the sharp cutoff at (1 + κ_s)max(t, t′). By (1.10)–(1.11) at either radius |d| is ≥ exp(−O(1)log M|Λ|) times that integral, as in the exponent bullet, and |Λ| ≤ O(M⁴) (p.358 l.26). So the shell changes log d by ≤ C(M)g_j²|δ_j|/κ_s per bond, for γ small. A density bound for a sharp cutoff doesn't work here: the cutoff is soft.
- The rest is the fine-lattice objects: U₀ (the minimiser of (1.3) on the run's fine lattice), H_{1,j}, Δ₁(ζ₀), Im∂U₀, and the argument of V. They enter d only through the action A(ζ₀, U_{j,Z}(V′V_Λ)) of its integrand. At equal couplings `WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §4(d), *The denominator.*, compares d as a whole by the Lemma above, |log d^B − log d^A| ≤ φ_d, with φ_d ≤ h_n per point of the layers of the component's history plus h_n per bond of Λ^{(j)}, n the history's birth scale. This is (γ) of the Open line, in the form (LF-2), derived at real configurations. On the complex class the same bullet gives |log d^B − log d^A| ≤ |D(1)| + 4e^{c(M)}C_d(M)C_hθ̃^n, with D(1) the two runs' difference of the exponents at V′ = 1, bounded by the comparisons of that note's §4(b)–(c) on the class (that note's *The complex class.*), and e^{−c(M)} = exp(−O(M⁴)) the lower bound of p.358 l.24–30; the factor e^{c(M)}C_d(M) depends on M only and is absorbed into C_h.

So ψ ≤ C_ψΣ_{points of Λ at the scale j}(|δ_j| + h_n) + Σ_{points of the history}h_n, with C_ψ a constant of d, L, G, and M; Λ lies in the component's history at the scale j. Hence |d^B| ≥ e^{−ψ}|d^A| and |1/d^B − 1/d^A| ≤ (e^ψ − 1)/|d^A|. The numerators' cutoff χ′ = χ({|B(b)| < g_j^{−1}δ′_j}) of LFR II (1.70), with δ′_j = g_jA₁p₁(g_j) (LFR I p.183 l.2), is LFR I's sharp (1.82), and a density bound controls its shell relative to Bałaban's positivity bound of the B-integral (LFR II p.383 l.30–34). The shell between the radii lies in the union over bonds b of {t ≤ |B(b)| < t′}, and under the modulus of the integrand each such set has mass ≤ C|t′ − t|t′^{d_G−1}(‖Q_{bb}‖/2π)^{d_G/2}, Q the form of the integrand, positive and bounded, so that the marginal variance of B(b) is ≥ (Q_{bb})^{−1}, with the other factors bounded. Its form ⟨DH″_{1,j,Z}B, ζDH″_{1,j,Z}B⟩ is bounded per bond, since H″_{1,j,Z} has the random-walk expansion of PBG (3.107) with the kernel bounds (3.108) in the norms of PBG (3.42)–(3.47) (LFR II p.362 l.42–48, PBG p.416). The action term contributes the factor exp(−A) ≤ 1, and the V(Y) are bounded by (1.68)–(1.69). So the shell is ≤ Cp₁(g_j)^{d_G}g_j²|δ_j| per bond relative to that bound. Hence |q^B − q^A| ≤ |n^B − n^A|/|d^B| + |n^A||1/d^B − 1/d^A| ≤ [e^ψ(e^φ − 1) + (e^ψ − 1)]q̄ ≤ 2(e+1)(φ + ψ)q̄ for φ, ψ ≤ 1, and ≤ q̄^A + q̄^B ≤ (φ + ψ)(q̄^A + q̄^B) by the triangle inequality otherwise, with q̄ = q̄^A := n̄^A/|d^A| Bałaban's majorant of run A's quotient and q̄^B run B's, the 𝐓′-bound of the component's factor that his derivation (1.92)–(1.97) uses (LFR II p.389 l.13–16).

*The Representation of d.* The main proof uses Bałaban's representation. After (1.49) d is no separate factor: −log d is part of 𝐂_k, through I(U₀) "from the denominator" (LFR II p.369 l.7), and 𝐂_k is localised with the old terms into the V(Y) of (1.68), among them V(X^∼) of (1.71) (p.373 l.10–17, p.377 l.2–17). Since (1.49) is an equality for (1.1), the transformed integral of a component equals its quotient n/d, and the comparison above is that of the whole: φ is the exponent difference of the integrand with the pieces of −log d left out, and ψ is that of −log d as a whole, so d is counted once, and the bound is that of e^{φ+ψ} − 1 = e^ψ(e^φ − 1) + (e^ψ − 1) for the combined exponent. The expansion (1.92)–(1.97) needs the difference per localised piece, since the pieces of −log d in a V(Y) with Y ∖ Z^∼ ≠ ∅ lie outside the component's quotient. That comparison is `WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §4(d), *The Localised Pieces of 𝐂_k (open).*, and it is open.

The exponent differences φ, ψ on the component: its points are those of its history, the layers Ω_m ∖ Ω_{m+1} and the large-field variables at the scales m ≤ j, with p_m(Z) scale-m points at the scale m, p_m(Z) ≤ L^d|Z_{m+1} ∩ Z|_{m+1} for m < j and p_j(Z) ≤ L^{2d}|Z_j ∩ Z|_j. Here p_m(Z) ≥ 1 at every scale m from n − 1 up to j, n the birth scale of the oldest region merged into Z, whose finest layer lies at the scale n − 1 (`WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §1; §11(c), Γ_{n−1} ⊂ Z_n), and Σ_mp_m(Z) ≤ (1 + L^d)P_j(Z) (the term m = j alone is ≤ L^dP_j(Z)), P_j(Z) the history volume of §11(c) at the scale j. On each of these points the exponent difference is ≤ 𝒟_m + h_n, 𝒟_m of §8.4, in the class norm (the frozen terms of the layer have age m − i for the generation i and are evaluated at U_m-type configurations, CRE p.280 l.15–17, so R-Δ^c gives D_m ≤ D^♭_m; the carried R-differences S_{m−1}; the coupling with (1.6); the depth terms; and (LF), ≤ h_n). At the points of Λ, at the scale j, the coupling part of the exterior part W_ext of 𝐂_j adds ≤ max_{n−1≤l≤j}|δ_l| + C_βD^♭_{n−1} (`WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §4(d), *The couplings of W_ext.*, with C_wcε̂² ≤ 1), which is ≤ (1 + C_β)max_{n−1≤m≤j}𝒟_m, since |δ_l| ≤ 𝒟_l for C ≥ 1 and D^♭_{n−1} ≤ 𝒟_{n−1}. So φ + ψ ≤ (3 + C_ψ)Σ_{m=n−1}^{j}p_m(Z)(𝒟_m + h_n) + (1 + C_β)p_j(Z)max_{n−1≤m≤j}𝒟_m, with the collar points of the last bullet below at m = j, ≤ C_nP_j(Z)²e^{½C_PP_j(Z)}𝒟_{[j]}, C_n := 2(1 + L^d + 3^d)(4 + C_ψ + C_β), since 𝒟_m ≤ e^{½C_P(j−m)}𝒟_{[j]} and h_n ≤ 𝒟_n ≤ e^{½C_P(j−n)}𝒟_{[j]} (n > k_s/8 for the histories where (LF) enters, below), with j − m + 1 ≤ P_j(Z) for n − 1 ≤ m ≤ j. The factor is paid by the component's weight. By (11.4) and (11.2) at the scale j, κ^tr_j(Z) ≥ κ̂_j(Z) + P^c_j(Z) + C_PP_j(Z) + ½G_j(Z), so the component's factor exp(−κ^tr_j(Z) − 2p₀(g_{j(Z)})) in (1.79) is ≤ exp(−C_PP_j(Z))·exp(−κ̂_j(Z) − P^c_j(Z) − 2p₀(g_{j(Z)})). In the last factor κ̂_j(Z) ≥ 0 satisfies (1.80), and P^c_j(Z) pays the integrand's history cost as in §11(c). Bałaban's derivation of the quotient bound (2.31) from (1.89) (LFR II (1.89)–(1.97)) uses of κ only κ ≥ 0, which is (1.89) (p.387 l.30–33), and (1.80). So for either run q̄ improves to e^{−C_PP_j(Z)}q̄, and |q^B − q^A| ≤ 2(e + 1)(φ + ψ)e^{−C_PP_j(Z)}q̄ ≤ 2(e + 1)C_n𝒟_{[j]}q̄, because P²e^{−½C_PP} ≤ (4/(eC_P))² ≤ 1 for C_P ≥ 2. The boundary terms add C^B_n(𝒟^∗_j + C_hθ̃^{k_s/8})P_j(Z) to φ, C^B_n := 2(1 + L^d)(2B₁ + C_XB₀C_w)C_B, by the count Σ_mp_m(Z) ≤ (1 + L^d)P_j(Z). The variation of the term V(Z^∼) (Bałaban's V(X^∼) of (1.71) for X = Z) at the points of its collar Z^∼ ∖ Z, which lie outside the history, adds ≤ C_VC_hθ̃^{k_s/8}P_j(Z), for the following reasons.
- On the class V(Z^∼) is bounded by (1.69), O(1)Σ_l|Γ^0_l ∩ Z^∼|.
- Cauchy's estimate on the disc of `WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §4(d)(ii) bounds the variation by 2r times this bound, with r the supremum of d̄/ρ over the collar. The collar lies in Λ_j, at the layer j > k_s/8: Z^∼ is a component of the neighbourhood of the large-field region Λ_j^c, and each component of Λ_j^c has its own (p.379 l.24–25 with (1.71)), so the collar meets no other component. Hence r ≤ C_hθ̃^{k_s/8} by that note's §4(e), "Into the quotients".
- The points of the old determining set in Z^∼ are those of the history, ≤ (1 + L^d)P_j(Z), and those of the collar. The collar is one layer of the cubes of which the large-field region is a union (CRE p.245 l.41–43), so each of its cubes touches a cube of Z, and |Z^∼ ∖ Z|_j ≤ (3^d − 1)|Z_j ∩ Z|_j ≤ (3^d − 1)L^{−d}P_j(Z) (§11(c)).
- So C_V := 2O(1)(1 + L^d + 3^d), with O(1) the constant of (1.69).
- The old terms at the collar points also carry their own two-run differences, part (i) of that note's §4(d), *The functions of the configurations.*, which is ≤ 𝒟_j per point, since the collar lies in Ω_j at the scale j. These points enter φ with the history points at m = j, and their number ≤ (3^d − 1)L^{−d}P_j(Z) ≤ 3^dP_j(Z) is the 3^d in C_n.

The deep terms meeting a young component add ≤ 2L^dC_Dk̄Ē₀2^{−k_s/16}P_j(Z). With Pe^{−C_PP} ≤ 1, |q^B − q^A| gains 2(e + 1)[C^B_n𝒟^∗_j + C^θ_nθ̃^{k_s/8} + 2L^dC_Dk̄Ē₀2^{−k_s/16}]q̄, C^θ_n := (C^B_n + C_V)C_h.

*From the quotients to the R-terms.* The terms 𝐑(X, V) of LFR I (0.5)–(0.6) are built from the quotients by Bałaban's expansion (1.92)–(1.97) of LFR II. There the 𝐓′-bound of each renormalised component X_{j_h}, times the weight exp((1 + β + β/(3·2^d))κM^{−d}|X_{j_h}|) of (1.93), "The product over h there is estimated by ∏_{h=1}^q exp2κ(100R_k)^d" (p.389 l.13–14), is ≤ exp(−(3/2)p₀(g_k)) under the condition of p.389 l.16, and the sum over the families of components converges by (1.96). Interpolating each renormalised component's operation, 𝐓′_t := 𝐓′^A + t(𝐓′^B − 𝐓′^A), every interpolated factor obeys the bound that both runs' factors obey, and the derivative in t is the same sum with one factor replaced by the two-run difference, which is ≤ 2(e + 1)[C_n𝒟_{[j]} + C^B_n𝒟^∗_j + C^θ_nθ̃^{k_s/8} + 2L^dC_Dk̄Ē₀2^{−k_s/16}] times that factor's bound. The factors V(Y) of (1.91) are interpolated alongside, V_t(Y) := V^A(Y) + t(V^B(Y) − V^A(Y)). These obey (1.68)–(1.69) whenever both runs' V(Y) do, since those bounds are bounds of the modulus and so hold for convex combinations. The derivative with respect to them replaces one factor V(Y) of (1.91), or one exponent t(Y)V(Y), by V^B(Y) − V^A(Y), which is (γ) of the Open line. The comparison holds for 𝐓′ applied to the integrand F = ΠV(Y)exp Σt(Y)V(Y) of (1.91), not only to 𝐓′1: the V(Y) depend on B and V″ localised in Y (p.377 l.7–8), and the sum of (1.71) acts on the last exponential of (1.72) (p.379 l.23–24). At fixed V_t(Y), F is common to both runs' integrands, so the exponent comparison above holds with the integrands multiplied by F and gives the same relative bound with the factor sup|F|, which is the bound of F(X′) that (1.92) uses.
- *No deep V(Y) in a young polymer.* Let every component of Λ_j^c in the polymer X′ be young: the X_{j_h} and the second-class components Y_i that X′ contains (p.389 l.27–28). Then no Y ∈ 𝐃 is deep. By LFR II p.377 l.5–7, Y ∩ Z^∼ is a union of components of Z^∼, Z = Λ_j^c being Bałaban's large-field region (LFR I p.177 l.16–17). A point y of Y at a layer ≤ k_s/8 lies in Λ_j^c, since Λ_j ⊂ Ω_j ⊂ Ω_{k_s/8+1} for j > k_s/4. So the component of Z^∼ containing y lies in Y ⊂ X′, and with it the component of Λ_j^c containing y. That component is one of the X_{j_h} (p.387 l.41–43, p.388 l.2–3) or a Y_i in X′, and its history reaches the layer of y ≤ k_s/8, so it is old, against the assumption. The polymers with an old component are those of "Fine scales and old histories" below, or, for an old second-class component, are carried on with it as deep terms (`WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §4(e), "Deep terms").
- *The non-deep V(Y).* By Cauchy's estimate on the disc of that note's §4(d)(ii), the variation of V(Y) at the two runs' configurations is ≤ 2r(Y) times the factor's bound in (1.92), with r(Y) := sup_{x∈Y}d̄(x)/ρ(x). Every point of Y lies at a layer > k_s/8, so r(Y) ≤ C_hθ̃^{k_s/8} by that note's §4(e), "Into the quotients". The bound is per factor, through the supremum r(Y), so no count of the domains at a point enters.
- *The Own Differences of the V(Y).* The V(Y) also differ by the old terms' own two-run differences, part (i) of that note's §4(d), *The functions of the configurations.* The re-summation is linear in the old terms, and each of its terms is bounded through the bound of its one old term (RG II (1.24)–(1.29), as that bullet reads them), so the re-summation of the differences, with one run's re-summing operator, is ≤ 𝒟^V_j times the factor's bound in (1.68). Here 𝒟^V_j := max_{k_s/8−1<m≤j}(D_m + S_{m−1}) + C^B_V(𝒟^∗_j + C_hθ̃^{k_s/8}), C^B_V := 2(2B₁ + C_XB₀C_w)C_B/B₀, bounds the old terms' differences relative to their bounds: the frozen 𝐄-terms at the points of Y of the layer m by D_m with their age factors, the old 𝐑-terms by the carried R-differences S_{m−1} (§8.4), and the 𝐁-terms by the bound of that note's §4(e). Every point of Y lies at a layer > k_s/8 − 1, so the indices are those that §8.7 controls. The differences are evaluated at run A's own pair of the chain, and on the complex class the 𝐄-part rests on the class bound (5.1c) there (§5), with (R-Δ^c-T) at the near domains that fail (N). The factor and the exponent of one Y contribute ≤ 2𝒟^V_j times the bound of F(X′), since |1 + t(Y)V(Y)| ≤ 2. 𝒟^V_j is an unweighted maximum, of the type of 𝒟^∗_j, and §8.7 absorbs it in the same way. The difference of the two runs' re-summing operators is part (iii) of that bullet; it needs the walk-factor inequality (W-δ) stated there, which is open.
- *The count of the factors.* With |t(Y)V(Y)| ≤ α ≤ 1, the factor and the exponent of one Y contribute ≤ 4r(Y) times the bound of F(X′). The number of domains, |𝐃| ≤ e^{|𝐃|}, is absorbed by eα in place of α in (1.92)–(1.93), which the smallness of α allows (α "can be chosen arbitrarily small for γ small enough", p.377 l.14; p.388 l.34). So the factors V(Y) add 4C_hθ̃^{k_s/8} + 2𝒟^V_j to the bracket.

The number q of factors is absorbed by q ≤ e^q, which changes the condition of p.389 l.16 to −2(1 + β₀)^{−1}p₀(g_j) + 2 + 2κ(100R_j)^d ≤ −(3/2)p₀(g_j), true for ḡ small. So |𝐑^B(X) − 𝐑^A(X)| ≤ {2(e + 1)[C_n𝒟_{[j]} + C^B_n𝒟^∗_j + C^θ_nθ̃^{k_s/8} + 2L^dC_Dk̄Ē₀2^{−k_s/16}] + 4C_hθ̃^{k_s/8} + 2𝒟^V_j}R₁′g_j^{κ₀}exp(−κd_j(X)), with R₁′ the constant of (2.31) given by Bałaban's derivation under this condition and with eα for α, uniform in j.

*Fine scales and old histories.* (LF) enters only at coarse scales. For a component renormalised at a step j ≤ k_s/4, the triangle inequality with the inductive bound (2.31) (CRE p.260 l.24), |𝐑^{(j)}(X, (𝐔, 𝐉))| ≤ g_j^{κ₀}exp(−κd_j(X)), gives |𝐑^B(X) − 𝐑^A(X)| ≤ 2g_j^{κ₀}exp(−κd_j(X)) ≤ 2R₁g_j^{κ₀}exp(−κd_j(X)), with R₁ ≥ 1, which p.263 l.29–30 allows, with g_j^{κ₀} ≤ (3bK/4 − C)^{−κ₀/2} by Lemma 2.1(i). For a component renormalised at j > k_s/4 whose history reaches a scale ≤ k_s/8, P_j(Z) ≥ j − k_s/8 ≥ k_s/8, and the improved bound e^{−C_PP_j(Z)}q̄ of each run, carried through the expansion as above, gives ≤ 2e^{−C_Pk_s/8}R₁′g_j^{κ₀}exp(−κd_j(X)). Let ν_j be these bounds per point of the generation j, together with L⁴R₁′[2(e + 1)(C^θ_nθ̃^{k_s/8} + 2L^dC_Dk̄Ē₀2^{−k_s/16}) + 4C_hθ̃^{k_s/8}](100MR_j)^dg_j^{κ₀} from the boundary terms and the factors V(Y) of the remaining components below. ν_j also contains the bound of the deep V(Y) in the young polymers, the input that *No deep V(Y) in a young polymer.* supplies; until that input is entered here, this part of ν_j is open. Then Σ_{j≤K}ν_j ≤ C_νw_K, with
- w_K := (K + 1)(θ̃^{k_s/8} + 2^{−k_s/16}) + μ_K and μ_K := K^{1−κ₀/2}ℓ_K;
- C_ν a constant of R₁, R₁′, b, C_P, C^θ_n, C_h, C_D, Ē₀, and Σ_g := sup_KΣ_{j≤K}(100MR_j)^dg_j^{κ₀}, which is finite (§8.4). C_ν = C_ν(j(C)) depends on j(C) through C_h, which `WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §4(b)–(c), "The result", states as a constant of j(C), both directly and through C^θ_n. The dependence is at most L^{Cε₀j(C)} times a polynomial in j(C), with C a constant of the exponents: the exponential factor comes from (a_k/ε)^{ε₀} ≤ L^{j(C)ε₀}L^{8nε₀} in the fine side of the large-field note's §4(d) (*The rate of (iv) and (α) in the layer.* and the ratios of (ii)), which the slack in n cannot remove, and the polynomial one from the absorption of the steps beyond the stopping scale below. At fixed j(C) the summability in K is unaffected.

There are ≤ K generations of the first kind, each ≤ 2R₁(3bK/4 − C)^{−κ₀/2}, which together are ≤ Cμ_K. The second kind sums to ≤ 2R₁′Σ_ge^{−C_Pk_s/8} ≤ 2R₁′Σ_g2^{−k_s/16}, since C_P ≥ 2 and e^{−1/4} < 2^{−1/16}. The boundary terms and the factors V(Y) sum to ≤ C(K + 1)(θ̃^{k_s/8} + 2^{−k_s/16}), since k̄ ≤ K. For the remaining components, renormalised at j > k_s/4 with the whole history at the scales > k_s/8, the bound of the preceding paragraph holds, and only there does (LF) enter, through h_n in 𝒟_{[j]}. At the steps j ∈ (k_s, K] the births still satisfy n > k_s/8, but j < 8n can fail, and only j ≤ K = k_s + j(C) < 8n + j(C) holds. The factors polynomial in the step that `WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §4(b)–(d) absorbs with j < 8n obey j^p ≤ (8n + j(C))^p ≤ (1 + j(C))^p(8n)^p, so the absorption holds there with C_h multiplied by (1 + j(C))^p, p the largest power absorbed, and the polylogarithms in K obey the same bound with K < 8n + j(C). Hence, for a component renormalised at step j,

(8.4) |𝐑^{B}(X) − 𝐑^{A}(X)| ≤ [C_R(100MR_j)^d𝒟_{[j]}g_j^{κ₀} + C^B_R(100MR_j)^d𝒟^∗_jg_j^{κ₀} + C^V_R(100MR_j)^d𝒟^V_jg_j^{κ₀} + ν_j]exp(−κd_j(X)), 𝒟_{[j]} := max_{k_s/8−1<m≤j}e^{−½C_P(j−m)}𝒟_m,

which is the inequality the Status called (G″), with C_R := 2(e+1)C_nL⁴R₁′, C^B_R := 2(e+1)C^B_nL⁴R₁′, C^V_R := 2L⁴R₁′, 𝒟^V_j of *The Own Differences of the V(Y).*, and 𝒟^∗_j the boundary terms' source of `WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §4(e), the running maximum over k_s/8 < i ≤ j of D_{i−1} + S_{i−1} + g_{i−1}²|δ_{i−1}| + e_i + max_{k_s/8<l<i}d_l. The factor (100MR_j)^d is a power of log g_j^{−2} by CRE (2.5), kept as slack, and is beaten in §8.4's sums by g_j^{κ₀}.

### 8.7 The Solution of the Recursion (Derived)

The unknowns are the class norms d_i (i = 1, …, K, with d_1 ≤ 2 from §8.2(d)), the R-differences d^R_i, and the coupling differences δ_i, on all shared scales up to the unit scale K, the stopping scale k_s = K − j(C) among them: the coupling differences δ_i = Σ_{l>i}Δβ_l run to K, and the steps beyond k, which both runs perform for the coupling prescription, obey (8.3) and (8.5) in the same way; the inequalities are (8.3), (8.5), |δ_i| ≤ C_βΣ_{l>i}d_l (δ_K = 0, §8.3), and the depth sources e_i ≤ C_eθ̃^i (§9, §10.5, and, at the large-field backgrounds of the young pairs, `WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §5(d), whose source C′_e is relative to the class norm of the dev insertions, so that C_e is enlarged by C′_e times that norm's bound), and h_i = C_hθ̃^i, through which the open input (LF) enters (§8.4), with θ̃ := max((1+θ)/2, L^{−γ_H}, L^{−(1−2ε₀)/3}, L^{−(1−ε₀)/4}, L^{−θ_V/8}) < 1, θ_V := β′/(2(4+β′)) of §5. The term L^{−θ_V/8} puts the variation groups (iv) and (α) of `WILSON_IT1_LARGE_FIELD_DEPTH_2026_10_27.md` §4(d)(ii) into the form of h_n (*The rate of (iv) and (α) in the layer.* there). This note and that one use θ̃ only through lower bounds on it and through θ̃ < 1, so its size changes only constants: C_τ, C_s, C_μ, C_∗, C″_μ, C_m, C′_m, C_S″, C_a, the threshold in K of s′_K ≤ 1 (§6.2), and the constants of the absorptions by the slack. Each is continuous in θ̃ on the compact range of θ̃ below, so they stay bounded uniformly in K and in γ small: every term of the maximum stays below a fixed number less than 1, (1+θ)/2 ≤ (3 + L^{−β′})/4 once ε^* ≤ (1 − L^{−β′})/2, a condition of γ small like (T1), and θ̃ ≥ (1 + L^{−β′})/2 stays above L^{−β′} and L^{−1/3} by a fixed margin. Put ℓ_K := (log(bK + ḡ^{−2}))^{rd+2p₀}, the polylogarithm that bounds (100MR_j)^d, p₀(g_j)², and c_j at every shared scale, μ_K := K^{1−κ₀/2}ℓ_K, w_K := (K + 1)(θ̃^{k_s/8} + 2^{−k_s/16}) + μ_K (§8.6), and m_i := iθ̃^i + w_K. Substituting (8.5) and the coupling bound into (8.3) gives d ≤ 𝒯d + s with the positive, monotone, and subadditive operator

D_{k_s} + S_{k_s} + Cε_{k_s}²|δ_{k_s}| + e_{k_s} ≤ C_a[(K+1)θ̃^{K−j(C)} + (j(C) + 2)w_K],

C_a a constant of the above and of sup_{g≤(1+β₀)γ}g²p₀(g)², which does not depend on K; it depends on j(C) through C_h and C_ν (§8.6), at most as L^{Cε₀j(C)} times a polynomial. This is the bound §7 uses, summable in K: at fixed j(C) the part (j(C) + 2)(K + 1)(θ̃^{k_s/8} + 2^{−k_s/16}) of (j(C) + 2)w_K decays exponentially in K, and μ_K = K^{1−κ₀/2}ℓ_K with κ₀ ≥ 7 (CRE p.263 l.50; the exponent κ₀ of (2.31) is Bałaban's, any κ₀ > 4 suffices for the summability of Σ_K(j(C) + 2)μ_K, and κ₀ > 6 for the Kμ_K in §6.2's s_K).

### 8.8 What §8 Gives

With R-Δ (§5), R-mark (§8.5), and (8.4), the recursion (8.3) is derived in the class norm with the constant ε_k^* of (8.2), the R-sector is bounded by (8.5), and the coupled system is solved by (8.6): the generated differences of one step are a fixed small multiple, ε^*, of the age-weighted sum of the input differences, plus the coupling term, plus the extra generation, plus the depth terms, and the carried differences at the stopping scale are geometric in the number of steps up to the term (j(C) + 2)w_K from the R-sector's fine-scale memory and the coarse histories. The content that made S a stability statement, the smallness of the step in the irrelevant directions, is thus reduced to the age decay of R-Δ^c, the evenness of the Gaussian measure, the Cauchy estimate in the fluctuation with the analyticity margin of RG I Lemma 4, and Bałaban's own marked expansion; the one condition is the threshold (T1) on the coupling at the stopping scale.

## 9. Classical Depth (Derived)

Throughout, V is regular at the stopping scale, VAR (7): |V(∂p′) − 1| < ε₁ ≤ a₁ on T_a, where ε₁ ≤ C_{ε₁}ε_k by CRE (3.12), and the stopping lattice has N_s ≥ 6R₁M₁ points per side, so that the cubes of VAR Theorem 1 fit in it. U^A := U_k(V) on T_ε and U^B := U_{k+1}(V) on T_{ε/L} are the two minimisers of VAR Theorem 1, Ũ := MU^B on T_ε, and Δ := A_{ε/L}(U^B) − A_ε(U^A). All constants depend on d, L, and the constants of VAR and PBG only; the lattice field strength of W = e^{iηA} is F_p := (1/iη²)log W(∂p), ∇ its covariant lattice difference.

The smallness conditions of §9 on ε₁ are

(9.0) ε₁ ≤ min(1, a₁, a₁′(2C₀/B₃)), C₀ε₁ ≤ 1, C_LC₀ε₁ ≤ a₀.

Here a₁ is VAR's threshold in (7), a₁′ that of VAR (113)–(121), used in §9.4, and a₀ that of PBG Theorems 3.1–3.13, used in §9.8: Theorem 3.1 (p.397) needs Mα₀ ≤ a₀, and at M = R₁M₁ and α₀ = 4C_L(B₃ + B₄(1))ε₁ this is C_LC₀ε₁ ≤ a₀, with C₀ of (9.2) and C_L the constant of the linear average in §9.8. The thresholds are constants of d, L, VAR, and PBG, so (9.0) holds at every scale j with the same ε₁, uniformly in j, k, K, and ε.

### 9.1 Two Identities

(9.1) Δ = [A_ε(Ũ) − A_ε(U^A)] − b(U^B), b(W) := A_ε(MW) − A_{ε/L}(W),

since A_{ε/L}(U^B) = A_ε(Ũ) − b(U^B). The bracket is ≥ 0: Ũ satisfies the constraint M^kŨ = M^{k+1}U^B = V and lies in VAR's ball (115) about U^A (§9.4, with B = 0), on which 𝔉 is convex with U^A its minimiser (VAR (116)–(121)). Also ℓ(U) := A_{ε/L}(ι(U)) − A_ε(U) = −b(ι(U)), since Mι = id; so both ends of (3.2) are values of b, and |Δ| ≤ |b(U^B)| + [A_ε(Ũ) − A_ε(U^A)].

### 9.2 Regularity of U^B (VAR Theorem 1 at k + 1 Steps)

VAR Theorem 1 for the (k+1)-step problem, fine lattice η = ε/L, all-small-field domains (Ω_j the whole lattice, Λ_{k+1} = T_a, L^{k+1}η = a): on every cube ◻ of side 2Ma, R₁M₁ ≤ M ≤ M(ε₁), there is a gauge u with U^{B,u⁻¹} = e^{iηA}, |A| < B₃Mε₁/a, |∇A| < B₃Mε₁/a², ‖A‖_{1,β} < B₄(β₀)Mε₁a^{−2−β} for 0 ≤ β ≤ β₀ = 1 (VAR (9)), and |∂*∂A|, |ΔA| < B₃Mε₁/a³ (VAR (10)). With M = R₁M₁ and β = 1 (the C^{1,1} bound controls every second difference of A):

(9.2) |F| ≤ C₀ε₁/a², |∇F| ≤ C₀ε₁/a³, |A| ≤ C₀ε₁/a on ◻ in the gauge u, C₀ := 4R₁M₁(B₃ + B₄(1)),

for ε₁ ≤ a₁, which (9.0) includes; the F-bounds are gauge invariant. VAR §F (p.300 l.24–25) proves (9)–(10) from criticality and membership in the regular space (6) alone, and CRE (3.12) is the same application of Theorem 1 to U_{k+1}.

### 9.3 The Blocking Artifact at a Regular Field (Taylor, First Order in ε/a)

Let W on T_{ε/L} satisfy (9.2). The averaging CMP 98 (15), Ū_c = exp[iΣ_{x∈B(c₋)}L^{−d}(1/i)log W(Γ_{c,x})W(c)^{−1}]W(c), multiplies the straight-line transport W(c) along the coarse bond c by the exponential of the block average of the logarithms of the holonomies W(Γ_{c,x})W(c)^{−1} around the closed loops Γ_{c,x} ∪ c^{−1}, each inside the block of c and spanned by at most L^{d}·L fine plaquettes. By the lattice Stokes identity with transports, (1/i)log of such a holonomy is the transported sum of (ε/L)²F over a spanning surface, up to commutators of two fluxes, O(ε⁴|F|²). For the coarse plaquette p = ⟨y, y + εe_μ, y + εe_μ + εe_ν, y + εe_ν⟩, the four bond corrections pair off: the correction of c₁ = ⟨y, y + εe_μ⟩ and that of its opposite c₃ (reversed) are block averages of fluxes through surfaces that are translates by εe_ν of one another, so their difference is at most Σ_{surface}(ε/L)²·ε|∇F| ≤ Cε³|∇F|, and likewise for c₂, c₄. Hence

(9.3) (1/iε²)log (MW)(∂p) = F̄_p + O(ε|∇F| + ε²|F|²), F̄_p := L^{−2}Σ_{p′ tiling p}F_{p′} (transported),
