and by (9.2) MW satisfies VAR (2) on T_ε with VAR's parameter ε₀ there equal to 2C₀ε₁ (plaquettes within 2C₀ε₁ε²/a², their first differences within 2C₀ε₁ε²/a³), and M^kMW = M^{k+1}W (the device of V4's sec:c3-9-1, the blocking artifact at a regular field, and of BalabanAVG Proposition 1 (51)). Now A_ε(MW) = Σ_{p⊂T_ε}ε⁴[½|F̄_p|² + O(ε|F||∇F| + ε²|F|³ + ε⁴|F|⁴)] and A_{ε/L}(W) = Σ_{p⊂T_ε}ε⁴L^{−4}Σ_{p′∈cell(p)}[½|F_{p′}|² + O(ε⁴|F|⁴)], cell(p) the L⁴ fine plaquettes parallel to p in the 4-cell of p (each fine plaquette lies in exactly one cell). All F-values entering one coarse term lie within 2ε of each other, so ||F̄_p|² − L^{−4}Σ|F_{p′}|²| ≤ 4|F|·ε|∇F|·(1 + O(ε|A|)), and with 6(a/ε)⁴N_s⁴ coarse plaquettes and (9.2):

(9.4) |b(W)| ≤ C_b (ε/a) ε₁² N_s⁴ = C_b L^{−k} ε₁² N_s⁴, C_b := 40C₀².

The same expansion gives the first variation of b at W in the direction δW = e^{i(ε/L)δ𝒜}W, δ𝒜 a Lie-algebra field on the fine bonds, in weak form. The fine side is exact: δA_{ε/L}(W)[δ𝒜] = Σ_{p′}(ε/L)⁴⟨F_{p′}, (Dδ𝒜)(p′)⟩(1 + O((ε/L)⁴|F|²)), D the covariant lattice curl; the term ⟨θ, [θ, δ𝒜]⟩ of the commutator correction vanishes identically (tr X[X, Y] = 0). The coarse side is Σ_pε⁴⟨F̄_p + O(ε|∇F|), (D̄δĀ)(p)⟩, δĀ := the linearised averaging CMP 98 (14) of δ𝒜 with transports in W; D̄δĀ is a weighted average, with weights w_{p,p′} ≥ 0, Σ_{p′}w_{p,p′} = 1, Σ_pw_{p,p′} = L^{−4}, computed in §9.8 (the Γ-pieces of the four averaged paths cancel exactly and the coarse curl is the block average of the fluxes through the L⁴ translates of an L × L square), of the fine curls (Dδ𝒜)(p′) over the fine plaquettes of the same orientation within 2ε of p, plus a transport correction: δĀ = (the untransported path average) + X with |X| ≤ Cε|A||δ𝒜|, and ⟨F̂, D̄X⟩ = ⟨D̄*F̂, X⟩ with D̄*F̂ = O(|∇F|) (F̂ := F̄ + the remainder of (9.3)), together with the commutator mismatch [Ā, ·] against [A, ·], O(ε|∇A||F||δ𝒜|): both are of the form Σ_bε⁴⟨O(ε|∇F||A| + ε|∇A||F|), δ𝒜⟩. Collecting, with F̃_{p′} := Σ_pL⁴w_{p,p′}F̄_p so that |F̃_{p′} − F_{p′}| ≤ 3ε|∇F|(1 + O(ε|A|)),

(9.5) δb(W)[δ𝒜] = Σ_{p′⊂T_{ε/L}}(ε/L)⁴⟨r₁(p′), (Dδ𝒜)(p′)⟩ + Σ_{b′⊂T_{ε/L}}(ε/L)⁴⟨r₀(b′), δ𝒜(b′)⟩, |r₁| ≤ C_rεε₁/a³, |r₀| ≤ C_rεε₁²/a⁴,

C_r := 8C₀(1 + C₀); in VAR's weighted norms |r₁|_{(−2)} ≤ C_r(ε/a)ε₁ and |r₀|_{(−3)} ≤ C_r(ε/a)ε₁². Only ∇F enters: the comparison is of the two quadratic forms at nearby points, not of the two currents.

### 9.4 The Tangential Current of Ũ Is the First Variation of b

For run A's problem (VAR (5)–(6), k steps, η = ε, Λ_k = T_a) take VAR's reference configuration U₀ := Ũ. It satisfies VAR (14) with C₁ there equal to 2C₀/B₃ (by (9.3)), at the cost of the smaller threshold a₁′(2C₀/B₃) on ε₁ in VAR (113)–(121), which (9.0) includes, and Ū₀^k = V exactly, so VAR's block datum B vanishes, 𝔄 = H₁B = 0, and by VAR Proposition 5 the minimiser is U^A = e^{iεA′*}Ũ, A′* = A₁* − HD(A₁*), QA₁* = 0, RD*A₁* = 0, with A₁* the unique fixed point in the ball (115) of T(A₁) := −𝔊J − 𝔊(δV/δA′)(A₁) (VAR (116) with B = 0), where J is the current of Ũ, ⟨δA, J⟩ := dA_ε(Ũ)[δA] (VAR (81): the linear term of 𝔉), and 𝔊 = G₁𝔓* = 𝔓G₁ (PBG (3.153)), 𝔓 the projection onto 𝒯 := {QA = 0, RD*A = 0} orthogonal for the form ⟨A, (Δ₁ + DRD* + aQ*Q)A′⟩ (VAR p.293 l.22–26). T is a contraction with constant ≤ ½ on the ball in the norm ‖·‖ := max{|·|_{(−1)}, |∇·|_{(−2)}} (VAR (120)–(121)). Hence

(9.6) ‖A₁*‖ ≤ ‖T(0)‖ + ‖T(A₁*) − T(0)‖ ≤ ‖𝔊J‖ + ½‖A₁*‖, i.e. ‖A₁*‖ ≤ 2‖𝔊J‖.

Let Q₁^{pl} := dM(U^B) be the plain linearised one-step averaging at U^B (§10.4 uses Q₁ for the double-bar one) and ℒ a linear right inverse, Q₁^{pl}ℒ = id, built as ℒg := Ig + Q₁^{pl}*(Q₁^{pl}Q₁^{pl}*)^{−1}(g − Q₁^{pl}Ig) with I the componentwise multilinear interpolation of a coarse bond field to the midpoints of the fine bonds in the cube gauge. Q₁^{pl}I reproduces the affine pure-gauge fields g = ∇̄Λ, Λ quadratic, since then Ig = ∇Λ exactly and the plain average is gauge covariant. It does not reproduce an affine field of constant curl: the Γ-pieces of the paths γ_{c,x} enclose with c a flux of order ε²|D̄g|. So, up to transport corrections, g − Q₁^{pl}Ig = 𝒦_ΓD̄g + O(ε²|∇∇g| + ε|A||g|), with 𝒦_Γ a translation-invariant local operator of norm at most Cε. The inverse of T^{pl} := Q₁^{pl}Q₁^{pl}* is bounded uniformly in the volume through a right inverse of Q₁^{pl} bounded uniformly in the volume. The norms are those of the inner products Σ_cε^d on T_ε and Σ_b(ε/L)^d on T_{ε/L}.

- *The lower bound at U = 1.* Let ν be a coarse bond field, Q^{str} the straight-tube average of (ii) of *Identities.* in §10.4, and T(1) := Q^{str}Q^{str}*. By the tents of *Regularity of the Lifts.* (§10.4), T(1) acts along each coordinate column as a convolution with spectrum in [1/3, 1], so ‖T(1)^{−1}‖ ≤ 3 and ‖Q^{str}*ν‖² = ⟨ν, T(1)ν⟩ ≤ ‖ν‖². So X₀ := Q^{str}*T(1)^{−1}ν has Q^{str}X₀ = ν and ‖X₀‖ ≤ 3‖ν‖. At U = 1, Q^{str} is the linear part Q̄₁ of the one-step double bar ((B4) of §10.1), so by (B1)–(B2) at n = 1 the field X := X₀ + Dω̃, with ω̃ equal to Θ₁X₀(y) on the block B(y), satisfies Q₁^{pl}X = ν. Θ₁X(y) is the block average over x ∈ B(y) of the sums of (ε/L)X along the contours Γ_{y,x} (the linear part of v₁ = (R_{0,y}U₁)‾ of CMP 98 (97), (105)), each of at most dL bonds inside B(y), so ‖Θ₁X₀‖ ≤ dL^{d/2+1}(ε/L)‖X₀‖. Since ω̃ is constant on the blocks, each of L^d sites of weight (ε/L)^d, its norm on T_{ε/L} equals the coarse norm of Θ₁X₀. Dω̃ lives on the L^{d−1} fine bonds crossing each block face, so ‖Dω̃‖ ≤ 2d^{1/2}L^{−1/2}(ε/L)^{−1}‖Θ₁X₀‖. Hence ‖X‖ ≤ C_{pl}‖ν‖ with C_{pl} := 3(1 + 2d^{3/2}L^{(d+1)/2}), and ‖ν‖² = ⟨Q₁^{pl}X, ν⟩ = ⟨X, Q₁^{pl}*ν⟩ ≤ C_{pl}‖ν‖‖Q₁^{pl}*ν‖. So T^{pl}(1) ≥ m_Q^{pl} := C_{pl}^{−2} in every volume.
- *The lower bound at U^B.* T^{pl} has finite range r_T^{pl}, a constant of d, since the paths γ_{c,x} of §9.8 stay within 2L fine spacings of c, and norm at most a constant M̃_Q^{pl} of L and d. It is gauge covariant, T^{pl}(U^u) = Ad(u)T^{pl}(U)Ad(u)^{−1} with u restricted to T_ε, by the covariance of the plain average (CMP 98 (11)), which gives Q₁^{pl}(U^u) = Ad(u|)Q₁^{pl}(U)Ad(u)^{−1}. So the localisation and the axial-gauge comparison of *Regularity of the Lifts.* apply with m_Q^{pl} in place of m_Q: with R_Q^{pl} fixed by C_E^{pl}/(R_Q^{pl})² ≤ m_Q^{pl}/4 and under C_ax^{pl}·d(R_Q^{pl} + 3)C₀ε₁ ≤ m_Q^{pl}/4, the spectrum of T^{pl}(U^B) lies in [m_Q^{pl}/2, M̃_Q^{pl}], with C_E^{pl} and C_ax^{pl} constants of L and d.
- *The decay.* With q^{pl} := 1 − m_Q^{pl}/(2M̃_Q^{pl}), the Neumann series (T^{pl})^{−1} = (M̃_Q^{pl})^{−1}Σ_{n≥0}(1 − T^{pl}/M̃_Q^{pl})^n, whose n-th term has norm at most (q^{pl})^n and range nr_T^{pl}, gives |(T^{pl})^{−1}(c, c′)| ≤ (2/m_Q^{pl})e^{−δ_Q^{pl}d_ε(c,c′)}, δ_Q^{pl} := (r_T^{pl})^{−1}log(1/q^{pl}), in every volume.

The correction Q₁^{pl}*(T^{pl})^{−1}(g − Q₁^{pl}Ig) is therefore exponentially localised about the support of g − Q₁^{pl}Ig, with fine gradient ≤ C(L|D̄g| + ε|∇∇g| + (ε₁/a)|g|) and fine curl 𝒦_LD̄g + O(ε|∇∇g| + (ε₁/a)|g|), where 𝒦_L := DQ₁^{pl}*(T^{pl})^{−1}𝒦_Γ at U = 1 is a translation-invariant, exponentially localised operator of norm at most a constant C_𝒦 of L and d. Let I_{pl} be the componentwise multilinear interpolation of a coarse plaquette field to the fine plaquettes, in the same gauge as I. Then D(Ig) = I_{pl}(D̄g) + O(ε|∇∇g| + (ε₁/a)|g|), by Taylor expansion of g along the paths, the last term being the commutator part of the covariant fine curl at U^B. For every δA ∈ ker Q_A (Q_A the linearised k-step averaging at Ũ), δ𝒜 := ℒδA lies in ker Q_B = ker(Q_A Q₁^{pl}) (chain rule for M^{k+1} = M^k∘M), the tangent space at U^B of {M^{k+1}W = V}, on which dA_{ε/L}(U^B) vanishes by criticality (the gauge directions included, A being gauge invariant). Therefore

(9.7) ⟨δA, J⟩ = dA_ε(MU^B)[Q₁^{pl}ℒδA] = d(A_ε∘M)(U^B)[ℒδA] = db(U^B)[ℒδA] = ⟨δA, ℒ*(D*r₁ + r₀)⟩ for all δA ∈ ker Q_A + 𝒯.

For δA ∈ 𝒯 the vanishing of dA_{ε/L}(U^B)[ℒδA] is checked separately, since 𝒯 is defined by VAR's constraint, the linear part of the double bar (§10.1). By the construction of ℒ, Q₁^{pl}ℒ = id, and the chain rule, Q_BℒδA = Q_AQ₁^{pl}ℒδA = Q_AδA for every δA, and by (B2) of §10.1, Q_AδA = −D_VΘ_kδA for δA ∈ 𝒯. With J(U^B) = Q_B*λ^B and D_V*λ^B = 0 (§10.3, *The Multipliers.*, at the scale of this section), dA_{ε/L}(U^B)[ℒδA] = ⟨Q_AδA, λ^B⟩ = −⟨Θ_kδA, D_V*λ^B⟩ = 0.

So J − ℒ*(D*r₁ + r₀) is annihilated by 𝔓* and 𝔊J = 𝔊ℒ*(D*r₁ + r₀): the residual of the fixed-point equation at Ũ is the propagator applied to the first variation of the blocking artifact.

### 9.5 The Residual (PBG (3.42), (3.44) for 𝔊)

PBG Theorem 3.13 (p.426 l.29–32) transfers Theorems 3.3, 3.10, 3.11 to 𝔊 "with the exception of the inequality in (3.42) involving the covariant Laplace operator", so (3.42) without its fourth entry and (3.43)–(3.47) hold for 𝔊, for backgrounds satisfying (3.35)–(3.36), which Ũ does by §9.8. Writing 𝔊_x := 𝔊(x, ·) and summing by parts on T_{ε/L}, (𝔊ℒ*D*r₁)(x) = Σ_{p′}(ε/L)⁴⟨D(ℒ𝔊_x)(p′), r₁(p′)⟩ with D(ℒ𝔊_x) = (I_{pl} + 𝒦_L)D̄𝔊_x + O(ε|∇∇𝔊_x| + (ε₁/a)|𝔊_x|) by §9.4. The symmetric kernel 𝔊(x, y) satisfies Σ_yε⁴|∇_y𝔊(x, y)| ≤ c_δB₀a and Σ_yε⁴|𝔊(x, y)| ≤ c_δB₀a² ((3.42), second and first entries, c_δ := sup_yΣ_{y′}e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)} the exponential factor summed over the cubes Δ(y′)), and the second-difference term enters only multiplied by ε, where the lattice inequality |∇f| ≤ (2/ε)sup|f| on T_ε gives εΣ_yε⁴|∇∇_y𝔊(x, y)| ≤ 4dΣ_yε⁴|∇_y𝔊(x, y)| ≤ 4dc_δB₀a; so, with c_𝒦 ≥ 2 a constant of L and d collecting c_δ, 4d, the factor 1 + C_𝒦 of 𝒦_L, and the constants of the O-terms of §9.4, PBG's B₀ being left unchanged,

sup|𝔊ℒ*D*r₁| ≤ c_𝒦B₀a·sup|r₁| ≤ c_𝒦B₀C_rεε₁/a², sup|𝔊ℒ*r₀| ≤ c_𝒦B₀a²sup|r₀| ≤ c_𝒦B₀C_rεε₁²/a²,

i.e. |𝔊J|_{(−1)} ≤ 2c_𝒦B₀C_r(ε/a)ε₁. For the gradient, (3.44) gives |∇𝔊∇*λ| ≤ B₀′(ε₀)Σ_{y′}e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)}(‖λ_{y′}‖^{ξ′}_{ε₀} + |λ_{y′}|) for every 0 < ε₀ ≤ 1 with λ := (I_{pl} + 𝒦_L)*r₁ (a coarse plaquette field, |λ| ≤ (1 + C_𝒦)sup|r₁|), and the Hölder seminorm of exponent ε₀ at the scale ξ′ = ε/a of a bounded function is at most 2(a/ε)^{ε₀} times its supremum, since its increments are over distances ≥ ξ′; the terms from ℒ's correction and from D(Ig) − I_{pl}D̄g have the form ∇*(a field of size O(|r₁|)) plus (ε₁/a)O(|r₁|) and are bounded by (3.44) again, with the same Hölder factor. The factor 1 + C_𝒦 of λ multiplies B₀′(ε₀), and c_𝒦 is taken to bound it together with c_δ. Hence

(9.8) ‖𝔊J‖ ≤ ρ := C_G(ε₀)(ε/a)^{1−ε₀}ε₁, C_G(ε₀) := 4c_𝒦C_r(B₀ + B₀′(ε₀)), and ‖A₁*‖ ≤ 2ρ by (9.6).

### 9.6 The Gap and the Classical Depth

By VAR (81) in VAR's variable A′, which equals A₁* here (B = 0), A_ε(Ũ) − A_ε(U^A) = 𝔉(0) − 𝔉(A₁*) = −⟨A₁*, J⟩ − ½⟨A₁*, Δ₁A₁*⟩ − V(A₁*); the terms below bound this and, with A′* = A₁* − HD(A₁*), the equivalent form VAR (74). With sup|A₁*| ≤ 2ρ/a, sup|∇A₁*| ≤ 2ρ/a², |HD(A₁*)| ≤ 4B₀C₂(2ρ)²/a and |∇HD(A₁*)| ≤ 4B₀C₂(2ρ)²/a² (VAR p.286 l.24, 42): ⟨A₁*, J⟩ = db(U^B)[ℒA₁*] by (9.7), bounded through (9.5) by N_s⁴a⁴[C_rεε₁a^{−3}·C_ℒ·4ρa^{−2} + C_rεε₁²a^{−4}·C_ℒ·2ρa^{−1}] ≤ 6C_rC_ℒN_s⁴(ε/a)ε₁ρ; |⟨HD(A₁*), J⟩| ≤ N_s⁴a⁴·16B₀C₂ρ²a^{−1}·2C₀ε₁a^{−3} = 32B₀C₂C₀N_s⁴ε₁ρ²; |⟨A′*, Δ₁A′*⟩| ≤ N_s⁴a⁴·C(4ρ/a²)² ≤ CN_s⁴ρ² (Δ₁ the second variation of the Wilson action at Ũ, a second-order lattice operator whose coefficients are polynomials in the transports of Ũ and in ε²F, bounded by (9.2) and (9.3), so that |⟨A, Δ₁A⟩| ≤ Σ_pε⁴(|DA(p)|² + C|F||A|²)); |V(A′*)| ≤ Σ_bε⁴|A′*|sup_t|δV/δA′(tA′*)| ≤ N_s⁴a⁴(4ρ/a)C₄(4ρ)²a^{−3} = 64C₄N_s⁴ρ³ (VAR Proposition 4, (97)). Hence, with (9.8),

(9.9) 0 ≤ A_ε(Ũ) − A_ε(U^A) ≤ C_gap(ε₀) (ε/a)^{2−2ε₀} ε₁² N_s⁴,

and by (9.1), (9.4), (9.9), for every 0 < ε₀ < ½ and k ≥ k₀(ε₀),

(9.10) |A_{ε/L}(U^B_{k+1}(V)) − A_ε(U^A_k(V))| ≤ 2C_b L^{−k} ε₁² N_s⁴.

This is the classical depth inequality of the Status with σ = 1. Taking into account the coupling, the term (g^A_k)^{−2}[A_{ε/L}(U^B) − A_ε(U^A)] of (3.1) is at most 2C_b g_k^{−2}L^{−k}ε₁²N_s⁴, summable in K at fixed stopping lattice since k = K − j(C).

### 9.7 The Minimiser Distance (Configuration Depth, Minimiser Half)

(9.8) is also the coarse-side configuration depth: U^A = e^{iεA′*}MU^B with sup|A′*| ≤ 4ρ/a, sup|∇A′*| ≤ 4ρ/a², ρ = C_G(ε₀)(ε/a)^{1−ε₀}ε₁, in the Landau gauge VAR (21) relative to Ũ (the axial representative of Proposition 2 differs by a gauge transformation, immaterial for the gauge-invariant frozen terms). For the fine side, the same argument applies to the one-step problem of ι(U^A): its reference configuration is U₀′ := e^{i(ε/L)ℒA′*}U^B, which satisfies VAR (14) (regular by (9.2) and the bounds on ℒA′*; MU₀′ = e^{iεA′*}Ũ·(1 + O(ρ²)) = U^A up to the second-order terms of M, within VAR's allowance |Ū₀ − V| < C₁ε₁), and its current J(U₀′) has 𝔊_BJ(U^B) = 0 (criticality of U^B) and J(U₀′) − J(U^B) = D*(DℒA′*) + O(|F||ℒA′*|) in weak form, a divergence of a field bounded by C_ℒ·4ρ/a² plus a bounded source, so by (3.42), (3.44) for 𝔊_B exactly as in §9.5, ι(U^A) = e^{i(ε/L)A″}U₀′ with ‖A″‖ ≤ C(ε₀)(a/ε)^{ε₀}ρ. Hence the stopping-scale configurations of the two runs on the common fine lattice T_{ε/L} differ by the field (ε/L)(ℒA′* + A″), with ‖ℒA′* + A″‖ ≤ C(ε₀)(ε/a)^{1−2ε₀}ε₁ in VAR's norm, i.e. supremum ≤ C(ε₀)(ε/a)^{1−2ε₀}ε₁/a and gradient ≤ C(ε₀)(ε/a)^{1−2ε₀}ε₁/a²: this is σ_k for the minimisers, with σ = 1 − 2ε₀. The one-step fixed point for ι(U^A) uses 𝔊 and 𝔓 at U₀′ rather than at U^B; the background-change term 𝔓*_{U₀′}J(U^B) is bounded by the Cauchy estimate of §10.3 with the distance (ε/L)ℒA′*, relatively O(ρ(ε/a)²ε₁), and is absorbed in C(ε₀). The same argument for the backgrounds of the step at scale j (the j-step minimiser on T_ε against the (j+1)-step minimiser on T_{ε/L} with the same block data at scale a_j := εL^j) gives (9.8) with a replaced by a_j: ρ_j := C_G(ε₀)L^{−(1−ε₀)j}ε₁ and σ_j := C(ε₀)L^{−(1−2ε₀)j}ε₁. The covariance half is §10.

### 9.8 Three Premises Supplied

*The Weights of the Linearised Averaging.* By PRT1 (1.7)–(1.8) (the same formula as CMP 98 (14)), Ā_c = L^{−(d+1)}Σ_{x∈B(c₋)}A(γ_{c,x}) with γ_{c,x} := Γ_{c₋,x} ∪ [x, x + Le_μ] ∪ Γ_{x+Le_μ,c₊} for c = ⟨c₋, c₋ + Le_μ⟩ (fine units), where Γ_{y,x} is the axial contour (1.7) from the block point y to x ∈ B(y), which depends on x − y only, and Γ_{x+Le_μ,c₊} is the contour from x + Le_μ to c₊, the reverse of Γ_{c₊,x+Le_μ} = Γ_{c₋,x} + Le_μ. For the coarse plaquette p with corner y = c₋ in the (μ, ν)-plane and a fixed x ∈ B(y), the four paths γ_{c₁,x}, γ_{c₂,x+Le_μ}, γ_{c₃,x+Le_ν}, γ_{c₄,x} (c₁ = ⟨y, y + Le_μ⟩, c₂ = ⟨y + Le_μ, y + Le_μ + Le_ν⟩, c₃ = ⟨y + Le_ν, y + Le_ν + Le_μ⟩, c₄ = ⟨y, y + Le_ν⟩) combine in the curl Ā_{c₁} + Ā_{c₂} − Ā_{c₃} − Ā_{c₄} into the closed loop γ_{c₁,x} + γ_{c₂,x+Le_μ} − γ_{c₃,x+Le_ν} − γ_{c₄,x}, in which every Γ-piece appears twice with opposite orientations (Γ_{y,x} from c₁ and c₄; Γ_{y+Le_μ,x+Le_μ} from c₁ reversed and c₂; Γ_{y+Le_ν,x+Le_ν} from c₃ and c₄ reversed; Γ_{y+Le_μ+Le_ν,x+Le_μ+Le_ν} from c₂ reversed and c₃); what remains is ∂S_x, the boundary of the L × L square S_x in the (μ, ν)-plane with corner x. Hence, abelian Stokes,

(9.11) (D̄Ā)(p) = L^{−2}L^{−d}Σ_{x∈B(y)}Σ_{p′⊂S_x}(DA)(p′) = Σ_{p′}w_{p,p′}(DA)(p′), w_{p,p′} := L^{−2−d}·#{x ∈ B(y) : p′ ⊂ S_x} ≥ 0,

so Σ_{p′}w_{p,p′} = L^{−2−d}·L^d·L² = 1 (each S_x has L² plaquettes), and for a fixed fine (μ, ν)-plaquette p′, since the pairs (p, x ∈ B(p₋)) with p of orientation (μ, ν) are in bijection with the fine points x (x determines its block point y = p₋), Σ_pw_{p,p′} = L^{−2−d}·#{x ∈ T_{ε/L} : p′ ⊂ S_x} = L^{−2−d}·L² = L^{−d}. The weights are translation invariant by construction and vanish unless p′ ⊂ S_x for some x ∈ B(y), i.e. p′ within the 2L × 2L square at y. In the non-abelian case the same identity holds for the transported curls, the transports along the cancelling Γ-pieces being the transport corrections X of §9.3.

*The Cube-Gauge Regularity (3.35)–(3.36) of Ũ = MU^B.* On a cube ◻ of side 2Ma, in the gauge u of (9.2), U^B = e^{i(ε/L)A} with |A| ≤ C₀ε₁/a, |∇A| ≤ B₃Mε₁/a², and every second difference |∇∇A| ≤ B₄(1)Mε₁/a³ (‖A‖_{1,1}). Then Ũ = e^{iεĀ} on the coarse bonds of ◻ with Ā = the linear average (9.11)'s Ā_c plus the second-order term of the nonlinear averaging CMP 98 (15), |Ā_c − L^{−(d+1)}Σ_xA(γ_{c,x})| ≤ C₂ε|A|² (VAR (44) at j = 1, the gauge u being a cube gauge for the averaged field too since the averaging is gauge covariant, CMP 98 (11)). The linear average is a weighted sum of A over fine bonds within 2L fine spacings of c, with translation-invariant weights, so its first and second coarse differences are the same weighted sums of first and second differences of A: |∇Ā| ≤ C_L(|∇A| + ε|A||∇A|) ≤ 2C_LB₃Mε₁/a², |∇∇Ā| ≤ C_L(|∇∇A| + ε|∇A|² + ε|A||∇∇A|) ≤ 2C_LB₄(1)Mε₁/a³, and |∂*∂Ā| ≤ 2d|∇∇Ā|. These are (3.35)–(3.36) with α₀ := 4C_L(B₃ + B₄(1))ε₁ in place of their O(1)Mα₀ bounds, and at M = R₁M₁, Mα₀ = C_LC₀ε₁ is below the threshold a₀ of PBG Theorem 3.1 (p.397) by (9.0), and with the plaquette bounds of (9.3); V4's sec:c3-9-1 establishes the same cube-gauge regularity of the averaged minimiser.

*The Localised Lift (derived).* For a localisation domain X the frozen difference Δ_j of (3.1) needs a lift ι_X with three properties. It is a function of U on X^∼ only, so that Δ_j depends on U on X^∼; CRE p.259 (i) has X, and the enlargement to X^∼ is the lift's. It satisfies Mι_XU = U on the coarse bonds of X^∼, and ι_X1 = 1. It is the minimal configuration of a one-step problem whose block data on X agree with those of ι, so that ι_XU^A_k is close to U^B_{k+1} on X.

*The definition.* ι_XU := U(𝐁_1(X^∼), M^·(Q_1^{s*}U)), Bałaban's construction of the local minimisers (CRE (1.2)–(1.3), and (2.16) at k-blocks), with (Q_1^{s*}U)(b) = 1 for b ⊂ B(y) and U(c) for b ∈ B(c). Here Ω₀ := X^∼, and Ω₁ is the union of the big blocks of size M₁ε of X^∼ at distance > RM₁ε/L from its complement, so VAR (1) holds at j = 0 with η = ε/L. The block data are U on Ω₁. The collar data are the fine field Q_1^{s*}U on the bonds of Λ₀, those without an endpoint in Ω₁.
- Q_1^{s*} is an exact local right inverse of the averaging. Every contour γ_{c,x} of CMP 98 (15) crosses from B(c₋) to B(c₊) on one bond of B(c), which carries U(c), and its other bonds carry 1, so M(Q_1^{s*}U) = U.
- Q_1^{s*} is covariant, Q_1^{s*}(U^v) = (Q_1^{s*}U)^{ṽ_b} with ṽ_b := v(y) on B(y), and polynomial in U, with Q_1^{s*}1 = 1.
- ι_XU depends on U on X^∼ only (CRE p.246 l.46).
- VAR Theorem 1 and Proposition 9 give the minimal orbit, its uniqueness, its regularity, and its analyticity in U in the axial gauge, since Q_1^{s*} is analytic. ι_X1 = 1.
- Mι_XU = U on the coarse bonds of X^∼. On those with an endpoint in Ω₁ this is the constraint, since VAR identifies Ω₁ with the bonds having an endpoint in it (p.277 l.18). On the others, the averaging contours run on bonds of Λ₀, which carry Q_1^{s*}U.

The free-boundary problem, the minimum of the Wilson action of the fine plaquettes in X^∼ under Mι_XU = U on X^∼, doesn't work: VAR Theorem 1 does not cover Ω₀ = Ω₁ (VAR (1), p.277).

*The tail.* As in CRE (3.6), ιU^A_k is the minimiser of the X^∼-problem at its own data M^·(ιU^A_k). The constraints of 𝐁_1(X^∼) fix the block averages of ι's problem on X^∼, so a competitor extended by ιU^A_k is admissible for ι's problem, with the same change of the action, and the minimal orbit is unique. So ι_XU^A_k = exp(i(ε/L)T)ιU^A_k, with T := ℋ(B), ℋ the solution map of VAR (179)–(181) at the background ιU^A_k, and B := (1/i)log[Q_1^{s*}U^A_k(ιU^A_k)^{−1}] on Λ₀, B = 0 on Ω₁.
- *The collar mismatch.* Take ιU^A_k in the block-axial gauge: ιU^A_k = 1 on the axial contours Γ_{y,x} of each block, a representative of its orbit with u(y) = 1, which leaves U^A_k and Q_1^{s*}U^A_k unchanged. An in-block bond b closes a loop with the contours of area ≤ dε²/L, so |ιU^A_k(b) − 1| ≤ dε(ε/L)|F|, with F the field strength of ιU^A_k. A crossing bond b ∈ B(c) differs from U^A_k(c) = M(ιU^A_k)(c) by the in-block parts of the contours and the strips between them, by ≤ C_Lε²|F|. With |F| ≤ C_Fε₁/a² on X^∼ (VAR Theorem 1 at one step, the data U^A_k regular as MU^B in (9.2)–(9.3)), B is a field of size ≤ LC_LC_F(ε/a)ε₁/a per fine bond.
- *The decay.* The derivative δℋ/δB decays as in VAR (190). Integrated along tB, t ∈ [0, 1], as in CRE (3.7), it gives |T| ≤ C_ι(ε/a)(ε₁/a)e^{−δ₀dist(·, Ω₁^c)/ε} and |∇T| ≤ C_ι(ε/a)(ε₁/(aε))e^{−δ₀dist(·, Ω₁^c)/ε}. The gradient entry of (190) carries one more factor (L^jη)^{−1} = ε^{−1} at j = 1. Here δ₀ is at most 1/16 of the rate of (190), half of it paying the sum over the collar, and C_ι := O(1)LC_LC_F. On the collar T is the mismatch itself, with fine differences ≤ 2L|T|/ε, inside the same bound.
- *The linearisation at 1.* At U = exp(ih) with h an affine block field and t → 0, the same computation bounds d(ι_X − ι)(1)h on X by C_ιe^{−δ₀dist(·, Ω₁^c)/ε} times the constant curl of h, since the mismatch dB(1)h is sourced by the curl. This is the input C″_Π of the moment (§5, *The moment of the lifted term*).

*The consequences.* On X and within 2L fine spacings of it, where the frozen term and its current slot live, dist(·, Ω₁^c) ≥ M₁εL^j − RM₁ε/L − M₁ε − 2ε, since X^∼ exceeds X by M₁-cubes of the scale a_j = εL^j (CRE p.256 l.62–63). So e^{−δ₀dist/ε} ≤ e^{δ₀(R/L + 2)M₁}e^{−δ₀M₁L^j}, which also covers the scales with L^j ≤ R/L + 2, where X can meet the collar. By the Baker–Campbell–Hausdorff formula, ι_XU^A_k = e^{i(ε/L)𝐒_X}U^B_{k+1} there, with 𝐒_X − 𝐒 = T + O((ε/L)|𝐒||T|). So 𝐒_X − 𝐒 is at most C(ε₀)ε₁e^{−δ₀M₁L^j}/a in sup and C(ε₀)ε₁e^{−δ₀M₁L^j}/a² in gradient, by ε/a ≤ 1, with C_ιe^{δ₀(R/L + 2)M₁} absorbed into C(ε₀). In the units of a_j these are C(ε₀)ε₁e^{−δ₀M₁L^j}L^{j−k} and C(ε₀)ε₁e^{−δ₀M₁L^j}L^{2(j−k)}, both ≤ C(ε₀, δ₀M₁)ε₁e^{−δ₀M₁L^j/2}L^{j−k}, C(ε₀, δ₀M₁) := 2C(ε₀)/(eδ₀M₁), since e^{−δ₀M₁L^j/2} ≤ 2/(eδ₀M₁). So σ̂_{k,j} := σ_k + C(ε₀)ρ_k + C(ε₀, δ₀M₁)ε₁e^{−δ₀M₁L^j/2} bounds the distance of §5's pair line on X and within 2L fine spacings of it. An additive tail E₀e^{−δ₀M₁L^j} per point doesn't work: counted over the L^{4(k−j)} points, it diverges at small j.

The pair-lift term G of §3 is gauge invariant. Let v be a gauge transformation on T_ε and ṽ_b := v(y) on B(y). The collar data transform as Q_1^{s*}(U^v) = (Q_1^{s*}U)^{ṽ_b}, and the block data as M(W^{ṽ_b}) = M(W)^v (CMP 98 (11)). So the X^∼-problems at U and at U^v correspond under ṽ_b, and the uniqueness of the minimal orbit (VAR Theorem 1) gives ι_X(U^v) = (ι_XU)^{ṽ}, with ṽ in the orbit of ṽ_b and ṽ| = v. With J(U^v) = Ad(v)J(U) and the covariance Q₁^{pl}(W^ṽ)* = Ad(ṽ)Q₁^{pl}(W)*Ad(v)^{−1} of §9.4, Q₁^{pl}(ι_X(U^v))*J(U^v) = Ad(ṽ)Q₁^{pl}(ι_XU)*J(U). 𝐄^B is invariant under (W, 𝐉) ↦ (W^ṽ, Ad(ṽ)𝐉) (RG I (1.19), p.263 l.34–37, under the gauge action (1.10); CRE p.259 (iii)), so G(U^v) = G(U).

## 10. Gaussian Depth (Configuration Depth, Covariance Half)

### 10.1 The Two Forms

At the step whose fluctuation field B lives on T_ℓ, ℓ := εL^{j−1} (run A's step j, run B's step j + 1), the Gaussian form of CRE (3.15) is −½⟨A_k, C*ΔCA_k⟩ with Δ = Δ^{(j)}(U) of RG I (1.4)–(1.5), and by RG I p.267 l.36–37 ("The quadratic form in B′ above is equal to −1/2⟨B′,Δ^{(k)}B′⟩, see the definition (3.156) [13] with the δ-function gauge fixing term replaced by the exponential one") this is PBG (3.156):

(10.1) Δ_U[B] = ⟨B, (Q_mG₁Q_m*)^{−1}B⟩ − a⟨B, B⟩ − 2⟨H₁D̃^{(2)}(B), J⟩,

G₁ = (Δ₁ + DRD* + aQ_m*Q_m)^{−1} on the fine lattice T_η (PBG (3.148), (3.153)), Q_m := Q_m(U) PBG's linearised m-fold averaging, the linear part of the m-fold double bar (below), H₁ = G₁Q_m*(Q_mG₁Q_m*)^{−1} (PBG (3.129)), J the current of U, R = R(U) the orthogonal projection onto Δ_UN(Q′_m), Q′_m the linear part of the averaged gauge transformation (Ru)‾^m (PBG p.394 l.1–2 for Q′_m and l.9–11 for R, CMP 98 (78)–(80)), D̃^{(2)} = C̃^{(2)} the quadratic term of the one-step nonlinear averaging of B at the block field M^mU (RG I p.267 l.28–30). Run A: η = ε, m = j − 1, U = U^A_j(V); run B: η = ε/L, m = j, U = U^B_{j+1}(V). Both are forms on fields on T_ℓ; δΔ_j := Δ^B − Δ^A. The principal part's kernel obeys PBG (3.132), |(Q_mG₁Q_m*)^{−1}(y, y′)| ≤ B₀ℓ^{−2}ℓ^{−d}e^{−δ₁d(y,y′)} at the single scale ℓ, and r_j is defined by |δΔ_j(y, y′)| ≤ r_jB₀ℓ^{−2}ℓ^{−d}e^{−δ₂d(y,y′)}, δ₂ := δ₁/4.

*The Plain and the Double-Bar Averagings.* The block fields are plain averages: M is the one-step averaging of CMP 98 (14)–(15), covariant under the restriction of gauge transformations to the coarse lattice (CMP 98 (11)). PBG's linear objects belong to the double bar Ū̄ of CMP 98 (89)–(91), which PBG uses throughout (p.392 l.44–52): Q_n(U) and C_n^{(2)}(U) are the linear and quadratic parts of the n-fold double bar of a fluctuation U₁ = e^{iηA} about U, in the units of PBG (3.14). For a configuration U on T_η put Q_n^{pl}(U) := d(M^n)(U) in the same units. The logarithms below are taken at backgrounds regular in the sense of CMP 98 (109), |V₀(∂p) − 1| < α₀ with α₀ ≤ c₃ of CMP 98 Proposition 3 (p.036), which holds at U^B and at Ũ_j by §9.2 and §10.3 (plaquettes within 2C₀ε₁) under 2C₀ε₁ ≤ c₃. Five facts are used.

- (B1) By CMP 98 (97), the relative plain average Ũ₁^n of U₁ is the double bar Ū̄₁^n transformed by the gauge transformation v_n of the lattice of spacing L^nη given there, a product of means of transported averages of U₁ and of its double bars Ū̄₁^i, i ≥ 1, with v_n = 1 at U₁ = 1. Linearising at U₁ = 1, with the gauge action of PBG (3.116), Q_n^{pl}(U) = Q_n(U) − D̄^nΘ_n, where Θ_n := d((1/i)log v_n) is linear and D̄^n is the covariant difference at the block field M^nU (PBG (3.115)).
- (B2) Hence Q_n^{pl}(U)*ν = Q_n(U)*ν for every ν with (D̄^n)*ν = 0, and Q_nX = 0 implies Q_n^{pl}X = −D̄^nΘ_nX. Q_n^{pl} is onto because Q_n is (PBG Theorem 3.12, (3.132), which give the inverse (Q_nG₁Q_n*)^{−1}): if Q_nX₀ = B, then X := X₀ + D_Uω, with ω equal to Θ_nX₀ on the coarse lattice, satisfies Q_n^{pl}X = B, by the covariance Q_n^{pl}(U)D_Uω = D̄^n(ω restricted to the coarse lattice) (CMP 98 (11), as in §10.3).
- (B3) For W near U put M̄_U(W) := Ū̄₁·MU, the one-step double bar of U₁ := WU^{−1} times the coarse background (CMP 98 (89)–(90)). By (B1) at n = 1, M(W) is the gauge transform of M̄_U(W) by v₁, so A_{Lη}(M̄_U(W)) = A_{Lη}(M(W)), and M̄_U(U) = MU.
- (B4) By CMP 98 (90)–(91), the n-fold double bar about U is the (n − 1)-fold one about MU applied to the one-step double bar about U. Linearising, Q_n(U) = Q_{n−1}(MU)Q̄₁(U), with Q̄₁(U) the linear part of the one-step double bar, Q(U) of CMP 98 (121)–(124). At second order, C_n^{(2)}(U)[X, X] = C_{n−1}^{(2)}(MU)[Q̄₁X, Q̄₁X] + Q_{n−1}(MU)C̄₁^{(2)}[X, X], with C̄₁^{(2)} the quadratic part of CMP 98 (122). Here C_n^{(2)}(U) is the quadratic Taylor coefficient in the units of PBG (3.14), so that the second-order part of the n-fold double bar of e^{iηX}U is C_n^{(2)}(U)[X, X]. The main term of Q̄₁ is the average over the L^d straight fine paths of the tube of a coarse bond, divided by L (CMP 98 (125)); the other terms of (124) vanish at U = 1.
- (B5) Covariance. PBG p.418 computes, at U₁ = 1, that the double bar of the gauge transform of U₁ by u = e^{iλ} is the coarse gauge transform of the trivial configuration by ū = (Ru)‾, which gives (3.114)–(3.115), Q_nD = D̄^nQ′_n. For general U₁, (B1) at n = 1 and the plain covariance give Ū̄(U₁^u) = Ū̄(U₁)^w with w := v₁(U₁^u)^{−1}·u|·v₁(U₁), u| the restriction of u to the coarse lattice, a coarse gauge transformation; by (B4) the same holds for the n-fold double bar. PBG (3.116) reads A^λ = A − Dλ + c(λ, A) + O(λ²) + O(λA²), with c(λ, A)(b) := ½i[λ(b₋), A(b)] − ½i[A(b), R_bλ(b₊)], and c̄ for the same term on the coarse lattice. With (1/i)log w = Q′_nλ + q_n(λ, A) + O(λ²) + O(λA²), q_n bilinear, the terms of first order in λ and in A of the covariance give

2C_n^{(2)}[Dλ, Y] = Q_nc(λ, Y) + D̄^nq_n(λ, Y) − c̄(Q′_nλ, Q_nY).

The identity is used only paired with a bond field ν with (D̄^n)*ν = 0, where the term D̄^nq_n drops out, so no bound on q_n is needed.

### 10.2 The Penalty Form and the Galerkin Identity (Derived)

G₁^{−1} is positive definite (PBG Theorem 3.11 for G₁, through Theorem 3.12) and Q_m is onto. For Q_mA = B write A = H₁B + A₀, Q_mA₀ = 0; G₁^{−1}H₁B = Q_m*(Q_mG₁Q_m*)^{−1}B is orthogonal to ker Q_m, so ⟨A, G₁^{−1}A⟩ = ⟨B, (Q_mG₁Q_m*)^{−1}B⟩ + ⟨A₀, G₁^{−1}A₀⟩, and ⟨A, aQ_m*Q_mA⟩ = a⟨B, B⟩ on the constraint set. Hence

(10.2) P_U(B) := ⟨B, (Q_mG₁Q_m*)^{−1}B⟩ − a⟨B, B⟩ = min{⟨A, (Δ₁ + DRD*)A⟩ : Q_mA = B}, attained at A = H₁B,

and, polarised, ⟨B′, P_UB⟩ = ⟨A′, (Δ₁ + DRD*)H₁B⟩ for every A′ with Q_mA′ = B′, because (Δ₁ + DRD*)H₁B = G₁^{−1}H₁B − aQ_m*B is orthogonal to ker Q_m.

### 10.3 Alignment of the Backgrounds (Cauchy)

By §9.7 at scale j, U^A_j = e^{iεA′*_j}Ũ_j with Ũ_j := MU^B_{j+1}, sup|A′*_j| ≤ 4ρ_j/a_j, sup|∇A′*_j| ≤ 4ρ_j/a_j², a_j = εL^j, in the Landau gauge VAR (21) relative to Ũ_j. In this subsection U^A := U^A_j, U^B := U^B_{j+1}, Ũ := Ũ_j, and V := M^jŨ = M^{j+1}U^B is the common block field on T_{a_j}. The block field of U^A is V^w, not V. VAR p.281 l.2–4 reaches the Landau representative from the axial one, U^A_ax with M^jU^A_ax = V, through the gauge transformation u with (R₀u)‾^j = 1, U^A = (U^A_ax)^{u^{−1}}, and this condition makes the averaged transformation trivial, not the restriction of u to T_{a_j}. So M^jU^A = V^w with w := u^{−1} restricted to T_{a_j}, by the covariance CMP 98 (11), and *The Size of w.* below gives |(1/i)log u| ≤ c_wρ_j at every point. For a configuration U on T_η with block field V_U := M^nU (n = j for η = ε, n = j + 1 for η = ε/L) let 𝒬(U) := d(M^n)(U), normalised by M^n(e^{iηX}U) = e^{ia_j(𝒬(U)X + O(X²))}V_U. The inner products are Σ_bη^d⟨·, ·⟩ on each lattice, and the current J(U) is defined by ⟨X, J(U)⟩ := dA_η(U)[X]. The chain rule gives 𝒬(U^B) = 𝒬(Ũ)Q₁^{pl}, with Q₁^{pl} := dM(U^B) as in §9.4, and 𝒬(U) = 𝒬′Q_m^{pl}(U), with Q_m^{pl} the plain linearised averaging to T_ℓ of §10.1 and 𝒬′ := dM(M^mU) the last step, a field averaging, written 𝒬′ to keep Q′ for PBG's averaging of gauge parameters. 𝒬′ is the same operator for Ũ (m = j − 1) and for U^B (m = j), since M^{j−1}Ũ = M^jU^B.

The form (10.1) depends on the current through its last term and through G₁: in PBG (3.120), (3.128), and (3.134) the terms Δ′_π and Δ^{(2)}_π are linear in J. On the diagonal J is the current of the background. The currents J(U^A) and J(Ũ) are each of size ε₁a_j^{−3}, and their difference involves second differences of A′*, which §9.7 does not bound. The alignment therefore moves the background and the current along separate paths, through an intermediate current J′ built from the multipliers of the two constrained problems.

*The Pair Class.* Let 𝒟_j be the set of pairs (𝐔, 𝐉) with 𝐔 a G^c-valued configuration on T_ε satisfying PBG (3.37) at scale j (𝐔 = e^{iεA}U₀ with U₀ real satisfying (3.35) at the scale a_j, with PBG's M and α₀, |A| < α₁/a_j, and |∇A| < α₁/a_j²) and 𝐉 a 𝔤^c-valued bond field on T_ε with |𝐉| < α_Ja_j^{−3}, α_J := Mα₀. PBG's regularity condition (3.36) on U₀ is not imposed: it enters (3.131), (3.137), and Theorem 3.12 (p.423, stated under (3.35) and (3.36)) only through the size of the current, which 𝒟_j bounds through 𝐉. 𝒟_j is imposed at the scale a_j of the common block field V, which the multipliers below need, while run A's form lives at the scale ℓ = a_j/L: at the scale ℓ the conditions of 𝒟_j are (3.37) with α₁/L in place of α₁ and the current bound with α_JL^{−3}, so PBG's bounds at the scale ℓ hold on 𝒟_j. Writing 𝐉 for J in (3.120), in the first line of (3.128), Δ_π := Δ − Δ′_π[𝐉], in (3.134), and in the last term of (10.1) defines the form Δ_{(𝐔,𝐉)}, which on the diagonal 𝐉 = J(𝐔) is Bałaban's. The second printed line of (3.128) differs from the first at a pair by ⟨A, Δ′_π[J(𝐔) − 𝐉]A⟩; the first line is the one used, and β_π ≡ 0 in §10.4 holds for it. PBG builds G, G₁, H, H₁, and (QG₁Q*)^{−1} from G₀ = (Δ + DRD* + Q*aQ)^{−1} (p.421), which does not involve the current and satisfies Theorem 3.3 under (3.35), by the series (3.130) and its analogue for G₁ (pp.421–423). The current enters these series through Δ′_π, which is linear in 𝐉, and through Δ^{(2)} = 2⟨HC^{(2)}, 𝐉⟩, in which H itself depends on 𝐉 through the series. Their bounds use the current only through its size: (3.131), as printed with the factor O(1)Mα₀, through |𝐉| ≤ α_Jℓ^{−3}, and (3.137) through |H*𝐉| ≤ O(1)α_Jℓ^{−3}, which (3.133) gives (p.422, after (3.136)). Both follow from |𝐉| < α_Ja_j^{−3}, since a_j > ℓ. So the series converge on 𝒟_j for α₀ small, uniformly on 𝒟_j. Their terms are analytic on 𝒟_j, since G₀ and its complex extension are given by Theorem 3.4 (pp.403–407), and a uniformly convergent series of analytic functions is analytic, as RG I p.261 uses. So G, G₁, H, H₁, (QG₁Q*)^{−1}, and Δ_{(𝐔,𝐉)} are analytic on 𝒟_j. Δ_{(𝐔,𝐉)} obeys the kernel bound |Δ_{(𝐔,𝐉)}(y, y′)| ≤ B₀ℓ^{−2}ℓ^{−d}e^{−δ₁d(y,y′)}, and the propagators obey (3.132), (3.133), Theorem 3.10, and Theorem 3.12 on 𝒟_j. Theorem 3.11, the positivity, is used only at real pairs, where its proof (p.416) uses only the smallness of the perturbation. PBG's (3.38) is not needed: it controls J(𝐔) for complex 𝐔, and here the current is a separate variable. RG I p.261–262 treats 𝐉 as a variable independent of 𝐔 on an analogous class U^c_j ((1.10)–(1.14)), with |𝐉| < γ₀ in (1.14), as the configurations on which the terms of (1.3) are analytic (p.261, the paragraph after (1.8), where the variable 𝐉 replaces the current (1.8)); 𝒟_j is not the same class.

*The Multipliers.* U^A_ax minimises A_ε on {M^jU = V}, and A_ε and M^j are gauge covariant (CMP 98 (11)), so U^A minimises A_ε on {M^jU = V^w}. 𝒬(U^A) is onto (Q_m^{pl} by (B2) of §10.1, 𝒬′ as Q₁^{pl} in §9.4), so J(U^A) annihilates ker 𝒬(U^A), and J(U^A) = 𝒬(U^A)*λ^A for a unique bond field λ^A on T_{a_j}; likewise J(U^B) = 𝒬(U^B)*λ^B. Differentiating the gauge covariance of the averaging (CMP 98 (11)) gives 𝒬(U)D_Uω = D_{V_U}(ω restricted to T_{a_j}) for every gauge parameter ω, D_U the covariant difference, so D_U*𝒬(U)*λ = (a_j/η)^d1_{T_{a_j}}D_{V_U}*λ. With D_U*J(U) = 0 (PBG (3.117), the gauge invariance of the action), both multipliers are covariantly divergence-free, each at its own block field:

D_{V^w}*λ^A = D_V*λ^B = 0.

Ad(w)λ^B denotes the gauge transform of λ^B by w, (Ad(w)λ^B)(c) := Ad(w(c₋))λ^B(c), which satisfies D_{V^w}*Ad(w)λ^B = 0 by the same covariance. At the axial representative U^A_ax, whose block field is V, the multiplier is the transform of λ^A by w^{−1}.

For ν := 𝒬′*λ^B on T_ℓ the same covariance of the last step gives D̄*ν = 0, D̄ the covariant difference at the common block field M^{j−1}Ũ = M^jU^B on T_ℓ. So by (B2) of §10.1, Q_m^{pl}*ν = Q_m*ν in both runs: J(U^B) = Q^B_j*ν, and the intermediate current J′_j below is Q^A_{j−1}*ν, with PBG's Q_m.

Every field X on T_ε satisfies X = Q₁^{pl}ℒX, with ℒ the plain lift of §9.4, so by the chain rule dA_ε(Ũ)[X] = dA_{ε/L}(U^B)[ℒX] + db(U^B)[ℒX] = ⟨𝒬(Ũ)X, λ^B⟩ + db(U^B)[ℒX]. With (9.5) at scale j this is the exact current identity, which extends (9.7) from ker 𝒬(Ũ) to all fields:

(10.3a) J(Ũ) = 𝒬(Ũ)*λ^B + ℒ*(D*r₁ + r₀).

Let H₀(U) := G₀Q_j(U)*(Q_j(U)G₀Q_j(U)*)^{−1}, with G₀ the operator of PBG p.421 for the j-step problem and Q_j(U) PBG's j-step double-bar averaging (§10.1). It is PBG's H of (3.126) at 𝐉 = 0, since G = G₀ there by (3.130). So (3.132)–(3.133) hold for it, with Theorem 3.3 for G₀, under (3.37) at scale j alone. For a bond y of T_{a_j}, a unit vector e of the Lie algebra, and e_y the field equal to e at y and 0 elsewhere, |H₀e_y| ≤ B₀e^{−½δ₁d(·,y)} and |∇H₀e_y| ≤ B₀a_j^{−1}e^{−½δ₁d(·,y)}, with d in units of a_j. By (B1) of §10.1 at n = j, 𝒬(U)H₀ = 1 − D_{V_U}Θ_jH₀, so for every λ with D_{V_U}*λ = 0, ⟨𝒬H₀e_y, λ⟩ = ⟨e_y, λ⟩ − ⟨Θ_jH₀e_y, D_{V_U}*λ⟩ = ⟨e_y, λ⟩. λ^A is divergence-free at V^w = V_{U^A} and λ^B at V = V_Ũ, so

⟨e, λ^A(y)⟩ = f_y(U^A), ⟨e, λ^B(y)⟩ = f_y(Ũ) − a_j^{−d}db(U^B)[ℒH₀(Ũ)e_y], f_y(U) := a_j^{−d}dA_ε(U)[H₀(U)e_y],

the second by (10.3a).

The first variation of the action is in divergence form. For 𝐔 satisfying (3.37) and a field X, |dA_η(𝐔)[X]| ≤ c_WΣ_pη^{d−2}|∂𝐔(p) − 1||(D_𝐔X)(p)|, with c_W a constant of G. Indeed, the derivative of the plaquette term ½tr(2 − 𝐔(∂p) − 𝐔(∂p)^{−1}) in the direction X is −½i tr[η²(D_𝐔X)(p)(𝐔(∂p) − 𝐔(∂p)^{−1})], which vanishes at 𝐔(∂p) = 1.

Along U(t) := e^{itεA′*}Ũ with |t| ≤ α₁/(8ρ_j), U(t) satisfies (3.37) at scale j (|tA′*| ≤ α₁/(2a_j), |∇(tA′*)| ≤ α₁/(2a_j²)). Its plaquettes satisfy |∂U(t)(p) − 1| ≤ (6/5)·2C₀ε₁ε²/a_j² + (3/2)α₁ε²/a_j² ≤ 2α₁ε²/a_j² when 5C₀ε₁ ≤ α₁ (§9.3 for Ũ). Indeed, moving the four factors Ũ(b) of ∂U(t)(p) to the right writes it as e^{iZ₁}⋯e^{iZ₄}∂Ũ(p), with Z_k the transported values of tεA′*, Σ_k|Z_k| ≤ 2α₁ε/a_j, and Σ_kZ_k the covariant curl, at most 2ε²sup|∇(tA′*)| ≤ α₁ε²/a_j². With c_B the constant of the BCH bound |(1/i)log(e^{iX}e^{iY}) − X − Y| ≤ c_B|X||Y| for |X|, |Y| ≤ r_B (complex X, Y, c_B and r_B constants of G), applied three times, e^{iZ₁}⋯e^{iZ₄} = e^{iY_p} with |Y_p − Σ_kZ_k| ≤ 2c_B(Σ_k|Z_k|)², so |Y_p| ≤ (1 + 8c_Bα₁)α₁ε²/a_j², when 4α₁ ≤ r_B and 4c_Bα₁ ≤ 1. Under 64c_Bα₁ ≤ 1 and 8α₁ ≤ 1, e^{|Y_p|} ≤ 6/5 and |Y_p|e^{|Y_p|} ≤ (3/2)α₁ε²/a_j², and |e^{iY_p}h − 1| ≤ e^{|Y_p|}|h − 1| + |Y_p|e^{|Y_p|} for h = ∂Ũ(p) gives the bound. Also |D_{U(t)}H₀(U(t))e_y| ≤ 3B₀a_j^{−1}e^{−½δ₁d(·,y)} (two differences and the transports). Let c_e := sup_ya_j^{−d}Σ_pη^de^{−½δ₁d(p,y)}, a constant of d and δ₁ for every spacing η ≤ a_j (d in units of a_j). In d = 4 each bond lies on 2(d − 1) = 6 plaquettes, so the same sum over bonds is at most the sum over plaquettes, and c_e bounds it as well. Then the analytic function t ↦ f_y(U(t)) is bounded by 6c_Wc_eB₀α₁a_j^{−3} on the disc, and Cauchy's estimate, for 16ρ_j ≤ α₁, gives |f_y(U^A) − f_y(Ũ)| ≤ (16ρ_j/α₁)·6c_Wc_eB₀α₁a_j^{−3} = 96c_Wc_eB₀ρ_ja_j^{−3}. At t = 0, with |∂Ũ − 1| ≤ 2C₀ε₁ε²/a_j², the same computation gives |f_y(Ũ)| ≤ 6c_Wc_eC₀B₀ε₁a_j^{−3}.

For the artifact term, ℒ is §9.4's plain lift, built from Q₁^{pl}. Its sup and gradient bounds, with the exponential decay of (Q₁^{pl}Q₁^{pl}*)^{−1} at U^B, come from the argument of *Regularity of the Lifts.* after (10.5′), run at scale a_j with §9.4's m_Q^{pl}, M̃_Q^{pl}, and δ_Q^{pl} in place of m_Q, M̃_Q, and δ_Q, under §9.4's condition C_ax^{pl}·d(R_Q^{pl} + 3)C₀ε₁ ≤ m_Q^{pl}/4 and δ₁ ≤ δ_Q^{pl}, and with §9.4's bound |g − Q₁^{pl}Ig| ≤ C(ε|D̄g| + ε²|∇∇g| + ε|A||g|) in place of the Taylor bullet; only the sup and gradient bounds are used, not the oscillation bound. They give |ℒH₀(Ũ)e_y| ≤ C_ℒB₀e^{−½δ₁d(·,y)} and |DℒH₀(Ũ)e_y| ≤ 3C_ℒB₀a_j^{−1}e^{−½δ₁d(·,y)}, with C_ℒ the constant of §9.4's lift, a constant of L, d, G, δ₁, and B₀(γ)/B₀, uniform in k, K, ε, and the volume. So (9.5) at scale j gives a_j^{−d}|db(U^B)[ℒH₀(Ũ)e_y]| ≤ 4c_eC_rC_ℒB₀(ε/a_j)ε₁a_j^{−3}. Hence, e being arbitrary,

a_j³|λ^A − λ^B| ≤ x_λ(ρ_j + (ε/a_j)ε₁), a_j³|λ^B| ≤ B_λε₁, x_λ := c_eB₀(96c_W + 4C_rC_ℒ), B_λ := c_eB₀(6c_WC₀ + 4C_rC_ℒ).

*The Adjoint Averaging.* For a real configuration W on T_η put κ_W := sup_p|∂W(p) − 1|, W̄ := MW on T_{Lη}, and Q₁^{pl}(W) := dM(W). For every bond field ν on T_{Lη}:

- (i) D_W*Q₁^{pl}(W)*ν = L^d1_{T_{Lη}}D_{W̄}*ν;
- (ii) sup|Q₁^{pl}(W)*ν| ≤ (1 + c_QL^{d+1}κ_W)sup|ν| + L^{d−1}·Lη sup|D_{W̄}*ν|, with c_Q := 9(d + 1)c_T.

(i) is the one-step case of the identity 𝒬(U)D_Uω = D_V(ω restricted to T_{a_j}) above.

For (ii), CMP 98 (14) and PRT1 (1.7)–(1.8) give, at W = 1, Ā_c = L^{−(d+1)}Σ_{x∈B(c₋)}A(γ_{c,x}) with the paths γ_{c,x} of §9.8. So (Q₁^{pl}*ν)(b) = L^{−1}Σ±ν(c), summed over the pairs (c, x) with b on γ_{c,x}, with the orientation signs. For real W the linearisation of CMP 98 (15) carries each ν(c) to b by the transports of W along the paths. It also composes ν(c) with dlog at the loop holonomy h_{c,x} := W(γ_{c,x})W(c)^{−1}, with dexp at Y_c := L^{−d}Σ_x(1/i)log h_{c,x}, and with Ad(e^{iY_c}). The variations of the outer factor W(c) of (15) and of the factors W(c)^{−1} inside the logarithms cancel at W = 1. In general they leave one term of weight L^{d−1} on the bonds of c, with an operator made of these factors and Ad(h_{c,x}). The loop γ_{c,x} ∪ c^{−1} bounds the surface swept by Γ_{c₋,x} under the translation by Lηe_μ, at most dL² plaquettes. So |h_{c,x} − 1| and |Y_c| are at most 2dL²κ_W, and each of the three operators dlog, dexp, and Ad(e^{iY_c}), and also Ad(h_{c,x}), is within c_TL²κ_W of the identity, with c_T a constant of d and G. The column of a fine bond b then has three groups:

- Straight segments. b = ⟨z, z + ηe_μ⟩ lies on [x, x + Lηe_μ] for exactly the L points x = z − sηe_μ, s = 0, …, L − 1, each of which determines its coarse bond. These terms have weight L^{−1} and isometric transports, so their sum is at most sup|ν|.
- Contours. Let b lie in the block B(y) and on Γ_{y,x} for N_b ≤ L^d points x ∈ B(y). Then b lies on γ_{c,x} for the bonds c leaving y and, reversed, for the bonds c entering y (x + Lηe_μ ranging over B(y)), N_b times for each of the 2d bonds at y. The contours run coordinate by coordinate (CMP 98 p.024), so the part of Γ_{y,x} from y to b is the contour from y to the nearer endpoint of b, and the transport P_b from y to b is common to all these terms. Their sum is L^{−1}N_bP_b* applied to the sum over the 2d bonds at y of ±ν carried to y. Once the transports W(c) of the entering bonds are replaced by W̄(c) = e^{iY_c}W(c), this sum is −LηD*_{W̄}ν(y). The group is therefore at most L^{d−1}·Lη|D*_{W̄}ν(y)| plus deviations.
- Deviations. Each of the at most L + 2dL^d terms of weight L^{−1} changes by at most 4c_TL²κ_W times its weight when the three operators dlog, dexp, and Ad(e^{iY_c}) are replaced by the identity (c_TL²κ_W ≤ ¼). The term of weight L^{d−1} has an operator, made of these three and Ad(h_{c,x}), of norm at most 5c_TL²κ_W. In all the deviations are at most (8d + 9)c_TL^{d+1}κ_W sup|ν| ≤ c_QL^{d+1}κ_W sup|ν|.

*(a) The Composite.* Let U be a real configuration on T_η with block field V_U = M^nU, a_j = L^nη, and κ_U ≤ 2C₀ε₁η²/a_j², and put W_i := M^iU on T_{L^iη}. CMP 98 Proposition 2 ((52), (54)), applied to the i-fold averages, gives κ_{W_i} ≤ 4C₀ε₁L^{2i}η²/a_j² when 2C₀ε₁ ≤ c₂. For μ on T_{a_j} with D_{V_U}*μ = 0 put μ_n := μ and μ_i := Q₁^{pl}(W_i)*μ_{i+1}, so that 𝒬(U)*μ = μ_0 by the chain rule. By (i) every μ_i is divergence-free, and by (ii) sup|μ_i| ≤ (1 + 4c_QL^{d+1}C₀ε₁L^{2i}η²/a_j²)sup|μ_{i+1}|. Since Σ_{i<n}L^{2i}η²/a_j² ≤ (4/3)L^{−2}, the product of these factors is at most exp((16/3)c_QL^{d−1}C₀ε₁) ≤ 2 under

(AQ) 8c_QL^{d−1}C₀ε₁ ≤ 1,

and

sup|𝒬(U)*μ| ≤ 2 sup|μ| for D_{V_U}*μ = 0, with sup|μ_i| ≤ 2 sup|μ| at every level.

*(b) The Difference.* Let U and U′ = e^{iηA}U both satisfy the hypotheses of (a), with block fields V_U and V_{U′} = V_U^w for a gauge transformation w on T_{a_j}, and put W′_i := M^iU′. For μ with D_{V_U}*μ = 0 put μ′_n := Ad(w)μ, which is divergence-free at V_{U′}, and μ′_i := Q₁^{pl}(W′_i)*μ′_{i+1}, so that 𝒬(U′)*Ad(w)μ = μ′_0. By CMP 98 (159)–(163) (p.042), applied to the i-fold averages, i ≤ n, |W′_i(b)W_i(b)^{−1} − 1| ≤ c_vL^iη sup|A| on every bond of T_{L^iη}, with c_v a constant of d (the O(1) of (162)–(163)), when a_j sup|A| is below the α₁ of CMP 98 (158). Let δ_i := sup|μ′_i − μ_i|, so that δ_n ≤ sup|Ad(w) − 1| sup|μ| and

μ′_i − μ_i = Q₁^{pl}(W′_i)*(μ′_{i+1} − μ_{i+1}) + [Q₁^{pl}(W′_i) − Q₁^{pl}(W_i)]*μ_{i+1}.

The divergence of the difference field is the transport mismatch alone, D*_{W′_{i+1}}(μ′_{i+1} − μ_{i+1}) = −(D*_{W′_{i+1}} − D*_{W_{i+1}})μ_{i+1}, at most 4dc_v sup|A| sup|μ_{i+1}|. So by (ii) the first term is at most (1 + c_QL^{d+1}κ_{W′_i})δ_{i+1} + 4dc_vL^{d−1}L^{i+1}η sup|A| sup|μ_{i+1}|, and the defect does not accumulate over the levels. The second term changes the transports along paths of at most (2d + 1)L bonds in each term of the column, and with them the three operators dlog, dexp, and Ad(e^{iY_c}) and the operator Ad(h_{c,x}). So it is at most c_DL^{d−1}c_vL^{i+1}η sup|A| sup|μ_{i+1}|, with c_D a constant of d and G. With sup|μ_{i+1}| ≤ 2 sup|μ|, the product of the factors at most 2, and Σ_{i<n}L^{i+1}η ≤ 2a_j,

sup|𝒬(U′)*Ad(w)μ − 𝒬(U)*μ| ≤ (C′_Qa_j sup|A| + 2 sup|Ad(w) − 1|)sup|μ| for D_{V_U}*μ = 0, C′_Q := 8(c_D + 4d)c_vL^{d−1}.

For w = 1, the case M^nU′ = M^nU, the last term is absent.

*The Size of w.* Write U^A = U₁Ũ bondwise, U₁(b) = e^{iεA′*(b)}, as in CMP 98 §C with U₀ = Ũ. By CMP 98 (86)–(87) and (99), the axial transformation u has u(y) = v_j(y)^{−1} at the points y of T_{a_j}, with v_j(y) = ∏_{i=0}^{j−1}(\overline{\bar R^i_{0,y}\bar{\bar U}^i_1}) of (97), and (97) with (92) gives M^jU^A = (M^jU^A_ax)^{v_j}, so w = v_j. By (105) each factor is the exponential of the block mean over x ∈ B(y) of (1/i)log(\bar R^i_{0,y}\bar{\bar U}^i_1)(Γ_{y,x}), the transport of the i-fold double-bar average along a contour of at most dL bonds of T_{L^iε}. The norm on 𝔤 is Ad-invariant, so the conjugations R are isometric, and |(1/i)log(g₁⋯g_m)| ≤ Σ_k|(1/i)log g_k| whenever the right side is below the injectivity radius r_inj of G at 1, by the triangle inequality for the bi-invariant distance. Let θ_i := sup_b|(1/i)log(\bar{\bar U}^i_1)_b|, so θ_0 = ε sup|A′*| ≤ 4L^{−j}ρ_j. CMP 98 Proposition 3 ((122)–(126)) for the step from i to i + 1, with V₀ = M^iŨ, whose plaquettes are at most α₀^{(i)} := 4C₀ε₁L^{2(i−j)} by (a), gives θ_{i+1} ≤ Lθ_i(1 + c₁₂₄L²α₀^{(i)} + C₁Lθ_i) when α₀^{(i)} ≤ c₃ and θ_i ≤ c₃: the linear part (124) is at most (1 + c₁₂₄L²α₀^{(i)})θ_i by (126), the quadratic part (123) at most C₁L²θ_i², and the logarithm is L times the linear part plus the quadratic part by (122). Since Σ_{i<j}L²α₀^{(i)} ≤ (16/3)C₀ε₁ and, inductively, Σ_{i<j}Lθ_i ≤ 16ρ_j, the product of the factors is at most exp((16/3)c₁₂₄C₀ε₁ + 16C₁ρ_j) ≤ 2 under c₁₂₄C₀ε₁ ≤ 1/16 and C₁ρ_j ≤ 1/64, and θ_i ≤ 8L^{i−j}ρ_j. So each factor of v_j has |(1/i)log| ≤ dLθ_i, and |(1/i)log w| ≤ Σ_{i<j}dLθ_i ≤ 16dρ_j. By (106), u(x) at a point x of T_ε is a product over i < j of conjugated brackets, each the inverse of the i-th factor of (97) at x_{i+1} times the transport of \bar{\bar U}^i_1 along the contour Γ_{x_{i+1},x_i} of at most dL bonds, so each has |(1/i)log| ≤ 2dLθ_i. Hence

sup_{T_ε}|(1/i)log u| ≤ c_wρ_j, sup_{T_{a_j}}|(1/i)log w| ≤ c_wρ_j, c_w := 32d,

under 4C₀ε₁ ≤ c₃, 8ρ_j ≤ c₃, and c_wρ_j < r_inj. Ad(w) = e^{i ad_Y}, Y := (1/i)log w, is orthogonal on 𝔤, so |Ad(w) − 1| ≤ |ad_Y| ≤ c_brc_wρ_j, with c_br := sup|[X, X′]|/(|X||X′|).

*The Intermediate Current.* Let J′ = J′_j := 𝒬(Ũ)*λ^B and

δ𝐉 := J(U^A) − J′ = 𝒬(U^A)*(λ^A − Ad(w)λ^B) + [𝒬(U^A)*Ad(w)λ^B − 𝒬(Ũ)*λ^B].

Ũ satisfies the hypotheses of (a) by §9.3, U^A and U^B by (9.2) at scale j, and U^A = e^{iεA′*}Ũ with a_j sup|A′*| ≤ 4ρ_j. λ^A − Ad(w)λ^B is divergence-free at V^w and λ^B at V, and a_j³|λ^A − Ad(w)λ^B| ≤ a_j³|λ^A − λ^B| + |Ad(w) − 1|a_j³|λ^B| ≤ (x_λ + c_brc_wB_λ)(ρ_j + (ε/a_j)ε₁). So (a) at U^A, (b) with U = Ũ and U′ = U^A, and the multiplier bounds give, with ε₁ ≤ 1,

a_j³|δ𝐉| ≤ x_J(ρ_j + (ε/a_j)ε₁), x_J := 2x_λ + (4C′_Q + 4c_brc_w)B_λ, a_j³|J′| ≤ 2B_λε₁, a_j³|J(U^B)| ≤ 2B_λε₁.

J′ is real, and it differs from J(Ũ) by the artifact term of (10.3a). J′ and J(U^B) = 𝒬(U^B)*λ^B are the lifts of the same multiplier λ^B through the two runs' averagings, which is what §10.4 uses.

*The Alignment Estimate.* The path (𝐔(t), 𝐉(t)) := (e^{itεA′*}Ũ, J′ + tδ𝐉), t ∈ ℂ, joins the pair (Ũ, J′) at t = 0 to the diagonal point (U^A, J(U^A)) at t = 1. Let R := min(α₁/(8ρ_j), α_J/(4x_J(ρ_j + (ε/a_j)ε₁))). For |t| ≤ R, 𝐔(t) satisfies (3.37) at scale j as above, and |𝐉(t)| ≤ (2B_λε₁ + ¼α_J)a_j^{−3} < α_Ja_j^{−3} when 4B_λε₁ ≤ α_J. So the path stays in 𝒟_j, and t ↦ Δ_{(𝐔(t),𝐉(t))}(y, y′) is analytic and bounded by B₀ℓ^{−2}ℓ^{−d}e^{−δ₁d(y,y′)}. Cauchy's estimate with R ≥ 2 gives

(10.3) |Δ^A_{U^A_j}(y, y′) − Δ^A_{(Ũ_j, J′_j)}(y, y′)| ≤ [16ρ_j/α₁ + 8x_J(ρ_j + (ε/a_j)ε₁)/α_J]·B₀ℓ^{−2}ℓ^{−d}e^{−δ₁d(y,y′)},

both forms being run A's (fine lattice T_ε, m = j − 1), the left one on the diagonal. The conditions used are:

- 16ρ_j ≤ α₁ and 5C₀ε₁ ≤ α₁, and, for PBG's α₁, 64c_Bα₁ ≤ 1, 8α₁ ≤ 1, and 4α₁ ≤ r_B;
- 8x_J(ρ_j + (ε/a_j)ε₁) ≤ α_J and 4B_λε₁ ≤ α_J;
- (AQ), 2C₀ε₁ ≤ c₂ of CMP 98 Proposition 2, and 4C₀ε₁ ≤ c₃ of CMP 98 Proposition 3, which the logarithms of §10.1 and *The Size of w.* need;
- 4ρ_j below the α₁ of CMP 98 (158);
- c₁₂₄C₀ε₁ ≤ 1/16, C₁ρ_j ≤ 1/64, 8ρ_j ≤ c₃, and c_wρ_j < r_inj (*The Size of w.*);
- C_ax^{pl}·d(R_Q^{pl} + 3)C₀ε₁ ≤ m_Q^{pl}/4 and δ₁ ≤ δ_Q^{pl} (§9.4, for the artifact term).

By ρ_j ≤ C_G(ε₀)ε₁ they are smallness conditions on ε₁, uniform in j, besides the conditions on PBG's α₁ in the first item. For an analytic functional of the background alone the path 𝐔(t) suffices, with the factor 16ρ_j/α₁; this is how §9.7's background-change term and §10.5 use the estimate.

### 10.4 The Aligned Comparison: Chain Rule, Bilinear Defect, Oscillation (Derived)

Compare Δ^A at the pair (Ũ_j, J′_j) of §10.3 (T_ε, m = j − 1) with Δ^B at U^B := U^B_{j+1} on its diagonal (T_{ε/L}, m = j). §10.2 holds at the pair: G₁ and H₁ are built as on PBG p.421 l.12–17, from the form (3.127) through (3.128)–(3.129), with J′_j written for J; G₁^{−1} at (Ũ_j, J′_j) is real, and PBG's proof of Theorems 3.11–3.12 for G₁ uses the current only through its size, |J′_j| ≤ 2B_λε₁a_j^{−3}. By (B4) of §10.1, Q^B_j = Q^A_{j−1}Q₁ exactly, where from here on Q₁ := Q̄₁(U^B) is the linear part of the one-step double bar at U^B, in place of the plain Q₁^{pl} = dM(U^B) of §9.4 and §10.3. ℒ is the lift built from this Q₁ as §9.4's lift is built from Q₁^{pl}, ℒg := Ig + Q₁*(Q₁Q₁*)^{−1}(g − Q₁Ig), with the interpolation I of *Regularity of the Lifts.* after (10.5′), where it is bounded. So Q₁ maps {Q^B_j𝒜 = B} into {Q^A_{j−1}A = B} and ℒ maps back. The plain Q₁^{pl} enters below only through the artifact current δJ := J^A(Ũ_j) − J′_j of (10.3a), and δJ only through the crude bound of *The Constraint Part of β.* After alignment the block fields M^{j−1}Ũ_j = M^jU^B coincide, so D̃^{(2)} is the same polynomial in both runs. Let G_A^{−1} := Δ₁^A + D_AR_AD_A* on T_ε, with Δ₁^A := Δ₁ of VAR (79) at (Ũ_j, J′_j), and G_B^{−1} := Δ₁^B + D_BR_BD_B* on T_{ε/L}, with Δ₁^B at (U^B, J^B(U^B)) (the a-terms contribute a⟨B′, B⟩ to both forms and cancel), and the bilinear defect

(10.4) β(𝒜′, 𝒜) := ⟨Q₁𝒜′, G_A^{−1}Q₁𝒜⟩ − ⟨𝒜′, G_B^{−1}𝒜⟩ for fields 𝒜, 𝒜′ on T_{ε/L}.

With 𝒜^B := H₁^BB, 𝒜′^B := H₁^BB′, 𝒜^A := H₁^AB, 𝒜′^A := H₁^AB′, the polarised identity of 10.2 gives ⟨B′, P_AB⟩ = ⟨Q₁𝒜′^B, G_A^{−1}𝒜^A⟩ (Q₁𝒜′^B is admissible for run A) and ⟨B′, P_BB⟩ = ⟨𝒜′^B, G_B^{−1}𝒜^B⟩, whence, adding and subtracting ⟨Q₁𝒜′^B, G_A^{−1}Q₁𝒜^B⟩ and using ⟨𝒜′^A, G_A^{−1}E⟩ = 0 for E ∈ ker Q^A_{j−1},

(10.5) ⟨B′, (P_B − P_A)B⟩ = −β(𝒜′^B, 𝒜^B) + ⟨E′, G_A^{−1}E⟩, E := Q₁𝒜^B − 𝒜^A ∈ ker Q^A_{j−1}, E′ := Q₁𝒜′^B − 𝒜′^A.

E is characterised by ⟨Q₁ℱ, G_A^{−1}E⟩ = β(ℱ, 𝒜^B) for every ℱ ∈ ker Q^B_j (both ⟨Q₁ℱ, G_A^{−1}𝒜^A⟩ and ⟨ℱ, G_B^{−1}𝒜^B⟩ vanish). Let Q := Q^A_{j−1}, G₁ := G₁^A, and H₁ := H₁^A at run A's pair, and let ℒ*β(·, 𝒜^B) be the field on T_ε that represents X ↦ β(ℒX, 𝒜^B). Every X ∈ ker Q is Q₁ℒX with ℒX ∈ ker Q^B_j, since Q^B_jℒ = QQ₁ℒ = Q, and ⟨X, G₁^{−1}E⟩ = ⟨X, G_A^{−1}E⟩ for X ∈ ker Q, the term a⟨QX, QE⟩ vanishing. So G₁^{−1}E − ℒ*β(·, 𝒜^B) = Q*κ for a field κ on T_ℓ, and QE = 0 gives κ = −(QG₁Q*)^{−1}QG₁ℒ*β(·, 𝒜^B). Hence

E = G₁^Qℒ*β(·, 𝒜^B), G₁^Q := G₁ − G₁Q*(QG₁Q*)^{−1}QG₁ = (I − H₁Q)G₁,

the Green operator on ker Q at run A's pair. The identification uses only the invertibility of G₁ and QG₁Q* at the pair and H₁ = G₁Q*(QG₁Q*)^{−1}. It does not use PBG's 𝔊 of (3.153), the operator of §9.4, which rests on the identities (3.151)–(3.152); these need the N(Q′_A)-invariance that is not available at the pair (*The Landau Condition at the Pair.* below), and R_AD_A*E = 0 is not shown. The bounds of G₁^Q come from those at the pair (§10.3, *The Pair Class.*): G₁ obeys (3.42) without its Laplacian entry (Theorem 3.3 for G₁, through Theorem 3.12), (QG₁Q*)^{−1} obeys (3.132) with G₁, and H₁ obeys (3.133). Q is local, its kernel Q(y, ·) vanishing outside B^{j−1}(y₋) ∪ B^{j−1}(y₊) (CMP 98 p.031, after (91)), with sup norm at most 2 by CMP 98 (126) at each of the j − 1 steps under 16c₁₂₄C₀ε₁ ≤ 1. So H₁QG₁, and with it G₁^Q, obeys the bounds of (3.42) for G₁, entry by entry, with the decay rate ½δ₁ and with B₀ replaced by B₀ + 2c_δ′B₀². §4(d) of the large-field note uses E with G₁^Q and the functional form (10.6c) below. On the diagonal B′ = B the second term of (10.5) is ⟨E, G_A^{−1}E⟩ ≥ 0, since G_A^{−1} is positive on ker Q^A_{j−1} by (10.2); the Assembly below uses only this sign.

*Regularity of H₁.* PBG Theorem 3.12 (p.423 l.33–36) gives (3.133) and Theorem 3.10 for H₁ on both lattices: for B supported at y′ with |B(y′)| = 1, 𝒜 = H₁B satisfies |𝒜(x)| ≤ B₀e^{−½δ₁d(x,y′)}, |∇𝒜(x)| ≤ B₀ℓ^{−1}e^{−½δ₁d(x,y′)}, and ‖ζ∇𝒜‖_{γ_H} ≤ B₀(γ_H)ℓ^{−1−γ_H}e^{−½δ₁d(y,y′)} on each block Δ̃(y), 0 < γ_H < 1 (the Hölder exponent, written γ_H to keep γ := sup_Kg_K), so the oscillation of ∇𝒜 over any fine 4-cell of side 4ε, in the ℓ^∞ distance, is at most ω(x) := 4^{γ_H}B₀(γ_H)(ε/ℓ)^{γ_H}ℓ^{−1}e^{−½δ₁d(x,y′)}, and those of the curl D𝒜 and of the divergence D*𝒜, sums of 2 and of d differences, are at most 2ω(x) and dω(x). At j = 1, where ε = ℓ, a 4-cell need not lie in one block Δ̃(y); there the oscillation is at most twice the sup of |∇𝒜| over the cell, at most 2e^{1/5}𝔰(x) ≤ 3𝔰(x) for δ₁ ≤ 1/(10d) (the cell has ℓ¹ diameter at most 4dε = 4dℓ), and this is at most ω(x) once B₀(γ_H) is taken at least 3B₀, which changes only B₀(γ_H). The distance in the Hölder quotients is PBG's |x − x′| of (3.40), with the transports along a shortest contour Γ_{x,x′}. The oscillations below are taken over sets of Euclidean diameter at most ε(d + 6)^{1/2}, so the factor 4^{γ_H} holds whether that distance is the ℓ^∞ or the Euclidean one, since (d + 6)^{1/2} ≤ 4 for d ≤ 10, which covers d = 4. For such 𝒜 let 𝔰(x) := B₀ℓ^{−1}e^{−½δ₁d(x,y′)}, so that |𝒜| ≤ ℓ𝔰, |∇𝒜| ≤ 𝔰, and ω = h_H(ε/ℓ)^{γ_H}𝔰 with h_H := 4^{γ_H}B₀(γ_H)/B₀.

*Transports.* A gauge-covariant identity at a coarse point y is evaluated in the axial gauge of U^B about y. There every fine bond b within ℓ¹-distance 4dε of y has |U^B(b) − 1| ≤ 4dε(ε/L)|F|: b closes a lattice loop bounding at most 4dL fine plaquettes, each within (ε/L)²|F| of 1, and the holonomy of a loop is an ordered product of conjugated plaquette holonomies. The paths in Q₁ (Γ_{c₋,x} followed by a straight path of the tube, at most 3dL fine bonds), in Q′₁ (at most dL), and in the coarse links of Ũ_j (averages over the paths γ_{c,x} of the plain average, at most 3dL fine bonds, CMP 98 (15) adding a second-order term) therefore carry transports whose adjoint action is within δ_T := 32d²ε²|F| of 1. In a fine divergence summed over a block (Q′₁D_B*), summation by parts leaves each interior fine bond b = ⟨x, x + (ε/L)e_μ⟩ with the holonomy of the thin loop Γ_{y,x}·b·Γ_{y,x+(ε/L)e_μ}^{−1}, which bounds at most dL plaquettes, so its adjoint action is within 2dε(ε/L)|F| of 1; the face bonds carry transports of the first kind. Besides the transports, Q₁ = Q̄₁(U^B) contains the non-main terms of CMP 98 (124). Their operators, g(−i ad_Y)g^{−1}(−i ad_{Y_x}) − 1 (in the second and third terms), g(−i ad_Y)g^{−1}(i ad_{Y_x})e^{−i ad_Y} − 1, and e^{i ad_Y} − g(−i ad_Y)Σ_xL^{−d}g^{−1}(i ad_{Y_x}), vanish at Y = Y_x = 0 and are O(L²α₀) (CMP 98 p.036, after (124)). Here α₀ is the fine plaquette bound (ε/L)²|F|, so L²α₀ = ε²|F|, and by (126) these terms change Q₁𝒜 by at most c₁₂₄ε²|F| sup|𝒜| over the paths, with c₁₂₄ the O(1) of (126), a constant of d and G. An identity exact at U^B = 1 is built from the following pieces:

- at most two averages, each with sup norm at most 3d times that of the field;
- coarse differences, 2d terms at spacing ε, each with transports within δ_T of 1, contributing in all at most 2dδ_T/ε = 64d³ε|F| per unit of the field differenced;
- at most one block divergence, contributing at most (2d² + 32d³)ε|F| per unit: the thin loops of the interior bonds give 2d², and only the entering faces deviate from the convention of (iii), since at the exiting faces the field is carried along Γ_{y,b₋} as in Q′₁, so their dL^{d−1} bonds, of weight L^{−d}(L/ε) each with transports within δ_T, give dδ_T/ε = 32d³ε|F|;
- the non-main terms of (124), contributing at most 2dc₁₂₄ε|F| per unit once differenced.

The worst case is a coarse divergence of an average, as in e_f below: the averaged field's transports give 3d·64d³ = 192d⁴, the average's own transports and its non-main terms, differenced, give 64d³ + 2dc₁₂₄, and the block divergence gives 2d² + 32d³, in all at most 192d⁴ + 64d³ + 32d³ + 2d² + 2dc₁₂₄ ≤ 300d⁴ + 2dc₁₂₄ per unit; every other combination of at most these pieces is smaller. Each identity therefore holds up to a correction bounded by C_trε|F| times the sup over the cell of the field it acts on and of ε times its gradient, with C_tr := 1000d⁴ + 2dc₁₂₄. Let ν_F := εℓ sup|F| ≤ C₀ε₁(ε/ℓ)L^{−2}, by |F| ≤ C₀ε₁/a_j² ((9.2) at scale j) and a_j = Lℓ.

*The Parts of β.* By VAR (79), Δ₁ = Δ_π − Δ^{(2)}_π. By PBG (3.120), (3.128), and (3.134), for fields A′, A at a pair (𝐔, 𝐉),

⟨A′, Δ₁A⟩ = ⟨A′, ΔA⟩ − 2⟨HC^{(2)}[A′, A], 𝐉⟩ − π(A′, A).

Here Δ is the plain second variation of the action (PBG (3.12), (3.109)), and C^{(2)} is the second-order term of the m-fold averaging in the units of Q_mA (PBG p.421, after (3.127)), polarised. The remainder π(A′, A) := ⟨A′, Δ′_π[𝐉]A⟩ + 2⟨H(C^{(2)}_π − C^{(2)})[A′, A], 𝐉⟩, with Δ′_π[𝐉] the form of (3.120) with 𝐉 written for J and C^{(2)}_π(A) := C^{(2)}(A − DG′RD*A), collects the terms containing G′RD*A′ or G′RD*A; π_X denotes it in run X. Correspondingly β = β_act + β_con + β_π + β_gf: β_act is the defect of the plain second variations, β_con that of the terms 2⟨HC^{(2)}, 𝐉⟩, β_π := π_B(𝒜′, 𝒜) − π_A(Q₁𝒜′, Q₁𝒜), and β_gf is below. By *The π-Part of β.*, β_π ≡ 0, so β = β_act + β_con + β_gf.

*The Action Part of β.* By (B3) of §10.1, A_ε∘M̄ = A_ε∘M with M̄ := M̄_{U^B}, so the blocking artifact is b = A_ε∘M̄ − A_{ε/L}. Differentiating A_ε(M̄(e^{i(ε/L)(s𝒜′+t𝒜)}U^B)) in s and t at 0 gives the following identity. It uses dM̄(U^B) = Q₁ and d²M̄ = 2C₁^{(2)}, C₁^{(2)} := C̄₁^{(2)} of (B4), with |C₁^{(2)}(𝒜)| ≤ C₂ε|𝒜|² (VAR (44) at j = 1; CMP 98 (122)–(123), p.036, bound the whole nonlinear part, |C| ≤ C₁L²|A|², in that paper's units), so that polarisation in the sup norm gives |C₁^{(2)}[𝒜′, 𝒜]| ≤ 2C₂ε|𝒜′||𝒜|, the sups taken over the paths of the averaging:

β_act(𝒜′, 𝒜) := ⟨Q₁𝒜′, Δ^A(Ũ_j)Q₁𝒜⟩ − ⟨𝒜′, Δ^B(U^B)𝒜⟩ = d²b(U^B)[𝒜′, 𝒜] − 2⟨J^A(Ũ_j), C₁^{(2)}[𝒜′, 𝒜]⟩.

*The Constraint Part of β.* In both runs the current is the lift of λ^B through the averagings (§10.3): J′_j = Q^A_{j−1}*𝒬′*λ^B and J^B(U^B) = Q^B_j*𝒬′*λ^B, with 𝒬′ the last step at the common block field and PBG's Q_m, by (B2) of §10.1 and D̄*𝒬′*λ^B = 0 (§10.3). Since Q_mH = 1, ⟨HC^{(2)}[A′, A], Q_m*𝒬′*λ^B⟩ = ⟨C^{(2)}[A′, A], 𝒬′*λ^B⟩ in each run. The second-order part of (B4) is C_B^{(2)}(𝒜) = C_A^{(2)}(Q₁𝒜) + Q^A_{j−1}C₁^{(2)}(𝒜). Hence

β_con(𝒜′, 𝒜) := −2⟨C_A^{(2)}[Q₁𝒜′, Q₁𝒜], 𝒬′*λ^B⟩ + 2⟨C_B^{(2)}[𝒜′, 𝒜], 𝒬′*λ^B⟩ = 2⟨C₁^{(2)}[𝒜′, 𝒜], J′_j⟩,

and β_act + β_con = d²b(U^B)[𝒜′, 𝒜] − 2⟨J^A(Ũ_j) − J′_j, C₁^{(2)}[𝒜′, 𝒜]⟩. Here J^A(Ũ_j) − J′_j = δJ is the artifact current of (10.3a), and only its size enters. J^A(Ũ_j) = D*F̂(Ũ_j), with F̂ = F + O(ε⁴|F|³) the plaquette field of the first variation of the Wilson action, is at each bond a sum of d − 1 covariant differences of F̂(Ũ_j), each at most 2C₀ε₁a_j^{−3} by VAR (2) for Ũ_j (§9.3); a factor 2 covers the transports and the higher terms of F̂. With a_j³|J′_j| ≤ 2B_λε₁ (§10.3, *The Intermediate Current.*),

the artifact current satisfies |δJ| ≤ |J^A(Ũ_j)| + |J′_j| ≤ (4dC₀ + 2B_λ)ε₁a_j^{−3}.

*The Remainder of the Coarse Curvature.* Let p be a coarse plaquette of T_ε with corner y_p. The computation is in the axial gauge of U^B about y_p (*Transports.*), with η := ε/L. The cell of p is the set of fine bonds within ℓ¹-distance 4dε of y_p. It contains every fine bond on which (MW)(∂p) depends, the fine plaquettes p′ with w_{p,p′} > 0 in (9.11), and the paths γ_{p′} from y_p to their corners along the contours Γ. For W on the cell let

Φ_p(W) := (1/iε²)log (MW)(∂p), F^w_p(W) := Σ_{p′}w_{p,p′}Ad(W(γ_{p′}))F_{p′}(W), εr_p(W) := Φ_p(W) − F^w_p(W),

with F_{p′}(W) := (1/iη²)log W(∂p′), the weights of (9.11), and the logarithms of the holonomies based at the corners. So r_p is the remainder of (9.3) with the (9.11) average in place of the tiling average. Derivatives are taken along W ↦ e^{iηZ}W: d(εr_p)(W)[Z] := ∂_tεr_p(e^{iηtZ}W) and d²(εr_p)(W)[Z′, Z] := ∂_s∂_tεr_p(e^{iη(sZ′+tZ)}W) at s = t = 0, and |Z| is the sup of |Z(b)| over the cell.

*Analyticity.* Let 𝒫 be the set of complex configurations W(b) = e^{iζ(b)} on the cell with ζ(b) ∈ 𝔤_ℂ and |ζ(b)| ≤ 2r_G. Here r_G is a constant of d, L, and G, small enough for three properties: every holonomy entering Φ_p, F^w_p, and the F_{p′} on 𝒫 lies in the ball about 1 where log is analytic; |dlog_h| ≤ 2 there; and e^{iZ}e^{iζ} = e^{iζ′} with |ζ′| ≤ |ζ| + 2|Z| for |ζ|, |Z| ≤ r_G. The functions η²Φ_p = L^{−2}(1/i)log (MW)(∂p), η²F^w_p, and η²F_{p′} depend on the variables ζ(b) alone and are analytic on 𝒫. Let m_G be the sup over 𝒫 of η²(|Φ_p| + |F^w_p| + max_{p′}|F_{p′}|), a constant of d, L, and G. In the axial gauge U^B = e^{iη𝔞} on the cell, with |𝔞| ≤ 8dε|F| since |U^B(b) − 1| ≤ 4dε(ε/L)|F|; so η|𝔞| ≤ 8dε²|F| ≤ 8dC₀ε₁ ≤ r_G/2 under 16dC₀ε₁ ≤ r_G.

*Cauchy Estimates.* For W = e^{iζ} with |ζ| ≤ r_G, the function (s, t) ↦ εr_p(e^{iη(sZ′+tZ)}W) is analytic for |s| ≤ r_G/(4η|Z′|) and |t| ≤ r_G/(4η|Z|) and bounded there by m_Gη^{−2}. Cauchy's estimate gives

for all Z′ and Z, |d²(εr_p)(W)[Z′, Z]| ≤ k₂|Z′||Z|, k₂ := 16m_G/r_G²,

and the same bound for d²Φ_p, d²F^w_p, and d²F_{p′}. Along W_τ := e^{iητ𝔞}, the functions are analytic also in τ, for |τ − τ₀| ≤ r_G/(2η|𝔞|) about every τ₀ ∈ [0, 1], where |ητ𝔞| ≤ r_G. Cauchy's estimate in (τ, t), with the t-radius r_G/(2η|Z|), admissible for a single direction since |ηtZ| ≤ r_G/2, and in (τ, s, t), with the radii r_G/(4η|Z′|) and r_G/(4η|Z|), gives

for τ ∈ [0, 1], |∂_τ d(εr_p)(W_τ)[Z]| ≤ 4m_Gr_G^{−2}|𝔞||Z|, |∂_τ d²(εr_p)(W_τ)[Z′, Z]| ≤ 32m_Gr_G^{−3}η|𝔞||Z′||Z|.

*Vanishing at the Identity.* Three facts hold at W = 1:

- εr_p(1) = 0, both curvatures vanishing.
- d(εr_p)(1)[Z] = 0 for every Z. At W = 1, dΦ_p(1)[Z] = (D̄Q₁^{pl}Z)(p), the coarse curl of the untransported path average, and dF^w_p(1)[Z] = Σ_{p′}w_{p,p′}(DZ)(p′), the variations of the transports dropping out because F(1) = 0. (9.11) is the identity of the two.
- d²(εr_p)(1)[Z′_c, Z_c] = 0 for fields Z′_c, Z_c constant on the cell, Z_c(b) = Z_μ on the bonds of direction μ. Let Y := sZ′_c + tZ_c. For W = e^{iηY} each fine plaquette logarithm is i[Y_μ, Y_ν] + O(η|Y|³) by the Baker–Campbell–Hausdorff formula, and the transports of F^w_p do not enter the second derivative at 0: the terms with two variations of a transport vanish since F(1) = 0, and the cross terms (dAd)(dF) vanish since dF_{p′}(1)[Z_c] = (DZ_c)(p′) = 0 for the constant fields. The plain average of this configuration is invariant under the translations of T_ε, since the blocks and the paths Γ_{c,x} are translates of one another. So its coarse links are e^{iεȲ_μ}, with Ȳ_μ = Y_μ + O(ε|Y|²) from the loop holonomies of CMP 98 (15), and the coarse plaquette logarithm is i[Ȳ_μ, Ȳ_ν] + O(ε|Y|³) = i[Y_μ, Y_ν] + O(ε|Y|³). The weights sum to 1, so the second derivatives at s = t = 0 agree.

*The Variations of r_p.* For a field 𝒜 with the bounds of *Regularity of H₁.*, let 𝒜_c be the constant field on the cell with the axial-gauge values of 𝒜 on the bonds from y_p. A path of at most 4dε/η fine bonds joins every bond of the cell to one of these, and each step changes the axial-gauge value by at most η|∇𝒜| + 2|U^B(b) − 1||𝒜|, so |𝒜 − 𝒜_c| ≤ 4dε|∇𝒜| + δ_T|𝒜|. At W = 1 bilinearity and the third fact give d²(εr_p)(1)[𝒜′, 𝒜] = d²(εr_p)(1)[𝒜′ − 𝒜′_c, 𝒜] + d²(εr_p)(1)[𝒜′_c, 𝒜 − 𝒜_c]. Integrating the Cauchy estimates over τ ∈ [0, 1] from these values gives, at W = U^B:

- |εr_p(U^B)| ≤ ½k₂|𝔞|² ≤ 32d²k₂ε²|F|², by Taylor's formula along W_τ with the first two facts;
- |d(εr_p)[𝒜]| ≤ c_{r1}ε|F||𝒜|, c_{r1} := 32dm_G/r_G², by the second fact;
- |d²(εr_p)[𝒜′, 𝒜]| ≤ k₂(|𝒜′ − 𝒜′_c||𝒜| + |𝒜′||𝒜 − 𝒜_c|) + c_{r3}ε²|F||𝒜′||𝒜|, c_{r3} := 256dm_G/(Lr_G³).

The first variation has no term in the oscillation of D𝒜: with the (9.11) weights the coarse curl of the linearised average is exactly the weighted average of the fine curls at W = 1, and only the transport term of order ε|F||𝒜| remains.

*The Second Variation of b.* The Wilson density at spacing η is ½|X|² + κ_η(X) in the plaquette logarithm X, with |d^iκ_η(X)| ≤ c_κη⁴|X|^{4−i}, i = 0, 1, 2, for η²|X| ≤ 1, c_κ a constant of G. By the column-sum property Σ_pw_{p,p′} = L^{−d} of (9.11), which at d = 4 is L^{−4} = η⁴/ε⁴, as an identity in W,

b(W) = Σ_pε⁴[½|Φ_p(W)|² − ½Σ_{p′}w_{p,p′}|F_{p′}(W)|²] + Σ_pε⁴κ_ε(Φ_p(W)) − Σ_{p′}η⁴κ_η(F_{p′}(W)).

At U^B let f := dF_{p′}[𝒜], f′ := dF_{p′}[𝒜′], f″ := d²F_{p′}[𝒜′, 𝒜], and T := Ad(U^B(γ_{p′})). a and a′ denote the right-trivialised variations of W(γ_{p′}) in the directions 𝒜 and 𝒜′ (below), and ρ, ρ_t, ρ_s, ρ_{st} denote εr_p(U^B) and its variations in the directions 𝒜, 𝒜′, and (𝒜′, 𝒜). Then Φ_p = F^w_p + ρ, dF^w_p[𝒜] = Σ_{p′}w_{p,p′}(Tf + i[a, TF]), and |F_{p′}| = |TF_{p′}| for real W. So the second variation of the first sum is, at each p,

⟨dΦ_p[𝒜′], dΦ_p[𝒜]⟩ + ⟨Φ_p, d²Φ_p[𝒜′, 𝒜]⟩ − Σ_{p′}w_{p,p′}(⟨f′, f⟩ + ⟨F, f″⟩) = −Cov_p(Tf′, Tf) − Cov_p(TF, Tf″) + X_tr + X_r,

with Cov_p(X, Y) := Σ_{p′}w_{p,p′}⟨X_{p′}, Y_{p′}⟩ − ⟨Σ_{p′}w_{p,p′}X_{p′}, Σ_{p′}w_{p,p′}Y_{p′}⟩ the covariance under the probability weights w_{p,·}, X_tr the terms containing a or a′, and X_r the terms containing ρ, ρ_t, ρ_s, or ρ_{st}. |Cov_p(X, Y)| ≤ osc_pX·osc_pY, the oscillations taken over the fine plaquettes p′ with w_{p,p′} > 0. By (9.11) these lie in the box at y_p of side 2ε in the plane of p and of side ε in the other d − 2 directions, of Euclidean diameter at most ε(d + 6)^{1/2}.

*The Bounds.* Let 𝒜 = H₁^Be_{y′} and 𝒜′ = H₁^Be_y. On the cell of p, |𝒜| ≤ ℓ𝔰, |∇𝒜| ≤ 𝔰, and |D𝒜| ≤ 2𝔰, and likewise for 𝒜′ with 𝔰′. The weights are taken at their sup over the cell, which exceeds their value at y_p by at most e^{1/3} when δ₁ ≤ 1/(6d). By (9.2) at scale j, |F| ≤ C₀ε₁a_j^{−2} and |∇F| ≤ C₀ε₁a_j^{−3}, and ε²|F| ≤ ν_F ≤ C₀ε₁ ≤ 1; ε²|Φ_p| ≤ 1 by VAR (2) for MU^B (§9.3). The ingredients are as follows.

- f = dlog_h[(D𝒜)(p′)] at the plaquette holonomy h, so |f| ≤ 4𝔰 and |f − D𝒜| ≤ 2k_lν_F𝔰, with k_l the constant of |dlog_h − 1| ≤ k_l|log h|, a constant of G, and |log h| = η²|F| ≤ ν_F.
- The path γ_{p′} has at most 4dL fine bonds. With a taken as the right-trivialised variation, i a = (dW(γ_{p′})[𝒜])W(γ_{p′})^{−1}, so that dT[𝒜] = i ad_aT with no dlog factor, a is a sum of at most 4dL transported values η𝒜(b), so |a| ≤ 4dε|𝒜| ≤ 4dεℓ𝔰, and |i[a, TF]| ≤ c_{br}|a||F| ≤ 4dc_{br}ν_F𝔰 with c_{br} := sup|[X, Y]|/(|X||Y|), a constant of G. The second variation of T is i ad_{a″}T + (i ad_a)(i ad_{a′})T, with a″ the variation of a in the direction 𝒜′, a sum of commutators of the transported values over pairs of bonds of γ_{p′}, so |a″| ≤ ½c_{br}(4dε)²|𝒜′||𝒜|, and its norm is at most c_{br}|a″| + c_{br}²|a′||a| ≤ 24d²c_{br}²ε²|𝒜′||𝒜|.
- osc_p(Tf) ≤ 2ω + k_oν_F𝔰, k_o := 256d² + 4k_l. The oscillation of the curl D𝒜 over these plaquettes is at most 2ω (*Regularity of H₁.*). In the axial gauge the transports along the paths γ_{p′} and along the contours of the Hölder quotient are within δ_T ≤ 32d²ν_F of 1, which adds at most 4δ_T|D𝒜| ≤ 256d²ν_F𝔰, and f − D𝒜 adds 4k_lν_F𝔰.
- osc_p(TF) ≤ 4dε|∇F| + 4δ_T|F| ≤ 4d(1 + 32d)C₀ε₁εa_j^{−3}, the axial-gauge values changing along a path as those of 𝒜 below, and 4δ_T|F| = 128d²ε²|F|² ≤ 128d²C₀ε₁εa_j^{−3} by ε ≤ a_j and C₀ε₁ ≤ 1.
- |f″| ≤ k₂ℓ²𝔰′𝔰, so osc_p(Tf″) ≤ 2k₂ℓ²𝔰′𝔰, and |d²F^w_p| ≤ (k₂ + 32dc_{br} + 24d²c_{br}²)ℓ²𝔰′𝔰, the terms i[a′, Tf] + i[a, Tf′] and the second variation of T adding 32dc_{br}εℓ and 24d²c_{br}²ε²ℓ²|F|.
- By *The Variations of r_p.*, |ρ| ≤ 32d²k₂ε²|F|² ≤ 32d²k₂|F|, so |Φ_p| ≤ k_F|F| with k_F := 1 + 32d²k₂; |ρ_t| ≤ c_{r1}ν_F𝔰 and |ρ_s| ≤ c_{r1}ν_F𝔰′; and |ρ_{st}| ≤ [2k₂(4dεℓ + δ_Tℓ²) + c_{r3}ε²ℓ²|F|]𝔰′𝔰, so |F||ρ_{st}| ≤ (8dk₂ + 64d²k₂ + c_{r3})ν_F𝔰′𝔰, by εℓ|F| = ν_F and ν_F ≤ 1.

With these, at each p and in units of 𝔰′𝔰:

- |Cov_p(Tf′, Tf)| ≤ (2h_H(ε/ℓ)^{γ_H} + k_oν_F)² ≤ 4(h_H + k_o)²(ε/ℓ)^{γ_H}, by ω = h_H(ε/ℓ)^{γ_H}𝔰 and ν_F ≤ (ε/ℓ)^{γ_H};
- |Cov_p(TF, Tf″)| ≤ 8d(1 + 32d)k₂C₀ε₁(ε/ℓ)L^{−3}, since εℓ²a_j^{−3} = (ε/ℓ)L^{−3};
- |X_tr| ≤ (64dc_{br} + 40d²c_{br}²)ν_F: the cross terms of ⟨dF^w_p[𝒜′], dF^w_p[𝒜]⟩ give at most 32dc_{br}ν_F + 16d²c_{br}²ν_F², and ⟨F^w_p, ·⟩ of the transport terms of d²F^w_p at most 32dc_{br}ν_F + 24d²c_{br}²ν_F²;
- |X_r| ≤ [2(4 + 4dc_{br})c_{r1} + c_{r1}² + 32d²k₂(k₂ + 32dc_{br} + 24d²c_{br}²) + k_F(8dk₂ + 64d²k₂ + c_{r3})]ν_F, from ⟨dF^w_p[𝒜′], ρ_t⟩ + ⟨ρ_s, dF^w_p[𝒜]⟩ with |dF^w_p| ≤ (4 + 4dc_{br})𝔰, from ⟨ρ_s, ρ_t⟩, from ⟨ρ, d²F^w_p⟩, and from ⟨Φ_p, ρ_{st}⟩;
- the quartic terms are at most 2c_κ(k_F²k_Φ² + k_F³k_Φ″)ν_F, with k_Φ := 4 + 4dc_{br} + c_{r1} and k_Φ″ := k₂ + 32dc_{br} + 24d²c_{br}² + 8dk₂ + 64d²k₂ + c_{r3} bounding |dΦ_p| ≤ k_Φ𝔰 and |d²Φ_p| ≤ k_Φ″ℓ²𝔰′𝔰, since ε⁴|F|² ≤ ν_F² and ε⁴|F|³ℓ² ≤ ν_F², the fine side being bounded in the same way through Σ_{p′}w_{p,p′} = 1.

The current term is at most 2Σ_bε⁴|δJ|·2C₂ε|𝒜′||𝒜| ≤ 4C₂(4dC₀ + 2B_λ)ε₁(ε/ℓ)L^{−3} in the same units, summed over the coarse bonds. Over the coarse plaquettes the weights sum to Σ_pε⁴𝔰′𝔰 ≤ 2C_δB₀²ℓ^{−2}ℓ^de^{−¼δ₁d(y,y′)} by the triangle inequality, and over the coarse bonds to the same bound, with C_δ depending on δ₁ and d and taken to bound both sums; the factor 2 ≥ e^{2/3} covers the sup over the cell. With ν_F ≤ (ε/ℓ)^{γ_H}, every term of β_act + β_con at (𝒜′^B, 𝒜^B) is therefore bounded by

(10.6a) |(β_act + β_con)(H₁^Be_y, H₁^Be_{y′})| ≤ C_β(γ_H)[(ε/ℓ)^{γ_H} + ε₁(ε/ℓ)L^{−3}]·B₀ℓ^{−2}ℓ^de^{−¼δ₁d(y,y′)},

C_β(γ_H) := 2C_δB₀[4(h_H + k_o)² + k_ν + 8d(1 + 32d)k₂C₀ + 4C₂(4dC₀ + 2B_λ)], k_ν := 64dc_{br} + 40d²c_{br}² + 2(4 + 4dc_{br})c_{r1} + c_{r1}² + 32d²k₂(k₂ + 32dc_{br} + 24d²c_{br}²) + k_F(8dk₂ + 64d²k₂ + c_{r3}) + 2c_κ(k_F²k_Φ² + k_F³k_Φ″),

with h_H = 4^{γ_H}B₀(γ_H)/B₀, so that C_β is increasing in B₀(γ_H). The bound is for the bilinear form at the unit fields e_y and e_{y′}: since ⟨e_y, Ke_{y′}⟩ = ℓ^{2d}K(y, y′) for a kernel K on T_ℓ, it is a kernel bound C_β(γ_H)[(ε/ℓ)^{γ_H} + ε₁(ε/ℓ)L^{−3}]·B₀ℓ^{−2}ℓ^{−d}e^{−¼δ₁d(y,y′)}, of the form of the principal part's kernel bound (3.132), and (ε/ℓ)^{γ_H} = L^{−γ_H(j−1)}. The derivation uses only the sup, gradient, and 4-cell oscillation bounds of the two fields, and the leading term, the covariance of the first variations, is quadratic in their oscillation.

*The Gauge-Fixing Part of β.* Since R is an orthogonal projection,

β_gf(𝒜′, 𝒜) := ⟨Q₁𝒜′, D_AR_AD_A*Q₁𝒜⟩ − ⟨𝒜′, D_BR_BD_B*𝒜⟩ = ⟨r′_A, r_A⟩ − ⟨r′_B, r_B⟩,

with r_A := R_Af_A, f_A := D_A*Q₁𝒜 on T_ε, r_B := R_Bf_B, f_B := D_B*𝒜 on T_{ε/L}, and r′_A, r′_B the same for 𝒜′. By PBG (3.20)–(3.25) (p.394), R_X = 1 − G′_XQ′_X*K_X^{−1}Q′_XG′_X for X = A, B. Here Q′_X is the linearised averaging of gauge parameters to the scale ℓ, G′_X := (Δ_X + a_Xℓ^{−2}Q′_X*Q′_X)^{−1} with Δ_X the covariant Laplacian on gauge parameters, and K_X := Q′_XG′_X²Q′_X*. The coefficient a_X is PBG's a_m at a = 1 for the run's m, and the recursion a_{m+1} = a_m/(L^{−2} + a_m), a₀ = a₁ = 1, gives |a_A − a_B| = |a_{j−1} − a_j| ≤ 2L^{−j}. Hence r_X = f_X − φ_X(μ_X), with φ_X(μ) := G′_XQ′_X*μ and μ_X := K_X^{−1}Q′_XG′_Xf_X, and Q′_XG′_Xr_X = 0.

*Inputs.* PBG Theorems 3.1 and 3.2 hold on both lattices, uniformly in PBG's spacing η, which is ε for run A and ε/L for run B at the same ℓ = L^mη. For λ supported in Δ(y′), (3.42) gives |G′λ|, |∇G′λ|, |G′∇*λ| ≤ B₀(ℓ², ℓ, ℓ)e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)}|λ| on Δ(y), and (3.43), with a fixed cutoff equal to 1 on Δ(y), bounds the Hölder constant of exponent γ of ∇G′λ on Δ(y) by B₀(γ)ℓ^{1−γ}e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)}|λ|. (3.48) gives |K^{−1}(y, y′)| ≤ B₀ℓ^{−4}ℓ^{−d}e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)}. B₀ and B₀(γ) are taken large enough to cover these constants, and δ₁ ≤ δ₀ (decreasing δ₁ if necessary). Applied to a source bounded by a multiple of e^{−½δ₁d(·,y′)}, these operators then return the same decay times c_δ′ := sup_xΣ_ze^{−½δ₀d(x,z)}. Let B_G := c_δ′B₀ and B_G(γ_H) := c_δ′4^{γ_H}B₀(γ_H).

*Identities.* Let Q′₁λ(y) := L^{−d}Σ_{x∈B(y)}Ad(U^B(Γ_{y,x}))λ(x) be the one-step average of gauge parameters (CMP 98 (78)–(79)).

- (i) Q′_B = Q′_AQ′₁ (CMP 98 (80)) and Q′₁Q′₁* = 1, both exact.
- (ii) Q₁𝒜 = Q^{str}𝒜, where Q^{str} is the average of 𝒜 over the L^d straight fine paths of the tube of a coarse bond, divided by L: this is the main term (125) of CMP 98 (124) ((B4) of §10.1). The plain dM(U^B) differs from Q^{str} by the coarse difference of θ_𝒜(y) := −(ε/L)L^{−d}Σ_{x∈B(y)}𝒜(Γ_{y,x}), into which the Γ-pieces of the paths γ_{c,x} of §9.8 telescope, as (B1) requires; θ_𝒜 does not enter below.
- (iii) D_AQ′₁φ = Q^{str}D_Bφ, a coarse difference of block averages being the tube average of fine differences, and Q′₁D_B*g = D_A*Q̂_fg, summing a fine divergence over a block leaving the fluxes through its faces; Q̂_f is the average over the L^{d−1} fine bonds crossing a face. For a coarse bond c, Q̂_fg(c) is based at c₋: the value on a crossing bond b is carried to c₋ along Γ_{c₋,b₋}, b₋ ∈ B(c₋) being the endpoint of b in B(c₋). At the entering face of B(y), crossed by c′ = ⟨y − εe_μ, y⟩, D_A* then carries Q̂_fg(c′) from c′₋ to y by the coarse link Ũ_j(c′); the convention based at y differs from this one by a loop holonomy within δ_T of 1, which the count of *Transports.* covers.

(ii) and (iii) are exact at U^B = 1. In general they hold up to the corrections of *Transports.*, which include the non-main terms of CMP 98 (124) in Q₁. The transports in them run along the following paths: in Q′₁ along Γ_{y,x}; in Q^{str} along Γ_{y,x} followed by the straight path to the bond; and in Q̂_f along Γ_{c₋,b₋} to the endpoint of the crossing bond in B(c₋). These paths have at most (d + 1)L ≤ 3dL fine bonds, all within ℓ¹-distance 4dε of y, so each transport is within δ_T of 1, and the corrections are those of *Transports.*

*Sizes.* For 𝒜 = H₁^Be_{y′} put τ̂ := (ε/ℓ)^{γ_H} + ν_F. Then |f_B| ≤ d𝔰, |Q′_BG′_Bf_B| ≤ dB_Gℓ²𝔰, |μ_B| ≤ dB_G²ℓ^{−2}𝔰, |φ_B(μ_B)| ≤ dB_G³𝔰, and |∇φ_B(μ_B)| ≤ dB_G³ℓ^{−1}𝔰, so

(G1) |r_B| ≤ c_{g2}𝔰, c_{g2} := d(1 + B_G³), and osc_{B(y)}r_B ≤ c_{g1}τ̂𝔰, c_{g1} := d(h_H + dB_G³) + C_trc_{g2}.

The oscillation is that of the values transported to y over the block B(y) of side ε. For f_B it comes from *Regularity of H₁.*, for φ_B from dε|∇φ_B| with ε/ℓ ≤ (ε/ℓ)^{γ_H}, and the transports add at most δ_T|r_B| ≤ C_trν_F|r_B|.

*The Commutator.* For a fine source ρ bounded by a multiple |ρ| of e^{−½δ₁d(·,y′)}, let 𝔠(ρ) := G′_AQ′₁ρ − Q′₁G′_Bρ and ψ := G′_Bρ. Applying Q′₁ to (Δ_B + a_Bℓ^{−2}Q′_B*Q′_B)ψ = ρ and using (i) and (iii),

(Δ_A + a_Aℓ^{−2}Q′_A*Q′_A)Q′₁ψ = Q′₁ρ + D_A*[(Q^{str} − Q̂_f)D_Bψ + X] − Y + (a_A − a_B)ℓ^{−2}Q′_A*Q′_Bψ,

with |X| ≤ C_trε|F|(sup|ψ| + ε sup|∇ψ|) and |Y| ≤ C_trε|F|sup|∇ψ|, so

𝔠(ρ) = −G′_A{D_A*[(Q^{str} − Q̂_f)D_Bψ + X] − Y + (a_A − a_B)ℓ^{−2}Q′_A*Q′_Bψ}.

(Q^{str} − Q̂_f)D_Bψ is a difference of two averages of ∇ψ within one coarse cell, so by (3.43) it is at most B_G(γ_H)(ε/ℓ)^{γ_H}ℓ|ρ|; and |a_A − a_B| ≤ 2L^{−j} ≤ 2(ε/ℓ)^{γ_H}. With (3.42) for G′_A and G′_AD_A*,

(G2) |𝔠(ρ)| ≤ c_𝔠B_Gτ̂ℓ²|ρ|, c_𝔠 := dB_G(γ_H) + 2B_G + (2d + 1)C_trB_G,

since the X-term carries the factor d of the d components of G′_AD_A*, |G′_AD_A*X| ≤ dB_Gℓ·C_trε|F|(B_Gℓ² + εB_Gℓ)|ρ| ≤ 2dC_trB_G²ν_Fℓ²|ρ|, the Y-term gives C_trB_G²ν_Fℓ²|ρ|, and the a-term gives 2B_G²L^{−j}ℓ²|ρ| ≤ 2B_G²(ε/ℓ)^{γ_H}ℓ²|ρ|.

*The Defects.* Let e_f := f_A − Q′₁f_B, e_φ(μ) := φ_A(μ) − Q′₁φ_B(μ), and e_*(r) := Q′_AG′_AQ′₁r − Q′_BG′_Br. By (i), φ_A(μ) = G′_AQ′₁Q′_B*μ, so e_φ(μ) = 𝔠(Q′_B*μ) and e_*(r) = Q′_A𝔠(r). By (ii) and (iii), e_f = D_A*(Q^{str} − Q̂_f)𝒜 + X_f with |X_f| ≤ 2C_trν_F𝔰. The coarse differences of (Q^{str} − Q̂_f)𝒜 are ε times differences of averages of ∇𝒜 within a 4-cell, so its divergence is at most dω. Hence

(G3) |e_f| ≤ c_fτ̂𝔰, c_f := dh_H + 2C_tr; |e_φ(μ_B)| ≤ c_{g3}τ̂𝔰, c_{g3} := dB_G³c_𝔠; |e_*(r_B)| ≤ c_{g4}τ̂ℓ²𝔰, c_{g4} := B_Gc_𝔠c_{g2}.

Since Q′_XG′_Xr_X = 0 in both runs and f_A = Q′₁r_B + φ_A(μ_B) − e_φ(μ_B) + e_f, applying Q′_AG′_A (with Q′_AG′_Aφ_A(μ) = K_Aμ) gives

K_A(μ_A − μ_B) = e_*(r_B) + Q′_AG′_A(e_f − e_φ(μ_B)), δr := r_A − Q′₁r_B = e_f − e_φ(μ_B) − φ_A(μ_A − μ_B),

so |μ_A − μ_B| ≤ B_Gc_{g5}τ̂ℓ^{−2}𝔰 with c_{g5} := c_{g4} + B_G(c_f + c_{g3}), and |δr| ≤ c_{g6}τ̂𝔰 with c_{g6} := c_f + c_{g3} + B_G²c_{g5}.

*The Bound.* With δr := r_A − Q′₁r_B of *The Defects.*, r_A = Q′₁r_B + δr. Then ⟨Q′₁r′_B, Q′₁r_B⟩_ε − ⟨r′_B, r_B⟩_{ε/L} = −Σ_yε^dCov_{B(y)}(r′_B, r_B), the covariance of the values transported to y under the uniform distribution on B(y), Ad being isometric. So

β_gf(𝒜′, 𝒜) = −Σ_yε^dCov_{B(y)}(r′_B, r_B) + ⟨δr′, Q′₁r_B⟩ + ⟨Q′₁r′_B, δr⟩ + ⟨δr′, δr⟩.

For 𝒜′ = H₁^Be_y and 𝒜 = H₁^Be_{y′}, |Cov| ≤ osc·osc, τ̂ ≤ 2 when C₀ε₁ ≤ 1, and Σ_xε^d𝔰′(x)𝔰(x) ≤ C_δB₀²ℓ^{−2}ℓ^de^{−¼δ₁d(y,y′)}, 𝔰′ being the weight of 𝒜′. Therefore

(10.6b) |β_gf(H₁^Be_y, H₁^Be_{y′})| ≤ C_gf(γ_H)[(ε/ℓ)^{γ_H} + C₀ε₁(ε/ℓ)L^{−2}]·B₀ℓ^{−2}ℓ^de^{−¼δ₁d(y,y′)}, C_gf(γ_H) := 2C_δB₀(c_{g1}² + c_{g2}c_{g6} + c_{g6}²).

For the two fields, (10.6a) and (10.6b) use only the sup, gradient, and 4-cell oscillation bounds of *Regularity of H₁.*

*Functional Form.* For a general fine field ℱ, R_X being self-adjoint with R_Xr_X = r_X, β_gf(ℱ, 𝒜) = ⟨D_A*Q₁ℱ, r_A⟩_ε − ⟨D_B*ℱ, r_B⟩_{ε/L}, and D_A*Q₁ℱ = Q′₁D_B*ℱ + e_f(ℱ) with e_f(ℱ) = D_A*(Q^{str} − Q̂_f)ℱ + X_f(ℱ). Summing by parts once,

(10.6c) β_gf(ℱ, 𝒜) = ⟨D_B*ℱ, s₂⟩ + ⟨∇ℱ, s₁⟩ + ⟨ℱ, s₀⟩ (inner products of T_{ε/L}),

- s₂ := Q′₁*r_A − r_B = Q′₁*δr − (1 − Q′₁*Q′₁)r_B, so |s₂| ≤ (c_{g1} + c_{g6})τ̂𝔰;
- s₁ collects ⟨(Q^{str} − Q̂_f)ℱ, D_Ar_A⟩. (Q^{str} − Q̂_f)ℱ is a tent-weighted sum of longitudinal fine differences of slice averages of ℱ, the face slice being one of the slices. A fine bond carries total weight at most C_ldε, C_ld := 2 + 4dL^{d−1}, so this part of s₁ is at most C_ld sup_{cell}ε|D_Ar_A|. The ε∇ℱ-part of X_f(ℱ) adds a part of s₁, bounded below with s₀;
- s₀ comes from X_f(ℱ). The Transports count is term by term, so it bounds the absolute row sums: X_f(ℱ)(y) = Σ_b[k₀(y, b)ℱ(b) + k₁(y, b)ε∇ℱ(b)], with Σ_b|k₀(y, b)| + Σ_b|k₁(y, b)| ≤ C_trε|F|. The kernels vanish unless the fine bond b is within ℓ^∞-distance 2ε of y, so each b meets at most 5^d coarse points, and the change from ε^d to (ε/L)^d costs L^d. So the column sums are at most C_{5L}C_trε|F|, with C_{5L} := (5L)^d, and |s₀| ≤ C_{5L}C_trε|F||r_A|, while the part of s₁ from k₁ is at most C_{5L}C_trε²|F||r_A|.

Here |r_A| ≤ (c_{g2} + 2c_{g6})𝔰, since r_A = Q′₁r_B + δr and τ̂ ≤ 2, and ε|D_Ar_A| ≤ c_{g7}τ̂𝔰 with c_{g7} := dh_H + 4C_tr + 32d²(c_{g2} + 2c_{g6}) + B_G²(dB_G + 2c_{g5}). The coarse differences of f_A are differences of averages of ∇𝒜 within a 4-cell, with the transport corrections of two identities, at most 4C_trν_F𝔰. The coarse difference of r_A along a link carries the link's transport, within δ_T = 32d²ε²|F| of 1, which adds at most δ_T|r_A| ≤ 32d²(c_{g2} + 2c_{g6})ν_F𝔰, since ε²|F| ≤ ν_F. And ε|D_Aφ_A(μ_A)| ≤ (ε/ℓ)B_Gℓ²(|μ_B| + |μ_A − μ_B|). So |s₁| ≤ [C_ldc_{g7} + C_{5L}C_tr(c_{g2} + 2c_{g6})]τ̂𝔰 and |s₀| ≤ C_{5L}C_tr(c_{g2} + 2c_{g6})ν_Fℓ^{−1}𝔰, all with the decay of 𝔰 about y′.

*The π-Part of β.* At a pair (𝐔, 𝐉) of run X ∈ {A, B} the remainder π_X of *The Parts of β.* is the polarisation of its quadratic form, and that form vanishes when 𝐉 = Q_X*ν, ν := 𝒬′*λ^B. For a field A of run X let μ_A := G′_XR_XD_X*A and P_XA := A − D_Xμ_A. Then Q′_Xμ_A = 0, since Q′_XG′_XR_X = 0 by PBG (3.25), and π_X(A, A) = ⟨A, Δ′_π[𝐉]A⟩ + 2⟨H(C_X^{(2)}(P_XA) − C_X^{(2)}(A)), 𝐉⟩, since C^{(2)}_π(A) = C^{(2)}(P_XA) by definition.

- The bracket of (3.120). PBG (3.116) gives (D_Xλ)(b) = η^{−1}(R_bλ(b₊) − λ(b₋)), so i[λ(b₋), (D_Xλ)(b)] = c_X(λ, D_Xλ)(b) pointwise, with c_X the bracket c of (B5) of §10.1 in run X.
