The bracket of (3.117) is therefore 2c_X(λ, Y) − c_X(λ, D_Xλ), and at λ = μ_A and Y = A it is c_X(μ_A, A) + c_X(μ_A, P_XA) as a bond field. (3.120) is (3.117) at λ = μ_A, so ⟨A, Δ′_π[𝐉]A⟩ = ⟨𝐉, c_X(μ_A, A) + c_X(μ_A, P_XA)⟩ for every 𝐉.
- The C^{(2)}-terms. By bilinearity, C_X^{(2)}(P_XA) − C_X^{(2)}(A) = −C_X^{(2)}[D_Xμ_A, A] − C_X^{(2)}[D_Xμ_A, P_XA]. (B5) with Q′_Xμ_A = 0 gives 2C_X^{(2)}[D_Xμ_A, Y] = Q_Xc_X(μ_A, Y) + D̄q_X(μ_A, Y), and D̄*ν = 0 (§10.3, *The Multipliers.*), so 2⟨C_X^{(2)}(P_XA) − C_X^{(2)}(A), ν⟩ = −⟨Q_X*ν, c_X(μ_A, A) + c_X(μ_A, P_XA)⟩.
- The cancellation. At 𝐉 = Q_X*ν, Q_mH = 1 gives ⟨H(C_X^{(2)}(P_XA) − C_X^{(2)}(A)), 𝐉⟩ = ⟨C_X^{(2)}(P_XA) − C_X^{(2)}(A), ν⟩, so the two bullets give π_X(A, A) = ⟨Q_X*ν, c_X(μ_A, A) + c_X(μ_A, P_XA)⟩ − ⟨Q_X*ν, c_X(μ_A, A) + c_X(μ_A, P_XA)⟩ = 0. The current is Q_X*ν in both runs: J^B(U^B) = Q^B_j*ν at U^B, and J′_j = Q^A_{j−1}*ν at run A's pair (§10.3, *The Multipliers.*). π_A and π_B are symmetric bilinear forms with vanishing quadratic forms. Hence π_X ≡ 0 in both runs, so β_π ≡ 0.

*The Landau Condition at the Pair.* Since π_A ≡ 0, the form of run A at the pair is ⟨A′, Δ₁^AA⟩ = ⟨A′, Δ_AA⟩ − 2⟨C_A^{(2)}[A′, A], ν⟩. The bilinear part of (3.117), ⟨D_Aλ, Δ_AY⟩ = ⟨J^A(Ũ_j), c_A(λ, Y)⟩ with the background's own current, and (B5) give ⟨D_Aλ, Δ₁^AY⟩ = ⟨δJ, c_A(λ, Y)⟩ for λ ∈ N(Q′_A). So the form at the pair is invariant under N(Q′_A) only if ⟨δJ, c_A(λ, Y)⟩ vanishes identically, which is not given. The Faddeev–Popov calculation of PBG (3.121)–(3.126) then does not give R_AD_A*H₁^A = 0 there, nor the identities (3.151)–(3.152) on which PBG's 𝔊 of (3.153) rests. §10.4 uses neither: (10.2), (10.5), the positivity of G_A^{−1} on ker Q^A_{j−1}, the regularity of H₁^A, and the identification E = G₁^Qℒ*β(·, 𝒜^B) after (10.5) use only H₁ = G₁Q_m*(Q_mG₁Q_m*)^{−1}, the invertibility of G₁ and Q_mG₁Q_m*, and the series bounds, which hold at the pair.

*The J-Term of (10.1).* D̃^{(2)} is common to both runs. By §10.3, J′_j = Q^A_{j−1}*𝒬′*λ^B and J^B(U^B) = Q^B_j*𝒬′*λ^B with the same 𝒬′, and Q_mH₁ = 1 in both runs. So

⟨H₁^AD̃^{(2)}(B), J′_j⟩ = ⟨D̃^{(2)}(B), 𝒬′*λ^B⟩ = ⟨H₁^BD̃^{(2)}(B), J^B(U^B)⟩,

and the J-terms of the two forms are equal, at the pair (Ũ_j, J′_j) and at U^B.

*Assembly.* The lower bound below is (10.5) with ⟨E, G_A^{−1}E⟩ ≥ 0. The upper bound is the minimality in (10.2) for run B applied to ℒ𝒜^A, which is admissible because Q^B_jℒ = Q^A_{j−1}Q₁ℒ = Q^A_{j−1}; since Q₁ℒ = 1, ⟨ℒ𝒜^A, G_B^{−1}ℒ𝒜^A⟩ = ⟨𝒜^A, G_A^{−1}𝒜^A⟩ − β(ℒ𝒜^A, ℒ𝒜^A). Hence

(10.5′) −β(𝒜^B, 𝒜^B) ≤ ⟨B, (P_B − P_A)B⟩ ≤ −β(ℒ𝒜^A, ℒ𝒜^A).

*Regularity of the Lifts.* Here ℒ is the lift of the first paragraph of §10.4, built from Q₁ = Q̄₁(U^B), and I is gauge covariant: the multilinear interpolation with the coarse values carried to the fine bonds by the transports of U^B along coordinate paths. The node of the μ-component of a coarse bond c is the centroid of its tube in Q^{str}, the midpoint of the bond joining the centres of B(c₋) and B(c₊); under the corner blocks of PRT1 (1.6) this is the midpoint of c shifted by ((L − 1)/2)(ε/L) in every coordinate. With these nodes Q^{str}I reproduces affine fields at U^B = 1, which the Taylor bound below needs. Q₁ℒ = 1 holds for every I by the construction of ℒ. Let g := H₁^Ae_y, so g has the sup, gradient, and 4-cell oscillation bounds of *Regularity of H₁.* with the weight 𝔰′ := B₀ℓ^{−1}e^{−½δ₁d(·,y)}.

- In the axial gauge about a coarse point, Ig is a convex combination of transported coarse values. So |Ig| ≤ sup|g|; its fine differences are interpolated coarse differences of g up to (2d + 128d²)ν_F𝔰′ ≤ 2C_trν_F𝔰′. Two terms make this up. The coordinate paths that carry the coarse values to the two ends of a fine bond b, together with b, enclose a strip of at most dL fine plaquettes, so the two transports differ by at most 2dε(ε/L)|F|, which after division by ε/L is 2dε|F||g| ≤ 2dν_F𝔰′ by |g| ≤ ℓ𝔰′. The differences of the interpolation weights, of total at most 2/ε after division by ε/L, act on values carried along different node paths, which in the axial gauge are within 2δ_T of each other, so they add at most (2/ε)·2δ_T|g| = 128d²ε|F||g| ≤ 128d²ν_F𝔰′. And their oscillation over a fine 4-cell of side 4ε is at most that of ∇g over a coarse cell of side 6ε, (3/2)^{γ_H}ω, plus 4C_trν_F𝔰′.
- In the same gauge Q^{str}I reproduces affine fields, by the choice of the nodes, so Taylor's formula about the node of each coarse bond gives |g − Q₁Ig| ≤ Cε²|∇∇g| + C_trε²|F||g|, with C a constant of d and the second term collecting the transports and the non-main terms of CMP 98 (124) as in *Transports.*; and ε²|∇∇g| ≤ εω on the lattice. The correction is Q₁*T^{−1}(g − Q₁Ig), with T := Q₁Q₁* on the coarse bond fields, and T^{−1} is bounded at U^B as follows.
  - T has finite range, T(c, c′) = 0 unless d_ε(c, c′) ≤ r_T, with r_T a constant of d, since the paths of Q₁ stay within 2L fine spacings. Its norm is at most a constant M̃_Q of L and d, since Ad is isometric. At U^B = 1 its spectrum is bounded below by m_Q := 1/3. There T acts along each coordinate column as a convolution with the overlaps of the tents of (ii): its diagonal entry (2L³ + L)/(3L³) exceeds the sum (L³ − L)/(3L³) of the others by (L³ + 2L)/(3L³) ≥ 1/3.
  - The lower bound at U^B. Let χ_i be functions supported in boxes of side R_Qε, with Σ_iχ_i² = 1 and |χ_i(c) − χ_i(c′)| ≤ C_χd_ε(c, c′)/R_Q, C_χ a constant of d. Then T = Σ_iχ_iTχ_i + ½Σ_i(χ_i(c) − χ_i(c′))²T(c, c′), and the second sum has norm at most C_E/R_Q², with C_E a constant of L and d, since T has range r_T and each point lies in at most 2^d boxes. T is gauge covariant: differentiating CMP 98 (11) gives Q₁^{pl}(U^u)Ad(u) = Ad(u|)Q₁^{pl}(U), with u| the restriction to the coarse points, and Θ₁ is covariant in the same way, since v₁(y) = (R_{0,y}U₁)‾ (CMP 98 (97), (105)) is conjugated by u(y), so by (B1) Q̄₁(U^u)Ad(u) = Ad(u|)Q̄₁(U) and T(U^u) = Ad(u|)T(U)Ad(u|)^{−1}. Ad is isometric, so χ_iTχ_i is unitarily equivalent to its form in the axial gauge of its box. There the bonds of the box and of the paths of Q₁ that start in it have |U^B(b) − 1| ≤ d(R_Q + 3)ε(ε/L)|F|, so ‖χ_i(T(U^B) − T(1))χ_i‖ ≤ C_ax·d(R_Q + 3)ε²|F|, with C_ax a constant of L and d. R_Q is fixed by C_E/R_Q² ≤ m_Q/4. Under C_ax·d(R_Q + 3)C₀ε₁ ≤ m_Q/4, since ε²|F| ≤ C₀ε₁ by (9.2), the spectrum of T lies in [m_Q/2, M̃_Q].
  - The decay. With q := 1 − m_Q/(2M̃_Q), the Neumann series T^{−1} = M̃_Q^{−1}Σ_{n≥0}(1 − T/M̃_Q)^n, whose n-th term has norm at most q^n and range nr_T, gives |T^{−1}(c, c′)| ≤ (2/m_Q)e^{−δ_Qd_ε(c,c′)}, δ_Q := r_T^{−1}log(1/q). For δ₁ ≤ δ_Q, T^{−1} maps a source bounded by a multiple of e^{−½δ₁d(·,y)} to at most C_Q times the same multiple, C_Q := (2/m_Q)sup_cΣ_{c′}e^{−½δ_Qd_ε(c,c′)}, by the triangle inequality. Also sup|Q₁*ν| ≤ 2(1 + 2dL^{d−1})sup|ν|. The main term (125) of CMP 98 (124) has column sums 1. The third term of (124), the non-main operator on the straight paths [x, x′], has column weight 1, the Γ-terms have column weight at most 2dL^{d−1}, and the last term at most L^{d−1}. The operator factors of these non-main terms are at most c₁₂₄L²α₀ (CMP 98 p.036, after (124), and (126)), with L²α₀ = ε²|F| ≤ C₀ε₁, so at most ½ under c₁₂₄C₀ε₁ ≤ ½, and Proposition 3 applies under 2C₀ε₁ ≤ c₃. The total is at most 1 + ½(1 + 2dL^{d−1} + L^{d−1}) ≤ 2(1 + 2dL^{d−1}).
  - With δ₁ ≤ min(δ_Q, 1/(10d)), the weight 𝔰′ changes by at most the factor e^{1/4} < 4/3 over the ℓ¹ distance 5dε, which covers the sup and Taylor stencils (3dε) and the points of the 6ε-cell of the oscillation bullet (5ε per coordinate). The factor (3/2)^{γ_H} of the first bullet is the ratio of the cube sides for the ℓ^∞ distance, and at most 3^{γ_H} for the Euclidean distance (the side-6ε cell has Euclidean diameter 6ε√d = 12ε at d = 4). So the bounds of this paragraph hold with the weight taken at the point, the first bullet's (3/2)^{γ_H}ω becoming at most 4ω, since 3^γ·4/3 < 4 for every exponent γ < 1, in particular at γ_H and at 2γ_H, whichever distance PBG (3.40) uses; the factor 4 of C_ℒ′ below covers this.

  The correction therefore has sup at most C_I(εω + C_trεν_F𝔰′), with C_I := 2(1 + 2dL^{d−1})C_Q·max(C, 1). Its fine differences are at most 2(L/ε) times its sup, 2LC_I(ω + C_trν_F𝔰′).

With ν_F ≤ (ε/ℓ)^{γ_H} (C₀ε₁ ≤ 1), ℒH₁^Ae_y satisfies the sup, gradient, and 4-cell oscillation bounds of *Regularity of H₁.* with B₀ replaced by C_ℒ′B₀ and B₀(γ_H) by C_ℒ′(B₀(γ_H) + B₀), where C_ℒ′ := 4(1 + 2C_tr)(1 + 2LC_I(1 + h_H)). The bounds above are for fine differences; the curl and divergence, sums of 2 and of d fine differences, carry the factors 2 and d in their oscillations, as in *Regularity of H₁.* The derivation holds at every Hölder exponent γ of (3.133) in place of γ_H, with h_H(γ) := 4^γB₀(γ)/B₀ in C_ℒ′; at the exponent 2γ_H of *Polarisation.*, h_H(2γ_H) = 16^{γ_H}B₀(2γ_H)/B₀ and B₀(2γ_H) + B₀ replaces B₀(2γ_H).

*Polarisation.* 𝒜^B = Σ_yH₁^B(e_y ⊗ B(y)) expands over the points y, and ℒ𝒜^A likewise. Since β_π ≡ 0, β = β_act + β_con + β_gf. (10.6a) and (10.6b) apply at the exponent 2γ_H, which is admissible in (3.133) and (3.43) for 0 < γ_H < ½, to H₁^Be_y and to C_ℒ′(2γ_H)^{−1}ℒH₁^Ae_y. C_β^ℒ and C_gf^ℒ denote C_β and C_gf at the exponent 2γ_H (increasing in B₀(2γ_H)) with B₀(2γ_H) + B₀ in place of B₀(2γ_H). With ε/ℓ ≤ (ε/ℓ)^{2γ_H}, ε₁ ≤ 1, and C₀ε₁ ≤ 1, (10.5′) gives

(A1) |⟨B, (P_B − P_A)B⟩| ≤ r_βΣ_{y,y′}|B(y)||B(y′)|B₀ℓ^{−2}ℓ^de^{−¼δ₁d(y,y′)}, r_β := 2C_ℒ′(2γ_H)²[C_β^ℒ(2γ_H) + C_gf^ℒ(2γ_H)](ε/ℓ)^{2γ_H}.

P_B − P_A is symmetric. Polarisation, ⟨u, Tv⟩ = ¼[⟨u + v, T(u + v)⟩ − ⟨u − v, T(u − v)⟩] for u = e_y ⊗ X and v = e_{y′} ⊗ X′ with |X| = |X′| = 1, gives |(P_B − P_A)(y, y′)| ≤ 2r_βB₀ℓ^{−2}ℓ^{−d}, without decay.

*Interpolation.* P_B − P_A is also the difference of the two runs' (Q_mG₁Q_m*)^{−1}, the a-terms cancelling, so (3.132) gives |(P_B − P_A)(y, y′)| ≤ 2B₀ℓ^{−2}ℓ^{−d}e^{−δ₁d(y,y′)}. With min(u, v) ≤ (uv)^{1/2},

(A2) |(P_B − P_A)(y, y′)| ≤ 2r_β^{1/2}B₀ℓ^{−2}ℓ^{−d}e^{−½δ₁d(y,y′)} ≤ 3C_ℒ′(2γ_H)[C_β^ℒ(2γ_H) + C_gf^ℒ(2γ_H)]^{1/2}L^{−γ_H(j−1)}B₀ℓ^{−2}ℓ^{−d}e^{−½δ₁d(y,y′)}.

δΔ_j is this difference, the J-terms being equal, plus the alignment (10.3), whose factor is at most [16C_G(ε₀) + 8x_J(C_G(ε₀) + 1)]ε₁L^{−(1−ε₀)j}/α_* by ρ_j = C_G(ε₀)L^{−(1−ε₀)j}ε₁ and ε/a_j = L^{−j}. Hence

(10.6) |δΔ_j(y, y′)| ≤ r_jB₀ℓ^{−2}ℓ^{−d}e^{−δ₂d(y,y′)}, r_j ≤ C(ε₀, γ_H)[L^{−γ_H(j−1)} + ε₁L^{−(1−ε₀)j}/α_*] with α_* := min(α₁, α_J) and C(ε₀, γ_H) := 3C_ℒ′(2γ_H)[C_β^ℒ(2γ_H) + C_gf^ℒ(2γ_H)]^{1/2} + 16C_G(ε₀) + 8x_J(C_G(ε₀) + 1) + 1,

for j ≥ 1, 0 < γ_H < ½, 0 < ε₀ < ½, ε₁ small enough for the conditions listed after (10.3), for all j (ρ_j ≤ C_Gε₁), for C₀ε₁ ≤ 1, for 16dC₀ε₁ ≤ r_G (*Analyticity.*), for 16c₁₂₄C₀ε₁ ≤ 1 (the non-main terms of CMP 98 (124) in *Transports.*, *Regularity of the Lifts.*, and the identification of E, and *The Size of w.*), and for C_ax·d(R_Q + 3)C₀ε₁ ≤ m_Q/4 (*Regularity of the Lifts.*), with δ₁ ≤ min(δ_Q, 1/(10d)) and B₀(γ_H) ≥ 3B₀ (*Regularity of H₁.* at j = 1). The constants depend on d, L, γ_H, ε₀, and the constants of VAR and PBG only, and not on j, k, K, ε, or the volume. This is the Gaussian analogue of (9.10): the two runs' step covariances at the shared scale differ relatively by a quantity geometric in the number of steps already taken, L^{−γ_H(j−1)}, not in the number still to come.

### 10.5 What the Gaussian Depth Feeds (And What It Needs)

In (8.1) the quadratic insertion −½⟨A, C*δΔ^{(k)}CA⟩ on a localisation domain Y is a marked activity of §8.5's kind: its Gaussian mean is ≤ |Y|·sup_yΣ_{y′}|δΔ_k(y,y′)|·⟨|A|²⟩ ≤ |Y|c_δr_kB₀·4C_G(c_k + d_k(Y)) in scale-ℓ units, and its bound on Bałaban's fluctuation domain (3.43) is |Y|c_δr_kB₀(C₁p₁(g_k))², so by the two-part bound of §8.5 its contribution to the generated difference per scale-(k+1) point is ≤ C_KPC_Xc_δB₀[4C_Gc_k + a_k(C₁p₁(g_k))²]r_k ≤ C_e′r_k, uniformly in k (a_kp₁(g_k)² = g_kP(log g_k^{−2}) → 0 and c_k ≤ ℓ_K), geometric in k by (10.6): this is the Gaussian part of the depth source e_{k+1} of (8.3), which together with the classical part gives e_{k+1} ≤ C_eθ̃^{k+1}, and Σ_k of it is finite. The classical part of e_{k+1} is (9.10) at the fluctuated block field (§8.1). The configuration part, the variation group of (3.1) and its fluctuation-dependent version in §8.2, is the change of a frozen term under the scale-a smooth background change of §9.7, |𝒜| ≤ σ_k/a, |∇𝒜| ≤ σ_k/a²; the Cauchy estimate of 10.3 with the term's inductive bound (I.1.18) alone gives E₀(σ̂_k/r_{0,k})L^{−2(k−j)} per scale-(j+1) point, i.e. E₀(σ̂_k/r_{0,k})L^{2(k−j)}N_s⁴ at the stopping scale, whose sum over j is (a/ε)^{1+2ε₀}ε₁N_s⁴, not small: without the age factor the variation group is not controlled. The age factor comes from (5.1v) of §5 (*What it gives*). Each of the group's two values has the age bound of (5.1c) for one term, at a local minimiser of its own lattice, and the geometric mean of that bound with the Cauchy bound along the pair line of §5 gives E_V[(σ̂_{k,j}/r_{0,k})^{θ_V} + (C_Π(j)ε₁²e^{−δ₀M₁L^j})^{θ_V}](L^{j−k})^{4+β′/2} per scale-(j+1) point, θ_V = β′/(2(4+β′)), σ̂_{k,j} = σ_k + C(ε₀)ρ_k + C(ε₀, δ₀M₁)ε₁e^{−δ₀M₁L^j/2}. At the stopping scale this carries L^{−(β′/2)(k−j)}N_s⁴, and the sum over j is summable in K (§5). It needs the lift condition (§5). At the fluctuation-dependent configurations the two bounds hold on the complex lines t ↦ tA of §8.2: the age bound on the whole disc, where both configurations are local minimisers of complex data, and the Cauchy bound on its real diameter, where the block field is real and regular by (3.14) and §9.7 applies. On that diameter the block field carries the fluctuation up to the margin m_k, so the ε₁ of §9.7 is the plaquette bound at that level, at most O(1)α_{0,k}, and σ̂_{k,j}/r_{0,k} ≤ O(1)B₃C_σ̂(L^{−(1−2ε₀)k} + e^{−δ₀M₁L^j/2}), with the same sum over j as in §5. The harmonic measure of the circle at the points of the disc of a quarter of the radius, relative to the disc cut along the diameter, is ≤ (4/π)arctan(1/4) < 1/3. So by the two-constants theorem the group is ≤ (E_V[(σ̂_{k,j}/r_{0,k})^{θ_V} + (C_Π(j)ε₁²e^{−δ₀M₁L^j})^{θ_V}])^{2/3}(2E₁(α))^{1/3}(L^{j−k})^{4+2β′/3} on that smaller disc, and §8.2's argument uses it with T₀ ≥ 8, a threshold of the same kind as T₀ ≥ 2 there.

The 𝐑-terms' group (§5, *The 𝐑-terms' variation group*) has a fluctuation-dependent version at each step k, split at the age m(k) := ⌊(1 − 2ε₀)k/12⌋.
- At the ages k − j < m(k) (derived except the lift condition). The two bounds are (2.31) on the whole disc, 2g_j^{κ₀}exp(−κd_j(X)) per domain for the two values, and C′_σ(σ̂_{k,j}/r_{0,k})g_j^{κ₀}exp(−κd_j(X)) on its real diameter, C′_σ := C_σ + 16C_J, by the frozen-current split of §5. So the two-constants theorem gives ≤ 2^{1/3}(C′_σσ̂_{k,j}/r_{0,k})^{2/3}g_j^{κ₀}exp(−κd_j(X)) per domain on the smaller disc. At these ages e^{−δ₀M₁L^j/2} ≤ L^{−(1−2ε₀)k} except at finitely many k, so σ̂_{k,j} ≤ C_σ̂L^{−(1−2ε₀)k}ε₁, and C′_R absorbs the finitely many k. With C_X and the L^{4(k−j)} points this is ≤ 2^{1/3}C_XC′_σ^{2/3}(sup_{k−j<m(k)}σ̂_{k,j}/r_{0,k})^{2/3}L^{4m(k)}/(1 − L^{−4}) ≤ C′_RL^{−(1−2ε₀)k/3} per scale-(k+1) point. It is a part of e_{k+1}, since θ̃ ≥ L^{−(1−2ε₀)/3} (§8.7).
- At the ages k − j ≥ m(k) (open). Each run's term at its own fluctuation-dependent configuration, a U_k-type configuration (p.279 l.43–44), needs p.283's bound O(1)(L^jL^{−k})⁴g_j^{κ₀}exp(−κd_j(X)) on the disc, which is the 𝐑 version of (5.1c) at complex configurations, open. With it, g_j^{−2} ≥ ḡ^{−2} + b(K − j) − C with K − j ≥ K − k + m(k) ≥ (1 − 2ε₀)K/12 − 1 by Lemma 2.1(i), so these ages give ≤ C″_RK^{1−κ₀/2} per scale-(k+1) point at each step, and their sum over the steps k ≤ K is ≤ C″_RK^{2−κ₀/2}. What closes it: the 𝐑 version of (5.1c), and this source carried in S_K, which needs Kμ_K in place of μ_K in w_K, summable with Kw_K for κ₀ ≥ 9, a choice that CRE p.260 l.26–27 allows ("κ₀ can be chosen arbitrarily large"). The Gaussian depth itself needs no age factor.

## 11. The Relative Weight of the Large-Field Terms (Derived; Replaces (Q))

The continuum note's (7.1)–(7.3) reduce the relative weight of the large-field terms to the quotient (Q) of two integrals over the stopping-scale variables on the neighbourhood Z^∼ of a large-field region with the exterior variables fixed, and (Q) is not derived there. The stopping lattice T_a is fixed and finite, with N_s⁴ points, so the two integrals are compared as wholes instead: the whole all-small-field integral is bounded below by the Haar volume of a ball of configurations, and every large-field term's integral is bounded above by the supremum of its small-field exponent, each at the price of a constant exponential in N_s⁴ and independent of K. The relative weight is then bounded uniformly in K at every loop, which is what the Cauchy estimate uses, and it is small for ḡ small at a fixed loop and torus, the setting of the continuum limit (the infinite-volume limit is not part of D2). The one cost that this comparison does not bound by N_s⁴ is the small-field action on the inner layers of a component's history, which CRE's Theorem 2 counts at their own scales. It is paid in (c)–(d) from the costs and half of the gains of (1.79), which also leave a spare factor exponential in the history volume for §6 and §8.6.

### 11(a) The Terms at the Stopping Scale

By CRE (2.18) the stopping-scale density is ρ_k(V) = Σ_ℓρ_ℓ(V), the sum over the labelled terms ℓ (the sequences {Ω_j}, {Λ_j} and the R-operation labels), each ρ_ℓ(V) = χ_k(Ω_k)𝐓_k(ℓ)[exp A_k(1/g_k², U_k)] a function of the stopping-scale field V on the bonds of T_a, nonnegative on the real integration region, with the effective action (2.23), A_k = −A(1/g_k², U_k) + 𝐄_k + 𝐑_k + 𝐁_k − E_k. The weights are Z_ℓ := ∫ρ_ℓ(V) dV with the normalised Haar measure on G^{bonds(T_a)}, w_ℓ := Z_ℓ/Σ_{ℓ′}Z_{ℓ′}, and 1 − w_{ℓ₀} ≤ Σ_{ℓ≠ℓ₀}Z_ℓ/Z_{ℓ₀} (the continuum note's (7.1)).

Every term is written in the completed form of LFR II p.377 l.22–29 for its kept components, and in the factorised form with the normalised operations of (1.79) for its surviving components, (1.79) holding for an arbitrary large-field region (p.387 l.36–39), with the constants of the latter accounted outside 𝐓 in E^ℓ. Bałaban adds the terms of the effective action whose localisations meet the large-field domain "to complete the effective action, and we subtract them from the remaining expression". The term is then χ_k·Π_{X⊂Z(ℓ)}𝐓′_k(X)·exp A′_k, with A′_k "the whole new action connected with the new determining set 𝐁_k" (p.377 l.22–28). The subtracted constants, E_k(X) + (−½d(𝐠)log g_k^{−2} + log σ₀)|(𝐁₀∖T₀) ∩ X| − E_k(Λ ∩ X), stand inside the integration of 𝐓′_k(X) ((1.70); E_k(Z) is "the sum of normalization terms and vacuum energy terms with localizations intersecting the large field domain Z", p.378 l.2). LFR II p.387 l.36–39 states that (1.79) holds for the 𝐓-operation connected with an arbitrary large-field region and that (1.80) holds for such regions; CRE p.264 l.17–23 is the resulting estimate of ∫dV_kρ_k "by a sum of terms". The normalisation used below divides every term by e^{−E^{ℓ₀}}, the constant of the all-small-field term ℓ₀, one number for all terms. Then, for every term ℓ,

Z_ℓ ≤ Π_{X⊂Z(ℓ)}[e·sup 𝐓′_k(X)1]·sup exp(𝒜_ℓ), the second supremum over the term's integration region, with the following ingredients.

- 𝐓′_k(X) is the operation of the component X, normalised as in (1.79) and bounded by (1.79)'s right side (p.383 l.46–p.384 l.1). For a kept component it is the operation of LFR I p.177's renormalisation class after the completion of LFR II pp.377–378. For a surviving component it is CRE (2.20)–(2.21)'s operation, which the R-operation leaves unchanged (LFR I p.177 l.42–43).
- Inside 𝐓′_k(X) are the subtracted constants of (1.70), the normalisation constants of the large-field integrations with their logarithms (LFR II p.380 l.24–27, p.383 l.29–31), and the inner-layer action V(X^∼) of (1.69).
- The gauge-fixing δ-functions of a kept component (LFR II (1.72), p.378 l.21–22) are integrated first against the normalised Haar measure, so the bound holds for Z_ℓ and not pointwise in V. Removing the δ-functions of a first-class component gives a density that is "equivalent, but not equal" (p.378 l.18–20), with the same Z_ℓ.
- The factor e per component is from (1.92) (e^{|σ|} ≤ e by (1.73)); the supremum is over the integrated variables and the exterior field ((1.79), uniform in both).
- 𝒜_ℓ is the term's completed exponent less the common constant: −A(1/g_k²(·), U_k) + 𝐄_k + 𝐑_k + 𝐁_k + (E^{ℓ₀} − E^ℓ), with, for a kept component, the exterior couplings V(Y) of (1.68) and 𝐑′ of (1.99). Here E^ℓ is the constant of A′_k.
- The normalisation and vacuum-energy terms of E^ℓ are localised. Those whose localisation meets no layer and no large-field region of a component's history are the terms of ℓ₀, since the determining sets of ℓ and ℓ₀ coincide there and the expansions that produce the terms are local. The sources are CRE p.262 l.16–18, where E_k "is obtained by subtracting one-step vacuum energy expressions, generated in small field regions, from the initial constant E"; LFR II p.378 l.2, where E_k(Z) "denotes the sum of normalization terms and vacuum energy terms with localizations intersecting the large field domain Z"; and LFR II p.380 l.24–26, where the vacuum energy counterterms and normalisation constants obey the bounds of V(X^∼), with "the corresponding constant O(log g_j^{−2}) instead of O(1)" for the latter. C_λ absorbs the O(1)-layer enlargement of these localisations and their exponential tails. The renormalised components are removed from the sequence (LFR I (0.3)). So E^{ℓ₀} − E^ℓ is a sum over the histories. A normalisation constant is at most ½d(𝐠)log g_n^{−2} + |log σ₀| per integration variable ((1.70)), and the vacuum energies are at most E₂ per point (CRE p.264 l.14–15). Counting the constants of both terms, |E^{ℓ₀} − E^ℓ| ≤ Σ_{X⊂Z(ℓ)}Σ_{n≤k}C_λ(1 + log g_n^{−2})L^d|Z_n ∩ X|_n, with C_λ a constant of d, G, σ₀, and E₂. This is part of the history cost, paid in (c).

For ℓ₀ the same normalisation gives the identity ρ_{ℓ₀} = χ_k(T)exp(𝒜_{ℓ₀}), 𝒜_{ℓ₀} = −A(U_k(V))/g_a² + 𝐄_k(U_k(V)) + 𝐑_k(U_k(V)), since its 𝐓-operation is trivial. Here U_k(V) is the minimiser of VAR Theorem 1 on the whole torus; 𝐑_k carries the renormalised components of the history (LFR I (0.6)), and 𝐁_k = 0 without large-field regions (CRE (2.41)).

### 11(b) The Final-Scale Comparison

*Upper Bound.* The action is nonnegative, A(1/g², U) = Σ_p g^{−2}(x_p)η^{d−4}[1 − Re tr U(∂p)] ≥ 0 with the normalised trace (VAR (5); PBG p.392 l.2), so −A(1/g_k²(·), U_k) ≤ 0 everywhere. On the term's integration region, which the characteristic functions of (2.18) and of the large-field operations restrict (CRE p.256 l.27–29: V_{j−1} is regular on Γ_{j−1}), Theorem 2 gives |𝐄_k| ≤ E₁(1 − L^{−β})^{−1}Σ_n|Γ_n| ((2.45)), |𝐑_k| ≤ Σ_n|Γ_n| ((2.46), κ₀ ≥ 7), |𝐁_k| ≤ 2B_∂Σ_n|Γ_n| ((2.48), B_∂ the constant printed B₁ in (2.47)), |Γ_n| the number of points of the layer Γ_n of the term's determining set in the scale-n lattice T_1^{(n)} (p.263 l.21–23); this is (2.49) without its constants, which (a) treats. The exterior couplings of a kept component contribute ≤ C_∂#cubes_k(Z^∼) ≤ C_∂N_s⁴, C_∂ ≤ 1 for γ small (the continuum note's (7.3), from (1.68) and (1.99)). So, with C_ℰ := E₁(1 − L^{−β})^{−1} + 1 + 2B_∂, −A ≤ 0, and the bound of (a) on E^{ℓ₀} − E^ℓ, 𝒜_ℓ ≤ C_ℰΣ_{n≤k}|Γ_n(ℓ)|_n + C_∂N_s⁴ + |E^{ℓ₀} − E^ℓ| ≤ (C_ℰ + C_∂)N_s⁴ + Σ_{X⊂Z(ℓ)}P^c(X), P^c(X) := Σ_{n≤k}c_nL^d|Z_n ∩ X|_n, c_n := C_ℰ + C_λ(1 + log g_n^{−2}), since Σ_{n<k}|Γ_n ∩ X|_n ≤ Σ_{n≤k}L^d|Z_n ∩ X|_n by the inclusion below. This is a bound from above only, which is all that the upper bound uses. The last layer Γ_k lies in T_a, and every earlier layer Γ_n ⊂ Ω_{n+1}^c ⊂ Z_{n+1} lies inside a component of the stopping-scale region: the large-field regions grow and merge by (1.84) until renormalised, and a renormalised component is no longer in Z(ℓ), since the renormalised term is ρ(Z″, V) with that component removed (LFR I (0.3)); and |Γ_n ∩ X|_n ≤ L^d|Z_{n+1} ∩ X|_{n+1}. P^c(X) is the history cost of X, paid in (c).

*Lower Bound.* Let 𝔅 := {V : |V_b − 1| < r_𝔅 for every bond b of T_a}, r_𝔅 := g_a/(8B₃C_av), with C_av a Lipschitz bound on 𝔅 for the averaged extension M^·(Q_k^{s*}V) of (2.16). C_av = 1. The entries of Q^{s*}V are 1 on the bonds inside the blocks and V(c) on the bond b ∈ B(c) (CRE (1.3)). The averaging is that of CMP 98 (14)–(15), Ū_c = exp[iΣ_xL^{−d}(1/i)log U(Γ_{c,x})U(c)^{−1}]U(c). On Q^{s*}V every contour Γ_{c,x} carries U(Γ_{c,x}) = U(c), so the logarithms vanish and the average is exactly 1 or V(c), at every level. Hence the data plaquettes are 1 or plaquettes V(∂p) of the stopping-scale field, and |M^·(Q_k^{s*}V)(b) − 1| ≤ max_{b′}|V_{b′} − 1|. On 𝔅 the block data has plaquettes within ε₁ := g_a/(2B₃) of 1 (VAR (7)), ε₁ ≤ a₁ for g_a small, so VAR Theorem 1 applies to the local minimisers U_{k,□}(V) of (2.16) and to the global minimiser U_k(V): both lie in the space (8) with ε₀ = B₃ε₁ = g_a/2, i.e. |U(∂p) − 1| < (g_a/2)η² for every plaquette of T_η ((2) with j = k, Ω_k = T). Hence every test of (2.17) passes (g_a/2 < ε_k = g_ap₀(g_a), (2.4)), χ_k(T) = 1 on 𝔅; and 1 − Re tr U(∂p) = ½‖1 − U(∂p)‖²_{HS} ≤ ½|U(∂p) − 1|² gives A(U_k(V)) ≤ 6N_s⁴η^{−4}·½(g_a/2)²η⁴ = (3/4)g_a²N_s⁴, so −A(U_k(V))/g_a² ≥ −(3/4)N_s⁴ on 𝔅; and 𝐄_k + 𝐑_k ≥ −C_ℰN_s⁴ by (2.45) and (2.46), with Σ_n|Γ_n| = |Γ_k| = N_s⁴. The normalised Haar measure of the ball of radius r ≤ 1 about 1 in G is ≥ (c_Gr)^{dim G} (exponential coordinates), so Haar(𝔅) ≥ (c_Gr_𝔅)^{4N_s⁴dim G}. Together, in the normalisation of (a) and for g_a ≤ 1,

(11.1) Z_{ℓ₀} ≥ e^{−C_K}, Z_ℓ ≤ M_ℓ := e^{C_K}Π_{X⊂Z(ℓ)}[e·sup 𝐓′_k(X)1·exp(P^c(X))], C_K := C_fsN_s⁴(1 + log g_a^{−1}), C_fs := 1 + C_∂ + C_ℰ + 4 dim G·(1 + log₊(8B₃C_av/c_G)),

C_fs a constant of d, L, G, and the constants of CRE's Theorems 1–2, independent of K: the price of comparing whole integrals is exponential in the fixed volume N_s⁴ and in nothing else. M_ℓ is the term's majorant.

### 11(c) The Sum over Patterns

*Component Factors.* Let X be a component of Z(ℓ), Z′ the new large-field regions merged into it during its history (the Z_{j′}^{(i)} of (1.79), with birth scales j′ ≤ k), and Z″ the components of Z_n inside X at the scales n ≤ k. Its history volume is P(X) := Σ_{n≤k}L^d|Z_n ∩ X|_n, which counts at their own scales the layers Γ_{n−1} ⊂ Z_n and the large-field variables of the steps n − 1 → n. By (11.4) of (d), κ^tr_k(X) = κ̂_k(X) + G_k(X) + H_k(X), with κ^tr_k(X) the exact exponent of X's factor in (1.79), G_k(X) := ¼γ₀A₁²Σ_{Z′}p₀²(g_{j′})(d′_{j′}(Z′) + 1), H_k(X) := Σ_{n≤k}λ_nΣ_{Z″}M^dR_n^{d+1}d′_n(Z″), λ_n := 1 + 3·2^{d−1}L^dC_λ(1 + log g_n^{−2}), and κ̂_k(X) ≥ 0 satisfying (1.80) with O(1) + λ_n in place of O(1). The history volume and the history cost are bounded by H_k(X) and half of G_k(X). First, |Z_n ∩ X|_n = (MR_n)^dΣ_{Z″}#cubes_n(Z″), and #cubes_n(Z″) ≤ 3·2^{d−1}d′_n(Z″) when d′_n(Z″) ≠ 0 (LFR II p.384 l.31–32), so the points of Z″ cost (c_n + C_P)L^d|Z″|_n ≤ 3·2^{d−1}L^d(c_n + C_P)M^dR_n^dd′_n(Z″) ≤ λ_nM^dR_n^{d+1}d′_n(Z″) under the first condition of (H), since λ_nR_n ≥ R_n + 3·2^{d−1}L^dC_λ(1 + log g_n^{−2}). Second, a component with d′_n(Z″) = 0 has at most 2^d cubes with a common vertex: the linear size is the length of a shortest tree graph in continuous space meeting all the cubes (RG I p.257 l.14–16), and LFR II p.385 l.18 carries the corresponding 2^d. Such a component contains no S-image (S adds ten layers, p.384 l.7–8), so by (1.84) it consists of new large-field regions born at the scale n, each with its own gain in (1.79), used here once, at its birth. So

(11.2) P^c(X) + C_PP(X) ≤ H_k(X) + ½G_k(X) under (H): 3·2^{d−1}L^d(C_ℰ + C_P) ≤ R_n and 2^dL^dM^dR_n^d(c_n + C_P) ≤ ⅛γ₀A₁²p₀²(g_n) for every n ≤ k,

with C_P := max(2, 2C_M), C_M the count of §6.2, and the same at every scale j ≤ k for a component Z of Z_j, with P_j(Z), P^c_j(Z), H_j(Z), G_j(Z). Both thresholds are of Bałaban's kind (R_n ≥ (log g_n^{−2})^r and R_n < L(log g_n^{−2})^r by (2.5), p₀²(g_n) = A₀²(log g_n^{−2})^{2p₀} by (2.4) against R_n^d(1 + log g_n^{−2}), with 2p₀ > rd + 1). Hence, by (1.79) in the form of p.384 l.4–6 with κ^tr, e·sup 𝐓′_k(X)1·exp(P^c(X)) ≤ e·exp(−κ̂_k(X) − C_PP(X) − ½G_k(X) − 2p₀(g_{j(X)})), with p₀(g_{j(X)}) ≥ (1 + β₀)^{−1}p₀(g_a) ((2.7), j(X) ≤ k) and 2(1 + β₀)^{−1} ≥ 7/4 for β₀ ≤ 1/7 (the continuum note's (7.3), LFR II p.389 l.18). A surviving component, one failing (i) or (ii) at the stopping scale, has κ̂_k(X) ≥ c₀M^dR_{k+1}^{d+1}d′_{k+1}(S(X)) ≥ ĉ₀#cubes_k(X), the first term of (1.80) for κ̂ (the K of (1.80) is "the smallest positive integer" of p.384 l.15–17, so K ≥ 1 for these components, which fail (i) or (ii); the domain X of the definition (1.71), the component the R-operation renormalises, satisfies the assumption of (1.80) "with K=0" (p.387 l.30); this K is not the cutoff index), c₀ := O(1) + 1 ≤ O(1) + λ_{k+1}, ĉ₀ := c₀M^dR_{k+1}^{d+1}(3·2^{d−1})^{−1}(2L²)^{−d}, by the continuum note's (7.3) (d′_{k+1}(S(X)) ≥ (3·2^{d−1})^{−1}(2L²)^{−d}#cubes_k(X), from CRE (2.9) and p.384 l.7–8). Under (T2-i) ĉ₀ ≥ log C_d + 2, C_d the constant with at most C_d^n connected unions of n MR-cubes containing a given cube, its factor is ≤ exp(−(3/2)p₀(g_a) − (log C_d + 1)#cubes_k(X))·exp(−C_PP(X) − ½G_k(X)). A kept component, one satisfying (i) and (ii) ((1.89)), has #cubes_k(Y) ≤ 100^d and κ̂_k(Y) ≥ 0, and under (T2-ii) p₀(g_a)/4 ≥ (log C_d + 1)100^d + 1 its factor obeys the same bound. The spare factors exp(−C_PP(X)) and exp(−½G_k(X)) stay in the summands; §6 and §8.6 use the first.

*The Sum.* The components of Z(ℓ) are disjoint connected unions of MR_k-cubes of T_a, N_s⁴(MR_k)^{−4} cubes in all (N_s ≥ ℓ_C ≥ MR_k by the prescription of j(C), the continuum note l.9 with CRE (2.5), (2.9)), each component in one of the two classes, and the sum over the labels ℓ with a given region and class assignment (the sequences and the R-labels) is inside (1.79)'s first exponential, hence inside κ̂ (p.383 l.33–34; the continuum note's (7.2)). (1.79) bounds a supremum over the admissible sequences of a per-sequence expression with the entropy of the sequences inside its O(1); the history factor of a sequence and the weights below are inserted in that expression before the sum. So, by (11.1) and the component factors, for every choice of weights 1 ≤ ω(X) ≤ exp(C_PP(X) + ½G_k(X)),

(11.3) Σ_{ℓ≠ℓ₀}M_ℓ·Π_{X⊂Z(ℓ)}ω(X) ≤ e^{2C_K}Σ_{m≥1}(s^m/m!)·Z_{ℓ₀} = δ_LF·Z_{ℓ₀}, δ_LF := e^{2C_K}(e^s − 1), s := 2N_s⁴(MR_k)^{−4}e^{−(3/2)p₀(g_a)}Σ_{n≥1}C_d^ne^{−(log C_d + 1)n} ≤ 2N_s⁴(MR_k)^{−4}e^{−(3/2)p₀(g_a)},

and in particular, since Z_ℓ ≤ M_ℓ, Σ_{ℓ≠ℓ₀}Z_ℓ ≤ δ_LFZ_{ℓ₀}, so 1 − w_{ℓ₀} ≤ δ_LF and w_{ℓ₀} ≥ (1 + δ_LF)^{−1}. The same holds for either run with its own g_a, and δ_LF denotes the larger of the two values. It is bounded uniformly in K, since g_a lies in the K-independent window of the continuum note l.25 and N_s is fixed; this is what §6 and §7 use, and §8.6 uses (11.2) and (11.4).

*Smallness.* Under (T2-v) 8e^{2C_K}N_s⁴(MR_k)^{−4}e^{−(3/2)p₀(g_a)} ≤ 1, s ≤ ¼e^{−2C_K} ≤ 1 and δ_LF ≤ 2e^{2C_K}s ≤ ½. (T2-v) depends on the loop through N_s. The prescription of j(C) gives L^{j(C)} ≤ 2^{β₀}L^{q+4}M(log ḡ^{−2})^r for ḡ small (the continuum note l.9, with g_min = γ = ḡ under the pin), so N_s⁴ ≤ C_q(log ḡ^{−2})^{4r}, C_q := 2^{4β₀}(T_ph/u)⁴L^{4q+16}M⁴. With log g_a^{−1} ≤ log ḡ^{−1} + 1 and log g_a^{−2} ≥ log ḡ^{−2} − 2 log(1 + β₀) (CRE (2.6), the continuum note l.25), 2C_K ≤ 2C_fsC_q(log ḡ^{−2})^{4r}(2 + log ḡ^{−1}) against (3/2)p₀(g_a) of order (log ḡ^{−2})^{p₀}, so (T2-v) holds for ḡ ≤ ḡ₀(q, T_ph/u) when p₀ > 4r + 1. At a fixed ḡ it covers the loops with q ≤ Q(ḡ), Q(ḡ) = ((p₀ − 4r − 1)/4)log_L log ḡ^{−2} − O(1). The continuum note's third (C-LF) condition allows q up to order (log ḡ^{−2})^{p₀}/log L, so (T2-v) is the stronger restriction on the loop.

### 11(d) The Entropy Condition and Its Half-Rate Version

Bałaban's κ_j(Z) is defined by (1.83) and (1.85) from the factors of (1.79): each new large-field region Z′ = Z_{j′}^{(i)} brings the gain ½γ₀A₁²p₀²(g_{j′})(d′_{j′}(Z′) + 1) ((1.79)'s second line; (1.85) prints γ₀ where (1.79) has ½γ₀, and the gain available is (1.79)'s), each step n charges the cost O(1)M^dR_n^{d+1}d′_n(Z″) for every component Z″ of Z_n ((1.79)'s first exponential, which includes the sum over the admissible sequences, p.383 l.33–34, and the volume factors exp O(1)|Z_j ∩ Ω_j| of the fluctuation integrals, p.383 l.32–33), and the terms 2p₀ are rearranged with a nonnegative remainder (p.386 l.40–46). In one case Bałaban's definition is not the exponent of the factor: for Z = S(Z₀) with Z₀ satisfying (i), (ii) and a new large field in the preparatory operations, he puts κ_{j+1}(Z) := p₀(g_j) − O(1)M^dR_{j+1}^{d+1}d′_{j+1}(Z), discarding κ_j(Z₀) ≥ 0 (p.385 l.44–47, p.386 l.1–3). Let κ^tr_j(Z) be the exact exponent of Z's factor (p.384 l.4–6), given by the same recursion with κ_j(Z₀) kept in that case. (1.79) holds with κ^tr in place of κ, since κ^tr is the exact exponent of the product of (1.79)'s factors; κ^tr_j(Z) ≥ κ_j(Z) is what makes Bałaban's form of (1.79) true, with (1.85) read with (1.79)'s ½γ₀. The recursion for κ^tr is affine in the gain and the cost constants. Let κ̂_j(Z) be defined by the same recursion, κ̂_j(Z₀) kept in the preparatory case, with the gain ¼γ₀A₁²p₀²(g_{j′})(d′_{j′}(Z′) + 1) and the cost (O(1) + λ_n)M^dR_n^{d+1}d′_n(Z″), λ_n of (c). Then, for every j ≤ k and every component Z of Z_j,

(11.4) κ^tr_j(Z) = κ̂_j(Z) + G_j(Z) + H_j(Z), κ̂_j(Z) ≥ Σ_{n=j+1}^{j+K}(O(1) + λ_n)M^dR_n^{d+1}d′_n(S^{n−j}(Z)) ≥ 0, K as in (1.80),

with G_j, H_j as in (c) at the scale j, both summed over Z's whole history. The equality is the affine structure: the 2p₀ terms and the preparatory factors p₀(g_j) enter κ^tr and κ̂ alike and cancel in the difference. The inequality is Bałaban's proof of (1.80) (pp.384–387) read with the modified constants, by induction on j. In the preparatory case κ̂_{j+1}(Z) = κ̂_j(Z₀) + p₀(g_j) − (O(1) + λ_{j+1})M^dR_{j+1}^{d+1}d′_{j+1}(Z) ≥ p₀(g_j) − (O(1) + λ_{j+1})M^dR_{j+1}^{d+1}d′_{j+1}(Z), since κ̂_j(Z₀) ≥ 0 by the induction, and the right side is Bałaban's value with the cost raised by λ_{j+1}. The sizes of the gain and of the cost enter only through the condition at p.385 l.33–36 for a new region (there at j = 1; the same inequality at the scale of every new region, with ⅛γ₀ in place of ¼γ₀ and the cost (O(1) + λ_n)M^dR^{d+1} added to its right side), through the small-domain case p.385 l.44–47 and p.386 l.1–3 (p₀(g_j) against (O(1) + λ_{j+1})M^dR_{j+1}^{d+2} times a constant of d, over the R_{j+1} steps of K), and through the last inequality of (1.88) (2(1 + β₀)^{−1}p₀(g_{j+1}) against (O(1) + λ_{j+1})3(100M)^dR_{j+1}^{d+2}). The cost constant also enters (1.81), (1.83), (1.87), and the middle step of (1.88), on both sides alike, and the slack goes into the last inequality of (1.88). All three conditions hold for ḡ small with p₀ > r(d + 2) + 1, a form of Bałaban's "p₀ large" (p.385 l.37, p.387 l.28), the extra 1 for the logarithm in λ_n: p₀(g) = A₀(log g^{−2})^{p₀}, against λR^{d+2} ≤ C(log g^{−2})^{r(d+2)+1} (CRE (2.4)–(2.5)). (c) uses (11.4) at j = k: H_k(X) and half of G_k(X) pay the history volume (11.2), the stopping-scale reserve κ̂_k(X) ≥ c₀M^dR_{k+1}^{d+1}d′_{k+1}(S(X)) of a surviving component pays the shape entropy, and ½G_k(X) stays spare. §8.6 uses (11.4) and (11.2) at the scale j of a renormalisation.

α′) of §5) and r_{i,j} := α′_{i,j}/2 (the norm class 𝒫_j(X; r) and the class 𝒞_k of §5 and §8.2), with C₀ and C₁ at least λ_Q := 16B₃²C_{Q*} times Bałaban's lower bounds; C_{Q*} := 2(1 + (2d + 1)L^{d−1}) (the bound of the lifted current, §5 *Complex domain*);
